Implemented on branch `fix/destructive-after-freeze` (commits 6d49b97, 0375927, 79bda5e), not yet merged. — Rationale (recorded by the author, not stated by the user): The user approves the exact frozen text that will run, not a plan that can still change after the approval; a hash binding makes any later change visible. — Alternatives rejected by the author (not stated by the user): approval consumed at freeze (the A32 rule: the stop precedes the frozen plan, so the approved text and the executed text could differ); asking again on every refreeze (a fix-round reopen with the same hash would be asked for nothing).
- **A70. C-14: the three-reply limit stays (amended by A75: intent-grill's interview is exempt); every role asks all its questions in one NEEDS_INPUT round (user decision 2026-10-13; implementation stopped after two review rounds).** The architect asks its two questions in one round; the rule is enforced in the role sources and in `submit` (branch `fix/one-needs-input-round`, commit e440e44, NOT merged). Two blocking review findings stopped it. (a) `skills/intent-grill/SKILL.md` (team mode: one NEEDS_INPUT per round with exactly one question, fixtures/intent-grill/EXPECTED.md P1 and P9) contradicts the one-round rule, and the shared-rules bullet that all 18 skills carry says "never return NEEDS_INPUT again in the run": the skill needs either a carve-out for its interview rounds or a change of its protocol and fixture EXPECTED, which is the user's decision. (b) The per-role key rejects the first question of a later instance of the role (a second explorer, a redispatched architect); this is intentional, but reviewers want it stated. The user must decide: (1) exempt intent-grill's interview from the one-round rule (carve-out in the skill and in the shared bullet), or (2) batch intent-grill's questions and change fixture EXPECTED P1 and P9. — Rationale (recorded by the author, not stated by the user): a role that asks one question per round burns the three-reply limit on a plan that could be settled in one exchange. — Alternatives rejected by the author (not stated by the user): raising the limit (hides the cause); a per-run key instead of a per-role key (a redispatched role could never ask).
- **A71. C-3: `git stash` and `git checkout --` are forbidden in the project's working tree and free in proofline's evidence worktrees (user decision 2026-10-13, resolves the question of A66).** What the hook covers (A34, `adapters/claude/hooks/proofline-hook.sh`): while a run is active it denies `git stash|reset|clean|restore|switch|rebase|merge|cherry-pick|revert|am|apply|checkout --` for specialists only (a subagent or any non-lead role); it exempts every command that contains `git -C <path>`, `git worktree` or `--work-tree`, so the exemption is any worktree, not only proofline's evidence worktrees. The lead and sessions without an active run are not covered by the hook; the lead's compliance with C-3 is checked by the evaluator reading the transcript, not by the hook. EXPECTED C-3 (tests/e2e/expected/COMMON.md) is clarified: the two commands are not invoked in the project's working tree by any role, and are allowed in proofline's own evidence worktrees (D10). — Rationale (recorded by the author, not stated by the user): D10's hollow-test and mutation evidence needs to change files inside the worktree; the product tree stays clean. — Alternatives rejected by the author (not stated by the user): restricting C-3 literally to no invocation anywhere (would deny the evidence of D10); non-git edits in worktrees (clumsier, same effect).
- **A72. C-22 and S-6 are accepted as model behaviour (user decision 2026-10-13).** C-22 (a report claimed a stray temp file the hook had blocked) and S-6 (an EVIDENCE claim in a `path:line` form) stay as observed; no product rule is added. — Rationale (recorded by the author, not stated by the user): both are wording slips of the model; `verify-claims.mjs` accepts both forms by design and the hook already blocked the file. — Alternatives rejected by the author (not stated by the user): a new report rule for C-22 and a stricter evidence form for S-6 (more prose for the roles, no defect in the delivered work).
- **A73. Review follow-ups for A67 and A68 (info findings of the review agent; amends the record).** A68 supersedes the "reported once, a second stop is always accepted" of A44 and the "up to three times per reporting state" of A63 with: every final message of a reporting state is checked, at most three consecutive blocks; a message carrying the identifiers resets the counter; after three blocks the counter stays until a compliant message. Known limits of A67: the Turkish stems are matched case-sensitively on lower-cased text under the C locale, so capitalised `Ç`, `Ş`, `İ` and `YANLIŞ` can slip through; and they are matched as unanchored substrings, so `hata` matches "muhatap" and `çalışm` matches "çalışma". Both are recorded, not fixed. The header comment of the `stop)` branch in `adapters/claude/hooks/proofline-hook.sh` is stale after A68 (it still says "checked once per reporting state" and "a second stop is always accepted"); it is to be corrected in the next hook change. — Rationale (recorded by the author, not stated by the user): a false positive costs one debug-proof result; a false negative one defect word that the English words of the same check usually still catch. — Alternatives rejected by the author (not stated by the user): locale-aware lower-casing and word-boundary matching (not portable in the POSIX sh/awk the hook runs on).
- **A74. A defect task's completion report states the root cause in one sentence (S-11).** `COMPLETION_REPORT.md` of a defect task has a `## Root cause` section of exactly one sentence naming the function or symbol at fault and what it did wrong; the final message repeats it (in the user's language, symbol names untranslated) with the regression test, the gates, and that commit and push await approval. `publish-completion-report` requires the section when `reports/NNN-debug.md` exists (the debug-proof result is the run's proof that the task is a defect, A35), so no second defect-word scan is added. Tested by `scenario_report_root_cause` in `lifecycle-test.sh`. — A Sonnet lead omitted the root cause because `REPORT.md` had no rule for it (STANDARD.tr S-11). — Limits: only the heading's presence is machine-checked, not the one-sentence form or the symbol name; the Stop hook does not check the sentence in the final message (it checks identifiers only, A44/A68); the report sentence is English (SKILL.md rule 11) while the final message uses the user's language. — `skills/proofline-lead/references/REPORT.md`, `skills/proofline-lead/SKILL.md`, both COMPLETION_REPORT templates, `required_report_headings`; the expected e2e files are unchanged.
- **A76. Pull-request CI finishes in minutes, not half an hour; no test is removed.** (1) `tests/selftest.sh` splits every suite that lists its scenarios (`run_sc NAME` lines; the enforcement suite's `scenarios=` line) into one process per scenario (`LC_EXACT` in `test-lib.sh`), runs the units on all cores through `xargs -P`, keeps one log per unit, fails when any unit fails or when a scenario unit ran no case, and prints the slowest units; `SELFTEST_SHARD=I/N` runs one slice. (2) CI is split into parallel jobs: `static` (shellcheck, the three sync checks, generator tests, skill tests), `fixtures`, and `selftest` in four shards; the `checks` job always reports, a skipped job counts as passed. The proofline-lead fixture builds its five states in parallel. (3) Pull requests run Linux only; macOS runs the same jobs on pushes to `main`, the nightly schedule (03:17 UTC) and manual runs. Pushes to other branches no longer run CI a second time next to the pull-request run, and a new push cancels the superseded pull-request run (every other event has its own concurrency group, so queued `main` runs are not displaced). (4) A pull request that changes only plain documentation (`tests/ci/classify.sh`: root README, CHANGELOG, CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY, LICENSE, and `.md`, `.png` and `.svg` files under `docs/` except `docs/reference/`) skips generator tests, skill tests, fixtures and self-tests; shellcheck and the sync checks still run. Markdown under `skills/`, `roles/`, `control-plane/` and `adapters/`, every script and every `.github` change count as code; an empty or unknown change list counts as code. (5) `new_repo` copies a fixture built once per process. — Before: the self-tests ran in batches of four with a wait for the slowest of each batch, one job per OS, one step after another: 17 m 54 s of self-tests and 21 m total on Linux, 23 m 36 s on macOS, 24 to 32 m per run (59 m once), two runs per pushed commit. Measured locally with the machine shared with other agents: lifecycle-test 755 s, gates-test 472 s, cli-test 298 s, fixtures 160 s (145 s of them proofline-lead), enforcement 123 s; 1769 s of suites run sequentially. — After: the longest scenario unit and `cli-test` bound the self-test jobs; the work is spread over 4 shards x 4 cores. The measured local result is in the pull request; the expected PR wall time is the longest job (`cli-test` or the fixtures job), a few minutes. — Alternatives rejected: shortening waits behind a test-mode variable (the suites hold no real waits: the lock and dispatch timeouts are already set by `PROOFLINE_LOCK_TIMEOUT` and `PROOFLINE_DISPATCH_MIN_TIMEOUT` in the tests; three `sleep 1` remain for timestamp ordering); `paths-ignore` on the workflow (a skipped workflow leaves a required check pending, and it cannot keep markdown under `skills/` as code); moving the fixtures or `cli-test` to main and nightly only (nothing exceeds the budget once parallel, and a PR must keep its coverage). — Limits: `cli-test` is one linear script and stays one unit; a pull request that touches only docs can no longer break `fixtures` or the self-tests unnoticed, which is why `docs/reference/` and every other file type under `docs/` count as code; branch pushes without a pull request run no CI; shards balance by unit count, not by duration; no test needs a browser, so none was moved to the nightly run. — `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `tests/selftest.sh`, `tests/ci/classify.sh`, `tests/ci/classify-test.sh`, `control-plane/agents/scripts/test-lib.sh`, `tests/enforcement/enforcement-test.sh`, `fixtures/proofline-lead/setup.sh`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`.
- **A77. A `fix_scope` entry is `path`, `path:line` or `path:symbol` and scopes to its path part.** A fix round was refused when the gate or amendment named `public/app.js:setTagFilter`: the fix-bound check compared the whole entry to the changed path `public/app.js`, so the run stalled at IMPLEMENTING and never reached CODE_DONE (independent e2e evaluation, COMPLEX run). Rule: everything from the first `:` is dropped from each entry, once, in `scope_entry_paths` (`agent.sh`); `verify_fix_bound`, the `amend` frozen-scope authorization, and the stored amendment `fix_scope` use it, and gate records and `check-result.mjs` now strip a symbol the same way they stripped a line number. The symbol stays in the finding text and `fix_instruction`; scope is enforced per path, never per symbol. Tested by `scenario_fix_scope_symbol` and the `path:symbol` amend cases in `lifecycle-test.sh` (a symbol entry passes; a changed path outside the scope is still refused). — Alternatives rejected: keeping literal comparison and requiring bare paths (the role-facing format already allows `path:line`, and evaluators write symbols naturally); matching by prefix (`public/app.js` would also scope `public/app.jsx`); enforcing the symbol by diff inspection (language-specific, not deterministic). — Limits: a path containing `:` cannot be scoped; the fix may touch any part of an in-scope file.
- **A78. Risk-adaptive pipeline: deterministic risk signals select focuses inside the existing gates.** What: besides class and tier, config `risk:` (`.agents/config.yaml`, language-independent defaults) maps signals to one focus each. Signals come from changed or PLAN.md-scope paths (`contract_file`, `migration`, `ui_path`, `dependency_manifest`, `config_env`, globs as in `risky_paths`), from text the diff adds (`concurrency_hint`, `performance_hint`, extended regular expressions, empty by default), and from PLAN.md flags (`## Risk`: `contract_change`, `concurrency`, `performance_critical`; `## Data` = `data_change`, its `destructive: yes`; `## UI` = `ui`). Focuses: REVIEW_RULES (REVIEW_SMALL on tier small) `contract`, `concurrency`, `performance`, `config`; REVIEW_DATA `migration` (REVIEW_DATA becomes required on tier full by a migration signal); QA `visual`; SECURITY `dependencies`. The focus set and its gate are fixed in `agent.sh`; config only chooses which focus a signal triggers, so no gate type can be invented (`config-check` refuses any other value). A check runs only when its gate is required (TRIVIAL: no `visual`). `agent.sh pipeline` prints each signal with its source, the glob or flag that matched, its check, its flag state and its design role, plus `missing_flag.`, `focus.<GATE>.<focus>=<status>`, `design_role.` and `unsupported.` lines; before FREEZE the scope paths give the signals, and `next` dispatches the design role that must declare a missing flag. A gate's status is the worst over its own focus and every risk focus it carries, so `handoff CODE_DONE` needs each focus result. `record` adds a blocking finding to a risk focus's result for every diff signal whose flag is not `yes` (a contract file changed without `contract_change: yes`): the focus still runs and reports, and the result is FAIL; it is cleared by an amendment and refreeze or a user waiver. A focus whose skill or tool is missing (every focus skill now exists: contract-proof A83, concurrency-proof A86, performance-proof A87, visual-proof A82; each is UNSUPPORTED only when the tool or capability it needs is missing, for contract-proof a changed contract file it cannot parse that no compatibility tool already present covers, or no node) is submitted as `--result UNSUPPORTED --reason`: a blocking `UNSUPPORTED` finding, never a PASS, waivable only by the user, required in the completion report under `## Unsupported Checks`; the `dependencies` focus has a tool-free check (the manifest diff against `## Dependencies`) and refuses UNSUPPORTED. Consequence: a run whose focus cannot run (a missing browser, benchmark, test database or an unparseable contract) cannot reach CODE_DONE without that UNSUPPORTED result and the user's waiver. A path signal checked by the `migration` focus is a risky path for the tier, so a one-file migration is tier full and REVIEW_DATA carries the check (the tier still never decreases). `contract_file` also has language-independent content defaults (`^(openapi|asyncapi|swagger):`, `"$schema"`, `^syntax = "proto`) on added lines of files its globs do not match. A flag set to `yes` requires its check whether or not the diff carries the signal (the plan says the check is needed). Plain documentation and `.agents/` never carry a signal (a guide named like a migration, a README in `migrations/`). Freeze binds the flags (PLAN.md hash) and the `risk:` block (`freeze.risk_sha256`); the runtime hook already denies the lead any write to `.agents/config.yaml` during a run. Why: class and tier say how much review a change gets, not which specialist check it needs; without a signal a contract or migration change gets only the generic rules review, and the design role that should declare it is not called. Alternatives rejected: new gate types per check (more gates, more records; the gate set is fixed by D14); signals the lead or a model chooses (not deterministic; the lead must not select its own checks); the missing flag as a freeze refusal only (the diff can add a contract change after FREEZE); content heuristics on by default (language-specific noise); UNSUPPORTED as a non-blocking note (it would read as a pass); a separate waiver kind for UNSUPPORTED (the existing user-approved waiver already binds the finding). — Limits: path signals are glob matches, so a contract kept in an unusual file name is missed unless the project adds its glob; content hints see only added lines of text files; a scope path that is a directory matches only globs that cover directories. — `control-plane/agents/scripts/agent.sh` (`risk_*`, `gate_status`, `record_validate`, `submit_result`, `next_step`, `freeze`), `control-plane/agents/config.yaml`, `templates/RUN.yaml`, `GATES.md`, roles reviewer/qa/security_reviewer/architect, `risk-test.sh`, `fixtures/risk-signals/`, `tests/enforcement/enforcement-test.sh`. *Amended by A98:* UNSUPPORTED is no longer blocking by default: it is recorded `unsupported` (unproven, reported, never a PASS), does not hold its gate open, and must be listed with `not proven: <what>`; `risk.unsupported: block` restores the rule above, and an INCONCLUSIVE reason blocks in both modes.
- **A88. Security extension: a dependency audit inside the SECURITY gate and a config consistency check inside the REVIEW focus `config`, both deterministic, both inside existing skills.** What: (1) the `dependencies` focus of A78 gets its method in exploit-proof. `skills/exploit-proof/scripts/diff-manifests.mjs` (dependency-free Node, reads git objects and the working tree, no network, installs nothing) diffs every changed manifest or lockfile it recognises by file name (npm: package.json, package-lock, npm-shrinkwrap, yarn.lock, pnpm-lock; python: requirements*.txt, pyproject.toml, Pipfile, Pipfile.lock, poetry.lock, uv.lock; go.mod, go.sum; Cargo.toml, Cargo.lock; pom.xml, build.gradle(.kts); Gemfile, Gemfile.lock; composer.json, composer.lock; csproj, Directory.Packages.props, packages.lock.json; other known manifests are listed unparsed) into added, removed and changed entries, marks lockfile entries direct or transitive (a lock entry is direct when a manifest of its directory declares the name), and flags hazards from the declaration itself (a rename, `"al": "npm:real@1"` or Cargo `package = "real"`, is recorded under the real name with flag `alias`; requirements includes are followed whatever the file is called or listed unparsed; a non-default pip index becomes an entry `index:<host>`): `git_url`, `local_path`, `unpinned` (`*`, `latest`, open `>=`, no version), `install_script` (the project's own `preinstall/install/postinstall/prepare` hooks as `scripts.<hook>`, composer install hooks, a lockfile entry that records an install script) and `replaced`. It checks each direct manifest change against the plan's `## Dependencies` (whole word, case-insensitive; a hook is named by its hook name; `none` declares nothing): an undeclared added or changed direct dependency or install hook is a blocking change, a removed dependency, a version bump of a declared dependency, a hazard flag on a declared dependency and every lockfile-only or transitive change are notes (a lockfile line of a direct dependency repeats its manifest entry and is never a second plan finding). The license policy is `security.licenses.allow` / `security.licenses.deny` (SPDX ids; `OR` any alternative, `AND` all terms) applied to the license a lockfile records; both empty is reported as not configured, an entry whose license no file records is counted as unknown, never as compliant. The script detects, and never runs or installs, the project's own audit tool: `security.audit_command`, `scripts.audit` in package.json and the ecosystem tool on PATH (`npm audit`, `pip-audit`, `cargo audit`, `govulncheck`, `bundle-audit`, `composer audit`, `dotnet list package --vulnerable`). The reviewer runs a detected tool once and records `audit` as `measured` (command, exit code, output excerpt) or `tool_unavailable` (command, reason: for example it needs the network, which we do not use); a detected tool with no recorded result is rejected by the validator ("silence is not a result"); a vulnerability is accepted only as `{id, package, severity, source: audit_tool_output}` with its id present in the recorded output as a whole token (advisory ids of CVE, GHSA, RUSTSEC, PYSEC, GO, OSV and SNYK count as mentions), so nothing comes from model memory; a `measured` record must agree with itself (a non-zero exit code, or an output that mentions vulnerabilities or CVE/GHSA ids, with none listed is rejected unless `no_findings_reason` explains it, and a non-zero exit never gives confidence `high`); with `--repo`/`--base` the validator re-runs the diff and rejects a document that differs from it. `validate-dependencies.mjs` recomputes `class` and `reasons` of every change from the rule (a hand-edited class is rejected) and prints RESULT and CONFIDENCE: FAIL when a blocking change or a vulnerability exists; `high` only when the tool measured and the plan was checked, `medium` without a tool, with an unavailable tool or without a plan (an `info` row says "known vulnerabilities not checked" / "manifest diff and plan check only" / "license policy not configured"), `low` when a changed manifest could not be parsed (`partial: true` files, where a Maven property, Gradle variable or catalog reference, or MSBuild property could not be resolved, cap it at `medium`; "N new or changed dependencies have no recorded license: policy not applied" is an `info` row). The focus never reports UNSUPPORTED (the manifest diff always exists, as A78 fixed). Findings belong to the SECURITY gate: never waivable (D16, enforced by the gate evaluation). (2) The `config` focus of A78 gets `skills/proof-review/scripts/check-config.mjs` (dependency-free, deterministic, regex based) and `validate-config.mjs`, documented in `skills/proof-review/references/CONFIG.md`. Only lines the change adds (flags: also removed lines) are judged and a line the base file already had is not new. Blocking: an environment variable read by added code (`process.env`, `import.meta.env`, `os.environ`, `os.getenv`, `os.Getenv`, `env::var`, `ENV[]`, `getenv`, `System.getenv`, `Environment.GetEnvironmentVariable`) or `${VAR}` in an added config line that no example/sample/template file and no Markdown, reStructuredText or AsciiDoc file mentions (ambient variables such as PATH, HOME, NODE_ENV are never reported); a plain-text secret on an added line of a config (including `.tf`, `.tfvars`, `.hcl`), env (`.env`, `.env.*`, `*.env`, `config.env.*`) or example file (the SECRETS gate's rule names; the value never enters the document, only rule, key and length; the validator rejects a document in which any rule, generic included, matches the subject or detail; a line with `proofline:allow-secret`, the convention the SECRETS gate already honors, is a note); a feature flag added with default on that the plan does not name (a note when no plan is available); a flag removed from a config file while the repository still references it. Notes: a variable added to an example file that nothing reads, a flag defaulting to off, a planned flag, an allow-listed secret. A flag is a boolean key whose name contains feature, flag, toggle, experiment or rollout or that sits under a features/flags/toggles block. The gate is FAIL when a blocking item exists; confidence `medium` when files were skipped or no plan was available. A plain-text secret is also a SECRETS gate finding (never waivable); this focus adds where it is. (3) Wiring: the reviewer and security_reviewer role sources point to both skills; `config` joins `FOCUSES`, `FORBIDDEN_BY_FOCUS` (it reports its own items only, a `findings.json` for it carries suspicions only) and `RUN_DIR` in proof-review's `gate.mjs`; `security` is an accepted top-level config key (`config-check`), the shipped config documents the empty defaults, and every non-empty `security.*` value is bound by the freeze together with the `risk:` block (`freeze.risk_sha256`; empty keys are left out so a config that sets none hashes as before), so the lead or an agent cannot loosen the license policy or swap the audit command mid-run; adapters regenerated. Evidence class: every item is `measured` (a reproducible scan), nothing in either focus is judged. Tested: `fixtures/dependency-audit` (undeclared new dependency, a declared one, an undeclared postinstall hook, a git URL dependency and an unpinned range that are declared, a version bump of a declared dependency, a lockfile-only transitive change and a transitive install-script entry as decoys, a denied license, with and without the policy, a silent audit, a clean branch) and `fixtures/config-consistency` (undocumented variables in two languages, a plain-text secret, an unplanned default-on flag, a removed flag still referenced, a documented-but-unused variable, an allow-listed fake secret, a planned flag, and traps: a pre-existing undocumented variable, an ambient one, a documented one, a README-documented one, a `${VAR}` reference), each with `EXPECTED.md` written from the planted facts and `selfcheck.sh`; `tests/skills/exploit-proof-dependencies.test.mjs`, `tests/skills/proof-review-config.test.mjs`. — Alternatives rejected: **known-vulnerability lookup from model memory** (not reproducible, wrong or stale for any given version, and a confident invented advisory is worse than "not checked"; vulnerabilities come only from the project's own tool output, and without one the report says so); **installing an audit tool** (the shared rule forbids installs in the user's project and machine, and a tool we pick imposes an ecosystem; a project that wants vulnerability checks has or adds its own tool, and `security.audit_command` names it); our own network call to an advisory database (no network use by us, and it would send the dependency list off the machine); making the dependencies focus UNSUPPORTED without a tool (A78 fixed that the manifest diff always exists; that check is the floor, the tool only adds); a new skill, role or gate for either check (no new roles or gate types: both are focuses of existing skills); judging config consistency by model reading (non-deterministic and not reproducible; a regex scan with stated limits is); treating a flag's default as blocking without a plan (nothing written to violate: it stays a note, and with a plan the omission is the violated criterion); a numeric risk score per dependency (a guess, A91). — Limits: the manifest parsers are structural line/regex readers (TOML arrays and tables are read quote and bracket aware; pom and csproj properties and Gradle variables are resolved within the file), so a version from a parent, a catalog or a plugin, a workspace protocol or a hand-formatted file can be read as its raw spec or missed, a file with an unresolvable construct is `partial`, and unknown formats (`setup.py`, `setup.cfg`, `mix.exs`, ...) are listed unparsed with confidence `low`; npm overrides, resolutions and bundled dependencies are read as entries, requirement continuation lines and hash options are ignored; licenses exist only where a lockfile records them; transitive hazards are seen only through lockfiles that record them; `direct` for lockfile entries is name based; an unavailable audit tool leaves vulnerabilities unchecked (stated, confidence `medium`, not a PASS claim of safety); environment reads are matched by literal name in the listed idioms and JavaScript destructuring of the environment (not computed names, aliases of the environment object, wrappers, framework bindings or shell scripts) and "documented" is name presence in an example or documentation file; the secret scan is regex and entropy based and does not run the project's own scanner; the flag convention is a naming heuristic. — `skills/exploit-proof/scripts/diff-manifests.mjs`, `skills/exploit-proof/scripts/validate-dependencies.mjs`, `skills/exploit-proof/references/DEPENDENCIES.md`, `skills/exploit-proof/schema/dependencies.schema.json`, `skills/proof-review/scripts/check-config.mjs`, `skills/proof-review/scripts/validate-config.mjs`, `skills/proof-review/references/CONFIG.md`, `roles/reviewer.md`, `roles/security_reviewer.md`, `control-plane/agents/config.yaml`, `control-plane/agents/scripts/agent.sh` (`config_check`), `fixtures/dependency-audit/`, `fixtures/config-consistency/`.
- **A90. A benchmark report scores the existing fixtures and e2e runs; it invents no scenario and makes no model call.** `tests/benchmark/benchmark.mjs` (dependency-free Node) reads the known answers every `fixtures/*/EXPECTED.md` already holds: items under a MUST / MUST find / Process expectations heading are expected findings, items under MUST NOT / decoys are traps; an item is a numbered line, a table row keyed by an id (`E1`, `X1`) or a bullet that starts with a bold id (`**P1**`). A fixture with no such items (prose or sub-fixture sections only) is listed as `not measurable`, never guessed. Recorded outputs come in as a verdict file (`found` ids, `tripped` trap ids, `extra` findings that match nothing, planned scope and changed paths, duration, tokens) written by the evaluator who already compares a run with EXPECTED; an id that is not in EXPECTED is an error. Per skill the report gives correct findings, missed, false positives (trap tripped + extra), scope adherence (changed paths outside the planned scope), duration and tokens (`n/a` when not recorded, never estimated), plus, with `--run-validators`, each fixture's `selfcheck.sh` and skill test result. `--e2e-dir` takes tests/e2e run directories: the evaluator's table (`evaluation.md`: met/unmet/not-applicable, `E2E:` verdict), scope from `out/runs/*/PLAN.md` against the collected diffs, duration and tokens from `out/summary.txt` and `out/metrics/records.jsonl`. Output is Markdown plus JSON (`proofline.benchmark/1`). The PR job runs only the benchmark's own known-answer test (planted miss, planted false positive, exact counts, seconds); the full report with validators runs on main and nightly. — Alternatives rejected: new synthetic scenarios (they would be written to the benchmark's measure, a second source of truth next to the fixtures, and a number on a fixture no skill was developed against says nothing); LLM-judged scoring inside the benchmark (a non-deterministic, costly judge whose own error rate would hide the skill's; the existing evaluator sessions stay outside and their recorded verdicts are the input); guessing items from prose EXPECTED sections (an invented denominator); an aggregate quality score (the counts and the misses are the information, a single number invites tuning to it, same reason as the rejected numeric confidence score, A91). — Limits: a fixture's score is only as good as the verdict file (it must name an `evaluator` or give `evidence` per found id, but is otherwise trusted; the benchmark cannot verify it against the run); `--run-validators` failures only warn and count in `totals.validators_failed`, they never change the exit status; e2e items are requirements, so a tripped trap shows as an unmet item and false positives are `n/a` there; four fixtures (proof-review, proofline-lead, task-author, test-trust) are `not measurable` today because their EXPECTED files use sub-fixture or per-run sections; the e2e and metrics numbers come from few, expensive runs and are not statistically meaningful. — `tests/benchmark/`, `tests/benchmark/README.md`.
- **A80. Metrics record cost and effort per run, role and dispatch; routing does not read them yet.** `.agents/local/metrics/` gains `records.jsonl` next to `history.tsv`: one JSON object per line, schema `proofline.metrics/1` (every key always present, `null` when not observed; full table in `docs/concepts/metrics.md`). Three kinds, written by the control plane with no model-written text copied in (`dispatch` records come from the lead's `dispatch.sh` call by convention: `metrics-dispatch` is lead-only, but `AGENT_ROLE` is a declared claim, the same trust as `ledger-note`): `dispatch` (by `dispatch.sh` through `agent.sh metrics-dispatch`, once the first attempt has started, so on every exit after that; a usage error before it writes none: wall-clock duration of the whole call, `input_tokens`/`output_tokens`/`num_turns` from the runtime's own JSON, Codex's `tokens used` trailer (trusted only in the last non-empty lines of the log), retries = extra attempts from resubmission or failover, result), `step` (per recorded specialist result: duration from decision to record, gate and result), `run` (at DONE or terminate: duration, delegation count = specialist decisions in the run, fix rounds used, result). Token data is never estimated: a runtime that reports none (command-template runtimes, crashes, older versions) leaves `null`, and `null` is not zero. No finding, prompt or path is ever written, and identifier strings are reduced to `[A-Za-z0-9._+:-]`, so a record holds short identifiers and numbers only (this limits what can leak; it is not a secret scanner); the directory is git-ignored. `metrics: off` writes neither file (self-tested for `agent.sh` and the dispatch call). Tested: record shape, null tokens, retry counting, claude/Codex usage parsing, injected "token" text in a model message ignored, non-lead refused (`gates-test.sh` `sc_decide`, `dispatch-test.sh`). — **Routing hints from history: deferred, not implemented.** `exec-policy.sh` is a pure function of its inputs (no clock, no run state), which is what makes a decision reproducible, explainable and testable, and D5 puts quality and security above cost. A duration or outcome history would make the same run resolve differently on different machines and over time, and short durations correlate with easy tasks, not with a model being sufficient: routing on them would quietly lower review strength. A safe form needs a stated rule (for example: history may only break ties between two models that both meet the role floor, never lower a model for a gate or the security reviewer, and is recorded as a signal in the decision) and enough data from real runs to justify it; neither exists yet. Revisit after `proofline insights` (A89) shows real distributions. — Alternatives rejected: parsing token counts from model-written reports (spoofable, wrong by construction); estimating tokens from prompt size (an invented number reported as a measurement); a single wider `history.tsv` (a breaking column change for existing readers, and no room for nulls); a SQLite or other database file (a new dependency, and agents must not touch databases, A50/A52); per-token cost in currency (prices change and differ per plan, the insights report can multiply by a user-supplied table). — Limits: tokens are what the runtime reports at the end of a call (cache reads are not in `tokens_in`); a dispatch killed by timeout reports none; `step` duration includes queue time between decision and record; the `run` record carries no tokens, the report sums `dispatch` records; metrics are per worktree, not aggregated across clones; the consumer is `proofline insights` (A89); `AGENT_ROLE` is a declared claim, so a record is not tamper-proof. — `control-plane/agents/scripts/agent.sh`, `dispatch.sh`, `gates-test.sh`, `dispatch-test.sh`, `docs/concepts/metrics.md`.
- **A89. `proofline insights` reports cost and recurring failures; it only prints, it never changes anything.** `proofline insights [--run <ID>] [--json]` (POSIX sh + awk, `bin/lib/insights.sh`) reads `.agents/local/metrics/records.jsonl` (A80) and, per run, `LEDGER.log`, `gates/*.yaml` and `results/*.result.md`, and prints (1) cost per run, per role and model, per model and in total: duration, runtime-reported tokens (in, out and total as three columns, since a Codex-style record carries only `tokens_total`), retries, and from the `run` record delegations and fix rounds; (2) recurring failures with counts, run ids and a plain-language suggestion. It opens no file for writing: no rule, skill, config, ledger or metrics file is touched, no suggestion is applied (tested: every file hash identical before and after, and a run on a read-only checkout). Tokens are summed over recorded values only; `n/a` when none was recorded, `k (n/m)` when only n of m dispatches reported; null is never zero and nothing is estimated (A80). A record whose `schema` is not `proofline.metrics/1` is skipped and counted, a non-JSON line is counted separately and a blank line ignored; a second `run` record for the same task is ignored (the first counts) and counted. Dispatch durations (a call, retries included) and run durations (the whole run) are different measures, reported separately and never added. Patterns need two or more occurrences: `repeated-finding` (same gate, same file, identical text after whitespace is collapsed; line numbers ignored; two findings with similar but different text are two findings; `info` is not counted), `gate-fail` (ledger `gate:<GATE>:fail`), `scope` (blocking findings whose text names an out-of-scope or unmapped path: a text match, so judged, not measured), `unsupported` (the same line naming `UNSUPPORTED` in two or more recorded results), `approval` (per run and kind, requests that exceeded grants, when asked at least twice). A gate record or result counts only when a ledger row binds it, as the control plane itself does; a hand-written file is ignored. `metrics: off` reports "no metrics recorded"; no runs reports "no runs"; both exit 0. Tested: `fixtures/insights` (planted records with null tokens, an unknown schema, shifted-line repeats, a similar-text decoy, an unbound gate record, an `info` repeat; `EXPECTED.md`, `selfcheck.sh`) and `tests/cli/cli-test.sh`. — Alternatives rejected: **auto-applying suggestions** (editing rules, skills or config from a report would let a statistic change enforcement without a human decision, and a pattern in past runs is not proof that a rule is wrong; the report names the lever and the user pulls it); **sending data off the machine** (a hosted analysis or telemetry upload would move findings, paths and prompts out of the repository; the report is computed locally from local files and prints to the terminal); estimating tokens or cost in currency (an invented number, A80); a database or index of runs (a new dependency, and agents must not touch databases, A50/A52); a new gate type or role for retrospectives (no new roles, no new gate types). — Limits: scope and approval refusals that the control plane rejects leave no ledger entry, so only what findings and request/grant rows state is counted; metrics are per worktree; `UNSUPPORTED` is read from the recorded result text, so a result that quotes the word in prose counts; the `scope` match is a keyword heuristic. — `bin/lib/insights.sh`, `bin/proofline`, `fixtures/insights/`, `tests/cli/cli-test.sh`, `docs/concepts/metrics.md`.
- **A81. ui-design gains a grounded creative direction, a UX pattern comparison, a spec self-critique and per-criterion visual-proof marking; the designer never approves its own design.** UI specs covered states and accessibility but not why the screen looks and is organised the way it is, so a generated design drifted to generic defaults and nothing marked which criteria need visual evidence. Rule: three references read on demand (`skills/ui-design/references/CREATIVE-DIRECTION.md`, `UX-PATTERNS.md`, `SELF-CRITIQUE.md`; SKILL.md only points to them) and four optional `ui.json` keys, validated whenever present: `creative_direction` (basis `conform|redesign|new`, visual character, typography, layout, content hierarchy, rationale grounded in the users and the existing system; `new` is rejected when `DESIGN.md` exists and needs `avoided`, the generic defaults rejected; `redesign` needs the task's own words (`task_quote`, found in TASK.md with `--task` and containing a redesign-intent word such as redesign, restyle, re-theme, new look, visual overhaul) and the user's approval (`user_approval`, a reference to a recorded answer: `questions/<NNN>.answer.md`, `Q<n>` of the NEEDS_INPUT round or `INTENT.md#<decision>`; it may not name the designer, a first-person approval, an assumption or an implication)), `ux_patterns` (at least two alternatives, the chosen one among them, density, flow, rationale), `self_critique` (hierarchy, density, consistency, legibility, an anti-pattern checklist with `clear|fixed|accepted`), `criteria` (per UI-facing `AC-n`: screens, `visual_proof: required|not_required`, evidence target). `validate-ui.mjs` gains `--task` and `--class`: COMPLEX/CRITICAL in mode task make all four mandatory; without a class they stay optional, so every spec written before A81 still validates (fixture `legacy.json`). `--class` without `--task` is a usage error (a class implies a task); in a run the designer validates with both before the plan is frozen (designer role, LIFECYCLE, SECTIONS.md). With `--root`, `design_system.exists: false` while the file exists is rejected; mode `reverse` rejects the four keys and non-empty `screens`. The designer never gives final quality approval of its own design (SKILL.md rule 10, the designer role's Must-not column): verdict or first-person approval phrasing ("this design is approved", "signed off", "LGTM", "ready to ship", "production ready", "no issues remain", "meets all criteria", "I approve") in any free-text field of the four keys, or a top-level key naming an approval, sign-off or verdict, fails validation; domain words ("approval button", "not approved items") and a user's approval named in an `accepted` note do not (tested both ways). Visual-proof interface for A82 and verify-proof: the frozen PLAN `## UI` carries one line `visual_proof: <AC ids|none>`, validated by `validate-plan.mjs` (ids must be TASK criteria, no duplicates) and required for COMPLEX/CRITICAL plans with a `## UI` section; `validate-ui.mjs --list-visual-proof` prints `{"schema":"ui-design.visual-proof/1","required":[...]}` from `design/ui.json`; a marked criterion without visual evidence is unproven (consumed by A82; verify-proof is unchanged here). Known-answer cases in `fixtures/ui-design/cases/` (good, legacy, three traps: self-approval with a user-approval decoy, `visual_proof` on a criterion the task does not have, a redesign without approval while `DESIGN.md` exists), checked by `selfcheck.sh`. — Rationale: the direction and pattern choices are where a design is either grounded or generic, and recording them makes a reviewer able to disagree with a reason; the self-critique catches the common spec faults before anyone builds them; the designer judging its own work is the same conflict the gates exist to avoid. — Alternatives rejected: a ready-made style catalogue (produces the same generic looks the direction must avoid); making the new keys mandatory for every class (breaks every existing spec and costs a STANDARD change a design essay); a design-approval result field the designer may set (the approval belongs to QA's visual focus and the reviewers); keeping the visual-proof list only in `ui.json` (not part of the frozen plan, so the proof skills could not rely on it); checking the user's approval against the run's answer files inside the skill validator (a standalone skill cannot read the control plane; the shape check plus the role rule is the boundary, the residual is a designer that writes a false approval reference). — Limits: `--task` and `--class` are passed by the designer (the role method says to); a validator run without them checks only shape; the redesign-intent and self-approval checks are word lists, not an understanding of the text; `user_approval` is checked for the shape of a reference, not against the run's answer files (a standalone skill cannot read the control plane), so a designer that writes a false reference is a residual; `basis: conform` is a claim the validator cannot verify (whether the screens really follow `DESIGN.md` is judged by the reviewers and measured in part by QA's visual focus).
- **A79. Assumption register: a mandatory `## Assumptions` section in PLAN.md and EVIDENCE.md; an unverified high-risk assumption stops the freeze until the user answers it.** Plans and evidence carried guesses as prose ("presumably", "should be"), so a wrong premise surfaced only in review or production. Rule: PLAN.md and a consolidated EVIDENCE.md (where the class has one) each have `## Assumptions`: the single line `none`, or the table `| id | assumption | source | risk | verification | status | decisions |` (id `AS-<n>`, risk `low|medium|high`, status `verified|unverified`, decisions = the criteria or sections it affects). A `verified` row, and a `high` row in either status, needs a verification method; an id is unique per file and an id present in both files is one assumption (higher risk and less verified status win). `validate-plan.mjs` and `verify-claims.mjs` check the shape (the `accepted-by-user` status is never written in a file); `agent.sh freeze` and `refreeze` run the same check in `agent.sh` (`assumption_scan`) and then refuse while a `high` unverified assumption has no accepting answer: for each, freeze records one question through the existing D13 `ask` machinery, bound to the assumption by a line `ASSUMPTION: <id>@<12 hex of sha256(text)>`, so `next`/`questions`/`answer` and RESULT NEEDS_INPUT carry it to the user. The assumption becomes `accepted-by-user` only when its question has an answer file with `answered_by: user` (the existing capture check: the words are in the user's own prompt) whose text is exactly `accept <id>`. `answer` makes that so only when the user's captured prompt STARTS with `accept <id>` (several leading `accept AS-n` clauses joined by comma, `;`, `&` or `and` are allowed, the bound id among them) and the rest of the prompt holds no negation or condition word (not, never, no, n't, do not, only if, if, unless, but, except, until, yet, wait, without, maybe; Turkish: değil, hayır, olmasın, sadece eğer, ama, eğer). A prompt that merely contains the phrase ("please do not accept AS-1 yet", "I never said accept AS-1 ok", "accept AS-1 only if the table is empty", "the acceptance criteria") accepts nothing, the lead's `accept AS-1` is then recorded as `answered_by: lead`, and the user's accept is stored normalized as exactly `accept <id>`; the substring check of `answer` (A41) is still required first. The answer file is the reference and the `answer` ledger row names the assumption. A different answer, a lead-recorded one, or a changed assumption text (new hash) does not accept anything. `agent.sh verify-assumptions <ID>` is the read-only view. `COMPLETION_REPORT.md` has a mandatory `## Assumptions` section; `publish-completion-report` rejects a report that omits an unverified or accepted-by-user assumption id, that states a different status word on the line naming it (`AS-1 verified` fails while the register says unverified), or (for an accepted one) omits its answer file. Low and medium unverified assumptions do not block; they are listed. No separate skill and no new role: the section is part of plan-contract and evidence-map. A non-accepting answer is not final: the next `freeze` asks again (the lead is expected to have verified the assumption or changed the plan by then). Tested in `gates-test.sh` (`sc_assumptions`), `tests/skills/plan-contract.test.mjs` and `evidence-map.test.mjs`. — Alternatives rejected: a numeric confidence score per assumption (a guess about a guess; reproduced/traced evidence already separates what is known); the lead deciding which assumptions to accept (the point is that the user's answer is the only acceptance); a separate assumptions file or skill (a second source of truth that drifts from the plan it constrains); blocking on every unverified assumption (noise: low and medium risk are reported, not stopped on); storing acceptance in PLAN.md (it would change the frozen hash and invalidate the approvals bound to it). — Limits: risk is the author's judgement (a mislabelled `high` as `low` is not caught mechanically; the plan reviewer sees it); the shape check cannot tell whether a named verification method was actually run; acceptance is matched against the latest prompt capture only (an older prompt's phrase does not count once a newer prompt was captured); the negation and condition word list is a heuristic, not a parser (a negation phrased with a word outside it, or in another language, is not caught); `approve` and the other `answer` uses keep the plain substring check of A41, which has the same weakness for negated or quoted phrases, and are not changed here.
- **A85. evidence-map `impact` mode: a machine-readable, versioned impact report of what a change reaches, produced by the Explorer, with the limits of static analysis written into every report.** Plan scope, test-trust's diff mode, verify-proof and contract-proof each need "what else does this change touch" and each would have grown its own grep. Rule: `skills/evidence-map/scripts/impact.mjs` (dependency-free Node, read-only: file reads and `git ls-files`, `git diff`, `git show`; never runs project code, installs nothing) takes changed symbols (`--symbol name[@path]`), files (`--file`: the public declarations) or a base ref (`--base`: declarations whose lines the diff touches, plus symbols removed or deleted) and writes one JSON report, `version: 1` (`skills/evidence-map/schema/impact.schema.json`, documented in `references/IMPACT.md`). Per symbol: references with `path`, `line`, the trimmed `text` and a `kind`: `exact` (bound to the definition through the import graph: named, aliased, namespace, any number of re-exports, python `from` imports, go qualified or same-package use, or inside the defining file), `heuristic` (a code occurrence whose binding was not resolved, member access on an unknown receiver, a language without resolution, a test that only imports an affected file, a contract naming it with another case or separator) or `textual` (the identifier in a comment, string, prose or contract spelling); a same-named identifier bound to another definition is never a reference and is listed under `excluded_homonyms`; affected files and modules (`defines`, `references-symbol`, `imports-defining-module`); contract files (OpenAPI, AsyncAPI, JSON Schema, protobuf, GraphQL, Avro, Thrift) that mention the symbol or are themselves changed; tests (`references-symbol`, `imports-defining-module`, `covers-affected-file`). Every report carries `limits` (dynamic dispatch, reflection, string-built names, generated code, cross-repo consumers, unresolved imports, language coverage, the comment and string scanner) and `unknown` (`cross-repo-consumers` always, `dynamic-sites` as path:line when eval, reflection or computed names exist); "no references found" is "none found statically", never "safe". `validate-impact.mjs` checks shape, enums, the mandatory limits and the cross-repo unknown, the consistency of the lists and summary counts, and with `--repo` re-reads every cited line against its recorded text, so a consumer can trust a report it did not generate; the report is deterministic (no timestamp), so a consumer may regenerate it instead. The consumption interface (which fields plan scope, test-trust diff mode, verify-proof and contract-proof read) is in `IMPACT.md`; those skills are not edited by A85; contract-proof (A83) reads the report through the same interface. A language server or analysis tool that already exists on the machine is detected and recorded in `analysis.tools` (`used: []`); nothing is installed and nothing requires one. No new role and no new skill: the Explorer runs a mode of evidence-map; the role source and both adapters mention it. Tested by `fixtures/impact-map` (a diff with a known answer written from planted facts first: alias through a barrel, a test reaching the symbol through the re-export, a generated caller, a homonym in another module with its own tests, a file importing the same barrel without the symbol, a runtime-built name, the identifier in a comment, a string and the README, a contract that mentions it, one that names it with another spelling, one that does not) through `selfcheck.sh` and `tests/skills/evidence-map-impact.test.mjs` (also python, go, unresolved-import and removed-symbol cases). — Alternatives rejected: requiring a language server (installs are forbidden and most machines lack one; the text and import-graph result is the floor, and a tool result would be one more `exact` source later); text search only without binding (every homonym and every comment would be a dependency and the plan scope would balloon); a full parser per language (dependencies, and a maintenance surface far beyond a bounded Explorer step); a numeric risk score per symbol (a guess; kinds and limits state what is known); putting the report into the Explorer's markdown artifact (the claims table is verified claim by claim, a report of hundreds of rows is not a claim); a separate role or skill for impact (the same read-only, claim-verified discipline already lives in evidence-map). — Limits: text search sees no dynamic dispatch, reflection, runtime-built names, DI or route-table wiring, generated code that is not in the repository, or consumers in other repositories; import resolution covers relative imports (python also by path suffix, go by package directory), not aliases, workspace packages or `tsconfig` paths; default-export aliases, overloads and same-named members of different types are not told apart; the comment and string scanner is line-based; a module is a directory; a non-exported symbol is reported only when the diff touches it; detected language servers are recorded, not invoked.
- **A83. contract-proof: the REVIEW `contract` focus proves the compatibility of every changed API, event and data contract, classifies each change by a written rule, and fails a breaking change the plan does not version, migrate or deprecate.** What: a new standalone skill `skills/contract-proof`, run by the Reviewer for FOCUS `contract` inside REVIEW_RULES (REVIEW_SMALL on tier small) when the A78 pipeline selects it (`contract_file` signal or `contract_change: yes`); no new gate, no new role. Method: (1) a compatibility tool that already exists on the machine or in `node_modules/.bin` (`oasdiff`, `openapi-diff`, `buf`, `graphql-inspector`, `asyncapi`) is detected and, when present, run and recorded as measured evidence (`tools.used`); nothing is installed. (2) Always, for the formats it parses, `scripts/diff-contract.mjs` (dependency-free Node, read-only: file reads, `git diff`, `git show`, `git ls-files`) compares base and head: OpenAPI/Swagger, AsyncAPI and JSON Schema in JSON (own parser with line numbers) or a safe YAML subset (block mappings and sequences, quoted and plain scalars, one-line flow collections, block scalars; anchors, aliases, tags, merge keys, complex keys, multi-documents and multi-line plain or flow scalars are refused), with local and relative external `$ref` resolution (an external fragment is loaded from the same side and classified where the root uses it, also when only the fragment changed and the root did not; cycles, URLs and paths leaving the repository are compared as text; a schema reached from an operation is classified where it is used, so moving an inline schema into `components` is no change unless the resolved shape differs); GraphQL SDL and protobuf with a conservative line-level parser (one statement per line, simple single-line bodies split; multi-line argument lists, several fields on one line, statements spanning lines, `extend` and groups are refused). A file it cannot parse and no tool covers is `status: unsupported`, never a pass; so is a changed JSON/YAML file under a contract path (config-free defaults: `openapi*`, `asyncapi*`, `*schema*`, `api/`, `contracts/`, `specs/`, `schemas/`, `events/` ...) that is neither a contract nor reached by one, an external fragment that cannot be loaded, and a run that recognizes no contract file at all (review round 1 finding: such files were dropped and the run passed). Round 2: every changed JSON/YAML file that is not a contract (a JSON Schema is recognized by any root schema keyword, so enum-only or oneOf-only schemas count) and not reached from one is UNSUPPORTED, except a short documented not-a-contract list of config files (`package.json`, lock files, `tsconfig*.json`, linter, CI and compose files) that is listed under `ignored` as info and never when a `$ref` names it; roots are found by parsing every JSON/YAML file (quoted keys, flow style) and may be JSON Schemas; `/x.yaml` references resolve from the repository root; `required` is diffed as a set; the plan's `## Contracts` section ends at any level 1-2 heading, skips non-goals/out-of-scope children, fences and HTML comments; coverage is clause-level with a wider negation list and bare elements only as code spans; YAML 1.1 booleans, duplicate keys, beyond-2^53 integers and `|+` are UNSUPPORTED; parsed maps have no prototype. Round 3: every file is parsed first and a recognized contract is always compared, whatever its name (a real OpenAPI at `.github/openapi.yaml` was skipped by name); the not-a-contract list is narrowed (`.github/workflows/` and similar, not `.github/`); a JSON Schema needs a json-schema.org `$schema`, one schema-shaped strong keyword or two weak ones (a lone `type` in an i18n file or package.json is not one); `.agents/config.yaml` `contract.paths` / `contract.ignore` globs (validated by `config-check`, frozen with the risk mapping in `freeze.risk_sha256`, covered by `risk-test.sh`) force files in or skip non-contract files; `validate-contract.mjs --path` re-runs the differ with the report's paths; the negation lexicon covers hedges (`instead of`, `defer`, `pending`, `TBD`, `drop`, `?` ...) and RULES.md states that coverage is lexical and the plan is human-reviewed; numbered bullets count. (3) Every change gets one rule id of `references/RULES.md` (`CP-B01`..`CP-B10` breaking, `CP-N01`..`CP-N10` non-breaking, `CP-U01` needs-review, counted as breaking until a reviewer reclassifies it with traced evidence); the id fixes the classification. Rules are direction-aware: request side (parameters, request bodies, GraphQL arguments and inputs), response side (responses, GraphQL output fields), and both (messages, standalone schemas, protobuf, enums whose use is unknown), where the stricter of the two applies. Removed operations, fields, enum values or status codes, type narrowing, a changed `pattern`/`format`/`const` on any side, optional to required on the request, a new required request field (a `default` does not help in OpenAPI or JSON Schema, because `required` rejects the absent value first; a GraphQL non-null argument with a default is non-breaking) and protobuf renumbering are breaking; an added optional field, an added operation, a deprecation marker, documentation (including `info.version`), and an enum value or status code added on the response side are non-breaking, the last two only for consumers that tolerate unknown values, so they are info rows with their consumers. (4) In-repo consumers come from the evidence-map impact report (A85: `impact.mjs --symbol <element>`, checked with `validate-impact.mjs`, merged by `diff-contract.mjs --impact`; prose and comment references are not consumers); without it, a plain search (`consumers.source: search`, confidence medium); consumers outside the repository are always `unknown`. (5) Gate: a breaking or needs-review change is covered only by a PLAN.md `## Contracts` bullet that names the exact file path, the whole qualified element (`Order.customerEmail`, `POST /invoices.currency`, `Customer.orders(status)`; `Order.id` never covers `User.id`) and versioning, migration or deprecation that is not negated and not only `info.version`; an uncovered one is a blocking row (FAIL); judged observations are a separate section and block only when they violate a written spec or plan criterion; with no blocking row, an unsupported file makes the result UNSUPPORTED (A78 semantics). `scripts/validate-contract.mjs` re-derives coverage from the plan with `--plan` (a report's `covered: true` is never trusted without it), re-runs the differ with `--repo/--base` and refuses a dropped file or change or a rewritten rule (only a `CP-U01`, recorded in `original_rule`, may be reclassified, with traced evidence), and checks every change for a known rule id, a matching classification, measured or traced evidence (tool evidence names a used tool; traced evidence names what was read), coverage citing a bullet that really states versioning, migration or deprecation and names the element, consumers searched for every breaking change and `outside_repo: unknown`; it prints RESULT, CONFIDENCE and the `agent.sh submit` findings rows. Responsibilities: api-design (Architect) designs the contract change and proposes the `## Contracts` text before FREEZE; contract-proof (Reviewer) verifies what the diff actually changed against that frozen text; both SKILL.md files say so. Wired through `roles/reviewer.md` (skills list, the `contract` focus row, regenerated Claude and Codex adapters), `GATES.md` and `docs/concepts/gates.md`; A78's UNSUPPORTED text is amended (the `contract` focus is UNSUPPORTED only for an unparseable file no tool covers). Tested by `fixtures/contract-proof` (answers written from planted facts first: a breaking removal hidden in a move to `$ref`, a rename that keeps a deprecated alias in OpenAPI and GraphQL, a required field with a default, a documentation-only AsyncAPI change, a plan-covered enum removal, an uncovered protobuf renumbering, a YAML anchor that must be UNSUPPORTED, and from review round 1: a changed fragment behind an external `$ref` of an unchanged root, an unreferenced changed file under `api/`, negated, `info.version` and wrong-type coverage bullets, a response-side format change, a request enum addition; the whole diff FAILs with exactly the planted blocking rows (validated with `--plan` and `--repo/--base`), the covered/doc-only/GraphQL subset PASSes, the anchor file alone is UNSUPPORTED) through `selfcheck.sh` and `tests/skills/contract-proof.test.mjs`. — Alternatives rejected: requiring or installing a compatibility tool (installs are forbidden and most projects have none; the built-in differ is the floor and an existing tool is one more measured source); vendoring a full YAML library or a GraphQL/protobuf parser into the skill (a dependency and a large surface; a refused construct is reported UNSUPPORTED instead of guessed); a textual line diff of contract files (cannot see a removal hidden behind a `$ref` move and calls every reformat a change); classifying without direction (either every response addition or every request tightening would be misclassified); treating needs-review as non-breaking until proven otherwise (an unknown change would pass silently); any mention of the element in the plan as coverage ("refactor, no consumer-visible change" would cover the very break it hides), or a file name, a last name segment or a keyword without its negation as coverage (round 1: "no versioning needed", `b/openapi.yaml`, `Order.id` for `User.id` all covered); `info.version` or a version-like path as automatic coverage (a number moving is not a migration path for consumers); a separate gate or role for contracts (the gate set and the roles are fixed, D14 and A78); api-design both designing and verifying (the designer would approve its own design). — Limits: the YAML subset and the line-level GraphQL/protobuf parsers refuse valid but unusual syntax (UNSUPPORTED, never PASS: folded paragraphs, `{x:1}`, statements spanning lines); URL and out-of-repository `$ref`s are compared as text; the contract path defaults are fixed patterns, so a contract kept elsewhere and not recognizable by content is missed; `allOf`/`oneOf`/`anyOf` are compared by position and a length change is needs-review; semantics not written in the schema (a field whose meaning changes, server-side defaults) are invisible; GraphQL descriptions and protobuf comments are not compared; a protobuf rename is a removal plus an addition (conservatively breaking); consumers are found statically in this repository only. — `skills/contract-proof/` (`SKILL.md`, `references/RULES.md`, `scripts/diff-contract.mjs`, `scripts/validate-contract.mjs`, `schema/contract.schema.json`, `assets/`), `skills/api-design/SKILL.md`, `roles/reviewer.md`, `control-plane/agents/GATES.md`, `fixtures/contract-proof/`, `tests/skills/contract-proof.test.mjs`. *Amended by A98:* the UNSUPPORTED result is unproven and reported by default (blocking only under `risk.unsupported: block`); a FAIL with unsupported rows stays blocking.
- **A92. Review fixes of the control plane (risk signals, C-14, assumption acceptance, next, freeze, report check, metrics).** What: (F1) `risk_compute` matches a `content` pattern on the file's base version or its whole current version, not only on added lines, so editing or deleting inside a contract recognised by a root key (`openapi:`) still raises the contract signal; a file new in the diff matches on its content. (F2) the default `risk.contract_file.content` is narrowed to a `"$schema"` that names json-schema.org, and the contract content signal skips paths in `contract.ignore` and the not-a-contract list (`risk_not_contract` in agent.sh, a verbatim copy of contract-proof's `NOT_A_CONTRACT`, guarded by an equality test in risk-test; skills stay standalone). (F3) `record` clears the FOCUS of a task result (DONE, NEEDS_INPUT, BLOCKED of a non-reviewer role), so a risk focus cannot open a second C-14 round. (F4) `assumption_prompt_accepts` also refuses `reject`, `decline`, `or`, `actually`, `instead`, `rather` (and Turkish `reddet`, `yerine`). (F5) `next` prints no implementer fix-round lines when every failing blocking finding is an UNSUPPORTED or missing-flag script finding; it prints the waiver or amend-and-refreeze guidance only. (F6) `freeze` inserts `freeze.risk_sha256` when RUN.yaml lacks it, and `verify-freeze` fails closed with guidance when the key is missing. (F7) the freeze-mismatch message names `contract:` too, GATES.md lists `security.*`, and the completion-report check searches only the `## Unsupported Checks` section it names. (F8) metrics labels pass the run id to `gate_of`, so a small-tier record says REVIEW_SMALL. — Alternatives rejected: **importing contract-proof's list into the control plane** (skills are standalone, no cross-skill file reads); **binding a missing `risk_sha256` on first verify** (a verify that writes state is surprising and would bind whatever config exists then, not the frozen one); **a word-by-word allow list for acceptance** (an open vocabulary; the refusal list fails closed by asking again); **treating `$schema` of any host as a contract** (it fires on tsconfig, biome, renovate and similar configs and creates a finding the reviewer cannot clear). — Limits: content signals read the whole file, so a file that merely mentions `openapi:` at line start (a doc fixture) still signals unless ignored; the copied not-a-contract list can drift from the skill's (the risk-test equality guard catches it); an acceptance with a negation the list does not know is still taken as accepted. — `control-plane/agents/scripts/agent.sh`, `control-plane/agents/config.yaml`, `control-plane/agents/GATES.md`, `control-plane/agents/scripts/risk-test.sh`.
- **A84. data-design migration validation: the REVIEW_DATA `migration` focus applies a migration only on a throwaway database the project's own test infrastructure creates, and reports measured and judged findings separately.** The `data` focus compares the frozen plan with the diff by text; nothing observed what a migration does to a schema and to rows, whether it rolls back, or whether the old and the new application can run against it during a rollout. Rule: `skills/data-design/references/MIGRATION-VALIDATION.md` (no new skill, no new gate, no new role; A78 already routes the focus and the report path `reviews/data-migration/REPORT.md`). (1) Database safety (A50, A52) stays authoritative: the only database touched is one the test suite's own helper creates and removes; no connection string is read from env, config or docs and none is recorded; the runtime hook decides, the skill never works around a denial; destructive statements (the classifier's `destructive`/`unknown`) are never executed, even there, so such a migration is `applied: no` with "not executed (destructive statement)" and gets static findings only; the agent never deploys, rollback guidance stays in `## Rollback` and the completion report. (2) Capability: a harness proven by a real trial run of an existing test, else `throwaway_db: no` and status UNSUPPORTED, never PASS, nothing installed; the static scan still runs. (3) Method: the hook denies `database.migrate_commands` in every environment, so the agent issues no migrate, dump, seed or rollback command; applying, snapshotting (before, after, after rollback) and exporting seed rows happen only inside the project's own test command, whose output files the agent compares; no such test, or a hook denial of it, is `throwaway_db: no` and UNSUPPORTED. A down migration with destructive statements is never executed by the agent: the rollback check is `unsupported` ("not executed (destructive statement)") unless the project's own test runs it; without a down file the rollback is a new forward migration and the check is `na` with that reason. PASS needs `schema_diff` and `data_preservation` both `pass`; every `na` states its reason. (4) Evidence classes: `schema_diff`, `rollback`, `data_preservation` are measured (snapshot diff, row counts); `lock_risk` and `long_running` are judged unless a duration was measured; scan hazards (NOT NULL without default, type narrowing, full-table rewrite, index without concurrent option, drop or rename of a column the app still reads, unbatched backfill, constraint without NOT VALID) are `traced` when statement plus context prove them (the table declared populated, both types known and narrowing, a reader found at `path:line`) and `judged` otherwise; mixed-version compatibility (old app + new schema, new app + old schema after a rollback or when the app deploys first) is derived from the measured diff and is judged unless a reader is cited. A judged finding is `warn` and fails only by citing a written spec or plan criterion; measured and traced findings fail. (5) Dependency-free helpers: `scripts/migration-check.mjs` (`scan` reuses the classifier's lexer and statement classification; `diff` takes two schema dumps, DDL or JSON, and also prints the compatibility lists and whether the rollback restored the schema; `data` compares seed rows, recognising a renamed column as not a loss) and `scripts/validate-migration.mjs` (shape, classes, status derived from checks: any fail -> FAIL; no throwaway database, an unsupported check or a migration not applied -> UNSUPPORTED; connection strings refused). The reviewer role text carries the focus; adapters regenerated. Fixture `fixtures/migration-validation` (planted before any code: a clean additive migration, NOT NULL without default on a populated table, an expand/contract rename that is a decoy, the same rename in place with a reader, a non-concurrent index, a data-losing type narrowing, a column drop the app reads; dumps and seed files, no engine needed), asserted by `selfcheck.sh` and `tests/skills/migration-validation.test.mjs`. — Why: a migration's risk is in what it does to a real schema and rows and to a mixed-version rollout; a text diff cannot show that, but a destructive or unobserved check must never read as a pass. — Alternatives rejected: a new skill (the method belongs to data-design's review mode and shares its classifier); applying on any local or staging database (A50 and A52 are absolute; "throwaway" must mean created by the test suite); PASS for static-only analysis when no harness exists (it would read as a check that ran); executing destructive statements on the throwaway database to measure them (A52: no environment); lock durations estimated by the script (an invented number; judged until measured); installing an engine or a container to create a capability (nothing is installed); failing the gate on judged lock estimates alone (the shared rule: judged never closes the gate without a written criterion). — Limits: the test engine's lock and planner behaviour is not production's; seeds model only some data shapes; readers are a text match in the paths given and other repositories are unknown; the dump tool decides what a snapshot contains. — `skills/data-design/` (`SKILL.md`, `references/MIGRATION-VALIDATION.md`, `scripts/migration-check.mjs`, `scripts/validate-migration.mjs`), `roles/reviewer.md`, `fixtures/migration-validation/`, `tests/skills/migration-validation.test.mjs`. *Amended by A98:* no throwaway database (UNSUPPORTED) is unproven and reported by default, not blocking; the report says what was not proven.
- **A86. concurrency-proof: the REVIEW `concurrency` focus reports only proven problems, each a concrete interleaving, and never asserts safety.** The `concurrency` focus of A78 had no skill and could only report UNSUPPORTED. Rule: the Reviewer runs `skills/concurrency-proof` for FOCUS `concurrency` (REVIEW_RULES, REVIEW_SMALL on tier small; report `reviews/concurrency-proof/`). Scope: races, deadlock, lost update, double processing, idempotency, retry side effects, message ordering and redelivery, timeouts, transaction boundaries, lock lifecycle. Method: (1) `scripts/find-shared-state.mjs` (dependency-free, read-only: file reads, `git diff`, `git ls-files`) lists candidate lock, transaction, atomic, retry, timeout, queue, concurrency, suspension, shared-state, check-then-act and idempotency sites in the changed files with conservative text patterns, marking the lines the diff touches; it is a candidate list, never a verdict, and says so in its `limits`; a language-independent checklist per category (`references/SHARED-STATE.md`) turns candidates into shared state, actors and critical sections, with the runtime's interleaving model deciding where an actor can be interrupted (only at suspension points on a single-threaded event loop); an evidence-map impact report, when one exists, is read for the other callers (no cross-skill script call). (2) Every finding carries a traced schedule: at least two named actors, each taking a step, steps numbered in time order, `file:line` per step, the finding's location among the steps, and the observable outcome; `validate-concurrency.mjs --repo --base` re-reads every cited line (file inside the repository, line exists, the step's `code`, required with `--repo`, on the line) and requires a step on a line the diff adds or changes, otherwise the problem is `pre_existing` (info). (3) Where the project's own test runner can drive the schedule (concurrent calls, a slow fake, repeated runs), a reproduction runs in a throwaway `concurrency-proof-*` worktree with the command, runs (>= 2), failing runs (>= 1) and observed failure recorded; only databases the test setup creates and throws away (A50, A52); nothing installed (no race detector or load tool that is not already part of the toolchain). `reproduction.status` is always stated: `ran`, `unsupported` with the reason (traced findings stay valid; confidence medium; an `info` row says so) or `none_needed` (only with nothing found). (4) Gate rule, mechanical: an `open` finding (traced or reproduced, in changed code, not accepted by a cited plan or spec line) or a judged item that violates a cited written criterion is `blocking` -> FAIL; otherwise PASS, which means "no proven problem"; examined but unproven risks are a separate `unproven_risks` list (`## Unproven risks` in REPORT.md) and `info` rows; judged items are separate and never close the gate alone. The verdict summary and the Verdict and Summary sections of REPORT.md (any heading level and case) may not contain a safety phrase as whole words (`safe`, `safely`, `thread-safe`/`thread safe`, `race-free`, `deadlock-free`, `concurrency-safe`, `race-condition-free`, `free of races/deadlocks/race conditions`, `cannot race/deadlock`, `no races`, `guaranteed correct/safe`, `no concurrency issues/problems`) after fenced code, inline code and a double-quoted single token are removed (so quoted code and identifiers such as `safeParse`, `is_safe`, `thread_safe`, and the words `unsafe` and `safety`, never match, while a quoted phrase with spaces is still scanned; negated forms are rejected too, the report states what was proven), and a PASS/FAIL word in the Verdict section must equal the computed result. Because the skill exists, the focus is no longer UNSUPPORTED for a missing skill; a missing reproduction capability is the `unsupported` reproduction status inside a normal result. Tested by `tests/skills/concurrency-proof.test.mjs` (validator rules, safety-word true and false positives, CLI, the scanner) and `fixtures/concurrency-proof` (known answer written from planted facts first, each fact proven by `selfcheck.sh`: a lost update, a check-then-act double shipment, a timed-out retry that charges twice; decoys that must not be flagged: a mutex-guarded reservation with release in `finally`, a retry that reuses its idempotency key; trap: a synchronous cache check-and-set on the event loop). The reviewer role source lists the skill, adapters regenerated. — Alternatives rejected: declaring code "safe" or "thread-safe" after review (absence of a found schedule is not a proof of absence; a PASS worded as safety would be read as a guarantee no review can give); counting a pattern hit (lock, retry, shared variable) as a finding (most hits are correct code, the decoys and trap of the fixture show it; a finding needs a schedule); requiring a reproduction for every finding (many runtimes or projects cannot drive a schedule deterministically in a test; a traced schedule with cited lines is checkable evidence, and the missing reproduction is stated, not hidden); installing a race detector, model checker or stress tool (forbidden; a detector already in the toolchain may be used as a flag of the project's own runner); a per-language static analyzer (dependencies and a maintenance surface; the heuristic list plus the reviewer's reading is the floor); a numeric risk score per site (a guess; the evidence classes and the unproven list already say what is known); reporting unproven risks as blocking (noise that blocks correct code; they stay visible as `info`). — Limits: the scanner is text-only (no parsing, no call graph, aliases and framework wiring not seen) and only scans changed files; the validator proves shape, cited lines and the changed-line link, not that a schedule is possible in the runtime or that a guard really fails, which stays the reviewer's reading of the code; a reproduction of a timing-dependent race may show in few runs, so `failures` of `runs` is reported, not a probability; the safety-word check covers the verdict and summary only (English words), not every free-text field. *Amended by A98:* where an UNSUPPORTED focus result still occurs, it is unproven and reported by default, never a PASS.
- **A82. visual-proof: the QA gate's `visual` focus measures the changed UI in a browser the project or machine already has; judged quality is kept apart and never closes the gate alone.** What: a new skill `skills/visual-proof` run by QA as the `visual` focus A78 selects (UI path or PLAN `## UI`; TRIVIAL has none).
