Tooling is detected, never installed: `measure.mjs --detect` finds a Chromium-family binary on `PATH` or in the standard application folders, or a browser-test dependency the project has installed; a runtime's browser tool is an optional adapter; nothing found -> `UNSUPPORTED` (A78: blocking, never a PASS, user waiver only). `scripts/measure.mjs` (no dependencies) drives the browser over the DevTools protocol with a child process and Node's global `WebSocket`; for a project tool or adapter it prints the page probe (`--print-probe`) and builds the same report from the tool's raw results (`--assemble`). Pages load only from `file:` or localhost. Measured checks per cell (screen x state x viewport), each with check id, viewport, selector, value, threshold and the screenshot path with sha256: page overflow (elements inside a scroll or clip container other than html/body exempt), horizontal scroll, tab-reachable elements invisible even when focused, interactive elements covered at their centre, computed text contrast (WCAG 2.2, large-text threshold), touch-target size (24px with the 2.5.8 spacing exception, or the spec's minimum), focus order by real Tab presses, focus visibility by pixel change, every spec state rendered (a ready selector per state; a state that does not render is a failure and nothing else is claimed for that cell), configured interactions (form validation, modal, dropdown, long content) and the project's own `axe-core` when installed. Judged items (hierarchy, density, consistency, typography) sit in their own list, cite a screenshot and the spec section, and FAIL only as a `violation` that quotes a criterion written in the spec or plan (checked verbatim). Depth: STANDARD = default state x 2 viewports, measured only (judged rejected); COMPLEX/CRITICAL = every spec state x 3 viewports, measured plus at least one judged item per aspect. `validate-visual.mjs` checks the shape, every screenshot's existence and hash, status against value and threshold, cell coverage of every core check, the depth rules and the `visual_proof:` ids of the frozen plan, and recomputes the result: UNSUPPORTED (no tooling) / FAIL (measured failure, judged violation, failing regression run) / INCONCLUSIVE (a required cell not measured or a required criterion unproven; submitted as BLOCKED) / PASS. Visual regression only through the project's existing snapshot infrastructure; baselines are never created. Screenshots live in `reviews/visual-proof/` and are removed with the run; the completion report's `## QA Result` carries the validator's SUMMARY line. Interface with A81: the PLAN `## UI` line `visual_proof:` lists the criteria needing visual evidence; verify-proof's `validate-verify.mjs --plan` rejects a PASS row for such a criterion unless it cites `visual-proof: AC-n proven`, so without visual evidence it is MISSING; the verifier role reads `reviews/visual-proof/report.json`. Fixture `fixtures/visual-proof` (static HTML/CSS/JS, no build): planted overflow at 360px, a 2.32:1 contrast, a spec'd error state never rendered, a 16px crowded touch target; traps: an intentionally scrollable carousel and a visually hidden skip link. `selfcheck.sh` (browser-less, every pull request) proves the planted facts and the UNSUPPORTED path; `browser-check.sh` measures it in a real browser and compares with EXPECTED (main/nightly CI only; a runner without a browser prints UNSUPPORTED, the accepted result there). — Why: a UI criterion checked by reading code or by a model looking at a screenshot is an opinion; overflow, contrast, target size, focus and rendered states are measurable, and a hash ties each claim to the image it came from. — Alternatives rejected: proofline creating and keeping its own visual baselines (a baseline captured from the change under review proves nothing, and it adds files the project never asked for); installing or downloading a browser or a browser-test package when none exists (the no-install rule; a missing tool must be visible as UNSUPPORTED, not silently provisioned); a model judging screenshots as the gate (not reproducible; kept as the judged section that only fails a written criterion); a new gate type or role for visual checks (D14: the focus lives in QA); a third-party browser-driver dependency in the skill (skills stay dependency-free; the DevTools protocol is reachable with Node built-ins); measuring every state at STANDARD (cost without a matching risk; COMPLEX/CRITICAL carry it). — Limits: Chromium-family browsers only for the built-in driver (others through a project tool); contrast reads background colours only (images, gradients, non-ancestor overlays and ancestor opacity are listed as not measured or ignored); focus visibility counts any pixel change; focus order proves reachability and no positive tabindex, not visual reading order (judged); overlap is tested at the element centre; no iframes or shadow roots; states are only as complete as the spec's list and the QA's config, which the validator cannot compare with the spec text. — `skills/visual-proof/`, `roles/qa.md`, `roles/verifier.md`, `skills/verify-proof/` (SKILL.md rule 3, COVERAGE.md, `validate-verify.mjs`), `fixtures/visual-proof/`, `tests/skills/visual-proof.test.mjs`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`. *Amended by A98:* no tooling (UNSUPPORTED) is unproven and reported by default, not blocking; `visual INCONCLUSIVE:` stays blocking.
- **A87. performance-proof: the REVIEW `performance` focus measures base against change with the project's own benchmark and decides by a stated statistical rule; noise is INCONCLUSIVE, never FAIL and never PASS.** New skill `skills/performance-proof` (reviewer role, REVIEW_RULES or REVIEW_SMALL focus `performance`, dispatched only when `agent.sh pipeline` lists it: PLAN.md `## Risk` `performance_critical: yes`, or the optional `performance_hint` content signal, A78). Method: the same command, found only in the project's own scripts, build files, CI config or PLAN.md (never installed, never a tool that is merely on the machine), runs in a base worktree (the frozen base revision) and a change worktree, both built untimed first, interleaved in ABBA order to spread machine drift over both sides (`scripts/run-bench.sh`, POSIX sh, any command, wall time from the first working sub-second clock: `date +%s.%N`, perl, python3, node; a timed-out or failed run is recorded and is not a sample). The environment is recorded and mandatory in the report: OS, CPU count, load average before and after, runtime versions, command, warmup runs, runs per side, order, timer, revisions and timestamps. `scripts/compare-bench.mjs` (dependency-free) computes per side median, MAD, IQR, min and max, and percentile bootstrap intervals of the relative median change (95 % both ends and the 99 % upper end; 2000 resamples, seeded mulberry32, so a validator recomputes them exactly), and decides, first match wins: fewer than `min_runs` (default 10, floor 5) samples on a side = INCONCLUSIVE; median regression above the threshold T (default 10 %) with the 95 % lower end above T/2 = FAIL; noise (the larger relative MAD) above the noise threshold (default 5 %) = INCONCLUSIVE; the 99 % upper end within T (an improvement included) = PASS; otherwise INCONCLUSIVE. The rule is calibrated by a seeded simulation (`scripts/calibrate.mjs`, 500 trials per cell over normal noise 3 % and 8 %, true effects 0, 5, 12 and 15 %, 10 and 20 runs; table in `references/DECISION.md`): at 3 % noise no false verdict; at 8 % noise false FAIL 2.4 % (N=10) and 0.6 % (N=20) for a true +5 %, false PASS 0.2 % for a true +12 % (N=10), and a true +15 % FAILs 54 % (N=10) and 82 % (N=20) of the time, the rest INCONCLUSIVE. The first version (FAIL when the 95 % lower end was above zero, PASS on the 95 % upper end) failed a true +5 % 9.6 % of the time at 8 % noise; requiring the lower end above T instead found a true +15 % only 18 to 22 % of the time; both were rejected. Thresholds: defaults, `.agents/config.yaml` `performance:` (`regression_pct`, `noise_pct`, `min_runs`; `config-check` accepts integers; frozen with the plan through `freeze.risk_sha256`, so a lead cannot raise the threshold mid-run: `verify-freeze` fails, tested in `risk-test.sh`), and the plan's budget on its flag line (`## Risk` `performance_critical: yes budget_pct=5`, 0 < n <= 100, read with `--plan`, frozen with PLAN.md: raising it after freeze fails `verify-freeze` with `freeze mismatch: plan`, tested in `risk-test.sh`) wins over both; the source is recorded. An unparseable budget on the flag line is a note in the report, never silently dropped, and the validator refuses that plan. `compare-bench.mjs --budget-pct` is a local what-if whose source is `cli (unverified)`; the validator refuses that source and takes no budget option, so a reviewer cannot supply its own budget. `validate-performance.mjs --config --plan` recomputes the thresholds and their source and rejects a mismatch (a report declaring `regression_pct: 500` to pass a +30 % change is invalid); without them it prints `thresholds unverified` and the reviewer's CONFIDENCE is at most medium. Samples in different units are refused, never converted. `scripts/validate-performance.mjs` checks the report: environment block present, tool command and the file that defines it, both sides' samples, `min_runs` >= 5, every statistic and status recomputed from the samples, the report status the worst over its measurements (FAIL over INCONCLUSIVE over PASS), INCONCLUSIVE stating that it is not a FAIL with a next step, UNSUPPORTED with a reason and no measurement, every summary sentence that makes a performance claim citing a measurement row of the report and agreeing with its direction ("faster" citing a regression is refused), each side's sample count equal to `environment.runs`, and change samples not identical to the base samples (copied data). `--rows` prints the gate submission: findings rows, or the UNSUPPORTED reason, which always starts `performance INCONCLUSIVE:` or `performance UNSUPPORTED:`. Patterns read from code (N+1 queries, allocation in a hot loop) are `judged` items without measured numbers; they never close the gate, except one that violates a criterion written in the spec or plan, quoted in the item. Gate mapping: PASS no blocking row; FAIL one blocking row per failing measurement; INCONCLUSIVE is submitted as `--result UNSUPPORTED --reason "performance INCONCLUSIVE: ..."` (the text `--rows` prints) (blocking, never a PASS, only the user waives it, listed under `## Unsupported Checks` with "not a FAIL"; a re-measurement replaces it); no benchmark command = UNSUPPORTED. No new gate, role or result word in `agent.sh`. Tested by `fixtures/performance-proof` (synthetic samples with known answers written first: clear regression FAIL, noise INCONCLUSIVE, +4 % PASS, improvement PASS, 3 runs INCONCLUSIVE, equal medians with exploding variance INCONCLUSIVE, `ms` against `s` refused, +15 % FAIL by default and PASS under a 20 % plan or config budget with the plan winning, throughput drop FAIL with `--direction higher`; `selfcheck.sh`) and `tests/skills/performance-proof.test.mjs` (statistics, determinism, thresholds, sample parsing, validator traps, a `run-bench.sh` smoke run with a trivial command and N=2 that asserts no timing). — Alternatives rejected: **a single run per side** (one run cannot separate a change from run-to-run variation; any difference would be a verdict); **mean only** (one outlier run, a GC pause or another process, moves the mean, and a mean without a spread says nothing about noise); **FAIL on noise** (a noisy machine is not evidence against the change; it would fail correct work and teach users to waive the focus; INCONCLUSIVE with a next step keeps it honest without blaming the change); PASS on noise or when the median moved less than the threshold (does not exclude a larger regression hidden in the spread); a new result word INCONCLUSIVE in `agent.sh` (a new gate result needs new record, waiver and report handling; UNSUPPORTED already means "no verdict, never a pass, the user decides"); BLOCKED for INCONCLUSIVE (records no gate and offers no waiver, so a run on a noisy machine could never finish); installing a benchmark harness or using a machine-wide tool (forbidden, and a tool the project does not use measures something the project does not run); a static estimate of complexity as the verdict (a guess, kept as judged); converting units (a mislabelled unit would silently turn a regression into an improvement). — Limits: wall time includes process start-up and the clock's own start-up (the same offset on both sides); the interval assumes runs are exchangeable, so drift faster than the ABBA alternation is not removed; a benchmark that does not exercise the changed code proves nothing and is reported UNSUPPORTED only when the reviewer shows it; an absolute bound ("p95 under 200 ms") is an acceptance criterion for the verifier, not a threshold here; the `run-bench.sh` timeout path (minimum 30 s) is not exercised by the fast test suite; heavy real benchmarks are never run in CI. — `skills/performance-proof/` (`scripts/calibrate.mjs`), `roles/reviewer.md`, `control-plane/agents/config.yaml`, `control-plane/agents/scripts/agent.sh` (`config_check`, `risk_config`), `gates-test.sh`, `risk-test.sh`, `GATES.md`, `fixtures/performance-proof/`, `tests/skills/performance-proof.test.mjs`. *Amended by A98:* `performance INCONCLUSIVE:` stays blocking in both modes; `performance INCONCLUSIVE: thresholds unverified` (a measurement that cannot be verified) blocks too; `performance UNSUPPORTED:` (no benchmark command) is unproven and reported by default, blocking only under `risk.unsupported: block`.
- **A91. Deliberately not done.** Six additions were considered during the A78-A90 work and rejected; each stays out until a measured need shows otherwise. (1) *A new agent or role.* Every new capability (contract, concurrency, performance, visual, migration, dependency, config checks, impact, insights, benchmark) lives in a skill used by an existing role or as a focus of an existing gate, so the dispatch graph, the role sources, the two adapters and the roles-versus-gates mapping stay unchanged. Rejected: a role per capability (more prompts and handoffs, more places for the lead to misroute, no gain in independence because the gate already separates author from judge). (2) *An end-of-task user quiz.* The user already approves the plan, the commit and the push, and the completion report states what was proven and what was not; a quiz adds a blocking round that proves nothing about the code. Rejected: a comprehension quiz before delivery. (3) *A separate decision-memory system.* `docs/decisions.md` and the ADRs written by system-design are the durable, reviewable, diff-able record; a second store would drift from them. Rejected: a decision database or vector memory. (4) *Numeric confidence scores.* Evidence is classified (measured, reproduced, traced, judged) and findings carry a content fingerprint; a number invites false precision and a threshold argument that the classes already settle (judged never closes a gate alone). Rejected: a 0-100 score per finding or per gate. (5) *A ready-made style catalogue for UI design.* A catalogue yields generic output and bypasses the existing `DESIGN.md`; ui-design instead requires a creative direction grounded in the user, the content and the existing system (A81). Rejected: a built-in list of named visual styles. (6) *The agent deploying or managing a release.* The agent never deploys, publishes or releases (the lifecycle ends at the completion report and the user-approved commit and push, A41): delivery stays a user action after the commit and push approvals; the rollback plan stays in data-design's output and the completion report. Rejected: a release or deploy step in the lifecycle, even behind an approval. These were weighed during the review of A78-A90.
- **A93. Review round on performance-proof, visual-proof and concurrency-proof: a report can no longer reach PASS through values the agent chose.** What: (1) performance-proof: the bootstrap constants are pinned (seed 1, 2000 resamples, confidence 0.95); `compare-bench.mjs` has no `--seed` and `validate-performance.mjs` refuses any other seed, resample count or confidence and recomputes with the pinned ones, so a seed whose interval happens to clear the threshold cannot be shopped for. The direction follows the unit: a time (`ms`, `s`, `us`, `ns`, `ns/op`, or a metric named as a time, latency or duration) is lower-is-better and refuses `--direction higher`; a rate (`ops/s`, `req/s`) is higher-is-better and refuses `lower`; the validator checks the same on the report. Only verified thresholds can PASS: without both `--config` and `--plan`, `--rows` submits a PASS as `UNSUPPORTED` (`performance UNSUPPORTED: thresholds unverified ...`, blocking); a FAIL stays a FAIL. Config percentages are bounded to (0, 100] like the plan budget. (2) visual-proof: a pass or fail item needs a numeric value and the documented threshold of its check or a stricter one (contrast `>= 3` only with a `large text` note; `touch_target_min` below 24 refused), and its status is recomputed; `not_applicable` is only the fixed item `measure.mjs` writes when the page has nothing of that kind (selector `body`, the reason as value, alone for its check in the cell, the three focus checks together) and is counted apart from passes in `SUMMARY`. `measure.mjs` pauses every request (`Fetch.requestPaused`) and lets through only the page's own origin plus `data:`/`blob:`; everything else fails with `BlockedByClient` and is listed in `report.json` `network.blocked`; a headless-browser report without `network.interception: on` is invalid. The skill states the A50/A52 database rule: pages and form steps never run against a backend with a real database, only `file:`/static pages or a project test server with throwaway or in-memory data. INCONCLUSIVE is submitted like performance-proof's: `--result UNSUPPORTED` with a `visual INCONCLUSIVE:` reason (the `SUBMIT:` line of `--rows`), because `agent.sh` records UNSUPPORTED for a risk focus as a blocking, waivable gate finding listed under `## Unsupported Checks`, while BLOCKED from qa records no gate at all. (3) concurrency-proof: the agent's own labels never close a traced or reproduced finding. `pre_existing` is valid only with `--repo --base` showing no trace step on a changed line; `closed` needs a structured `recheck` the validator re-checks (a re-run reproduction with 0 failures whose saved copy exists next to `concurrency.json`, or a fix line that the diff changes, in a file of the schedule, not one of its steps, holding the cited code); `accepted_by` must quote, verbatim, a line of a file passed with `--plan`/`--spec`, and a citation of a changed file is refused. With `--repo --base`, `scope.candidates` must equal a fresh `find-shared-state.mjs` count. Why: an independent review reproduced each path to a false PASS (seed 19 instead of 1, `--direction higher` on a +30 % time regression, the visual fixture's 27 measured failures edited to `not_applicable` or loosened thresholds, the traced lost update relabelled `pre_existing`, `closed` with "looked again", or `accepted` by its own code), and found that visual pages could reach a backend and its database. Every repro is now a test (`tests/skills/*-proof.test.mjs`) and a fixture trap (EXPECTED.md items added, none loosened; visual tamper checks in `expect.mjs` on the measured report, main/nightly). Rejected: keeping `--seed` as a what-if option (a second code path that produces reports the validator must refuse anyway); accepting any direction with a warning (a warning does not stop a PASS); capping an unverified PASS at CONFIDENCE medium (medium still satisfies the gate); a per-project allow-list of extra origins for visual-proof (each entry is a way back to a shared backend; a project that needs one measures against its own test server on the same origin); host-resolver rules instead of request interception (they do not record what was blocked); trusting a free-text `recheck` reviewed by a human later (the gate is mechanical, A78); reading the plan for `accepted_by` without the agent passing it (the validator cannot know which file is the frozen plan). Re-review (same decision): unit aliases (`msec`, `milliseconds`, `MS`, `sec`, `seconds`, `usec`, `µs`/`μs`, `nsec`) are times in any case, rates include `fps` and `Hz`, and a unit that names no direction is refused unless the plan's `performance_critical: yes` line declares `direction=lower|higher`. A concurrency fix recheck must be a code line (not a comment) inside the traced function, not one of its steps, carrying a synchronisation token or a stated `fix_token`; a reproduction recheck re-runs a reproduced finding's own command with at least its runs, 0 failures, and a saved copy whose sha256 is recorded and verified and which contains the observed text (a traced finding is closed only by a fix). `accepted_by` must name the finding (id, `path:line`, or a symbol on one of its steps) on a line of a frozen plan or spec: a run artifact under `.agents/runs/<ID>/` (PLAN.md matched against `RUN.yaml` `freeze.plan_sha256`) or, standalone, a file pinned by `accepted_by.sha256`. visual-proof: workers are auto-attached paused and guarded before they run (a dedicated worker has no Fetch domain; its requests pass the page's interception, measured), WebSockets are refused in the page and in every worker by replacing the constructor (`Network.setBlockedURLs` does not stop a handshake, measured with a local listener), service-worker registration and shared workers are refused in the page; all of them are recorded in `network.blocked`, a WebSocket that still opens is `network.unguarded`. Own-origin requests with POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE are listed in `network.same_origin_writes`; without a `visual_writes:` line in the plan or spec, or with `network.interception` other than `on` (a project tool or adapter that does not apply the same allowlist), or with an unguarded target, the result is at most INCONCLUSIVE (submitted as UNSUPPORTED). A contrast threshold of 3 is accepted only when the item's `font_size_px`/`font_weight` are large text (>= 24px, or >= 18.66px bold); horizontal_scroll's bound is the recorded `client_width`, at most the viewport width (980 on a phone viewport of a page without a viewport meta tag, which a first version of this rule rejected wrongly). `fixtures/visual-proof/network-check.mjs` proves on a local listener that no request, worker request or WebSocket handshake reaches it; `abort-check.mjs` captures the child's stderr and exit, waits up to 90 s for a cold browser start and retries that wait once. Rejected: `Network.setBlockedURLs` for WebSockets (measured: the handshake still reaches the server); `--disable-features` flags for service workers (version-dependent names); a span of lines around the trace instead of the enclosing function (a fix in another function next to it would pass); requiring the cited plan text to be the whole finding title (plans word acceptances in their own terms). Limits: the validators check consistency, not truth. A report fabricated wholesale, with passing numbers and the hashes of real files, validates; so does a fix line that carries a token but does not remove the interleaving. The control plane never runs the validators (by design): the reviewer and verifier roles re-check the evidence, and the verifier opens the screenshots and `report.json` (visual) and the cited lines (concurrency) before it relies on a PASS. The enclosing-function check is a text heuristic, and the WebSocket and worker refusals hold for code that uses the global constructors. *Amended by A98:* the unverified-thresholds submission reads `performance INCONCLUSIVE: thresholds unverified ...` (blocking) instead of `performance UNSUPPORTED:`.

- **A75. C-14: intent-grill's interview is exempt from the one-round rule (user decision 2026-10-13; amends A70, resolves its open question).** The three-reply limit and "every role asks all its questions in ONE NEEDS_INPUT round" stay. intent-grill is an inherently multi-round interview (one question per round, in dependency order; fixtures/intent-grill/EXPECTED.md P1 and P9 stay as they are). In a run it submits as the task author with `ARTIFACT: design/INTENT.md`; `record_validate` in `agent.sh` neither counts nor refuses a NEEDS_INPUT submitted by the task author role with artifact `design/INTENT.md` whose `## Status` is `in-progress` (the key is role plus artifact plus that status; the rounds are bounded only by the user's replies). With any other status the NEEDS_INPUT is an ordinary round. `submit` replaces the artifact with an immutable snapshot (`reports/snapshots/<sha12>-task_author-design_INTENT.md`, A43) before `record`, so the check accepts that original-path snapshot name; the result is stored as a `grill: yes` line in the question file, so the interview's rounds are skipped when the role's own round is counted (a task author that grilled can still ask its one ordinary round). The carve-out is written in the shared-rules bullet carried by all skills, in the intent-grill skill, the task author role, `PROTOCOL.md`, `roles/README.md` and `HANDOFF.md`; `tests/e2e/expected/COMMON.md` C-14 gets one clarifying sentence (the rest of the expected files are unchanged). A70's implementation (role sources, `submit`/`record` check, per-role key stated as intentional: a later instance of a role has no second round) is carried over from the stopped branch `fix/one-needs-input-round`. Tested by `sc_questions_grill` and `sc_questions_keys` in `gates-test.sh`. — Rationale (recorded by the author, not stated by the user): a grill is an interview whose next question depends on the last answer, so it cannot be batched, and the user's replies in an interview are the point of it. — Alternatives rejected by the author (not stated by the user): batching the grill's questions and changing fixture EXPECTED P1/P9 (breaks dependency order, the user kept the fixtures); exempting by role (the task author's ordinary round must stay limited); raising the limit (hides the cause). — Limits: the exemption is enforced for the task author role only, through `submit` (architect, data_architect and designer may write under `design/`, so the role is part of the key, tested); rounds are unlimited, not capped, because the interview's length depends on the decision tree and the user's replies bound it; the task author writes `design/INTENT.md` and its `## Status` itself, so a task author that writes `in-progress` there for a non-interview purpose is exempt: a trust convention checked by shape (A75 stops the stub and the other roles, not a deliberate forgery), recorded as residual; a grill with more rounds than the harness reply limit is outside what C-14 counts for that fixture.
### Open technical points — how they were closed

- **User-invoked-only skills in role agents:** verified against the Claude Code docs: a skill with `disable-model-invocation: true` cannot be preloaded or invoked by a subagent, so role definitions point to the skill's SKILL.md path explicitly (A15). Codex reads `.agents/skills/` natively.
- **Platform formats:** verified on 2026-10-09 against code.claude.com docs (plugin, marketplace, subagents, skills, hooks, headless) and Codex 0.162 help output + docs (custom agents in `.codex/agents/*.toml`, skills in `.agents/skills`, hooks in `.codex/hooks.json`, `codex exec` flags). Verified live: PreToolUse input carries `agent_id`/`agent_type` for subagent calls and none for the main session; a PreToolUse deny blocks in `-p` with `bypassPermissions`; SubagentStop context goes to the subagent, so record outcomes are relayed to the lead by its next PostToolUse; Codex project hooks fire and deny. Details: `docs/concepts/enforcement-layers.md`.

- **A94. Review hardening of the skill validators: a check that cannot see the evidence must not pass, and a result the control plane accepts must not be rejected by the lead's parser.** What: an independent review of A78-A90 found validators that accepted a PASS on self-labelled or unverified input. Each fix keeps the evidence classes (measured, reproduced, traced, judged) and adds no role, gate or skill. (1) `skills/proofline-lead/scripts/check-result.mjs` mirrors `agent.sh record_validate`: the reviewer's risk focuses `contract|concurrency|performance|config|migration`, `FOCUS visual` for qa and `dependencies` for security_reviewer are valid, `UNSUPPORTED` is a gate result of a risk focus only (never `dependencies`, an `UNSUPPORTED_REASON` line required, no blocking rows), and the rules-gate focuses accept `REVIEW_RULES-n` or `REVIEW_SMALL-n` row ids because the tier is not known to the parser. (2) contract-proof: `--path` is added to the discovered changed set instead of replacing it; a named path that is not changed against the base, or does not exist, is an `unsupported` row (never `compared` with no changes); a tool in `tools.used` counts only when it is in `tools.detected` (the regenerated report's list when `--repo` is given), left a command naming it and non-empty output, and its `formats` cover the file's format; the plan-coverage negation lexicon gains `n/a`, `w/o`, `out-of-scope`, `as-is`, `impossible`, `later`, `unknown`, `=0`, `: no`; a `oneOf`/`anyOf` whose length changed is compared member by member (removed member `CP-B06` on any side, added member `CP-N03` on the request side and `CP-N04` otherwise) instead of `CP-U01`. (3) data-design: non-SQL migrations (python, JavaScript, TypeScript, Ruby, Go, XML, ...) are token scanned by the scanner `classify-migration.mjs` already had (`drop_table`, `drop_column`, `remove_column`, `DropTable`, `op.drop_*`, `schema.dropTable`, SQL string literals); a destructive or unknown action is `not_executable` (A52); a file with run-time built SQL or no recognised operation is listed under `unanalyzed` (and counts as destructive in `classify`), never skipped silently; `validate-migration.mjs` gains `--repo`, re-classifies each cited migration and refuses `destructive: no` on a destructive or unknown action (V16), an applied file the scanner cannot analyze or a cited file that does not exist (V17), and a `patterns: pass` while the scan traces a blocking hazard; with `down_migration: yes` the status can be PASS only when `rollback` is a measured pass (a `warn` or `judged` rollback derives UNSUPPORTED). (4) exploit-proof: unknown manifest names that decide what is installed or from where (`.npmrc`, `.yarnrc`, `packages.config`, `environment.yml`, `deno.json`, `pip.conf`, `nuget.config`, ...) are listed unparsed (confidence low); a lockfile entry with an unchanged version but another resolved host or integrity is a `replaced` change; a plan mention inside a negating clause ("do not add left-pad") no longer declares the dependency (the lexicon is duplicated from contract-proof, skills share no files); with no plan the validator prints `PLAN_CHECK: not_run` and the confidence is `low`, so the `PASS` is `pass_low` and cannot close the gate (the focus has no `UNSUPPORTED` result, A88), while a missing audit tool stays `medium` with the plan check run. `DEPENDENCIES.md` no longer says the audit step uses no network: the skill's scripts make none, the project's own audit tools may. The shipped risk mapping's `dependency_manifest.paths` now lists every manifest and lockfile name the manifest diff reads or lists unparsed (npm-shrinkwrap, requirements variants, `*.versions.toml`, setup.py/cfg, `.npmrc`, `packages.config`, `environment.yml`, `deno.json`, Package.swift, pubspec, Podfile, ...), with a risk-test scenario. (5) proof-review config: a new line is a line the diff added whose text the same diff did not also remove (position, not set membership in the base file), so a flag flipped to `enabled: true` next to an unchanged `enabled: true` is found; a flag is identified by its parent path plus leaf; `validate-config.mjs --repo --base [--plan]` re-runs the scan and rejects a document that dropped or added an item; `--gate REVIEW_RULES|REVIEW_SMALL` sets the row ids (the SKILL no longer hard-codes `REVIEW_RULES`). (6) evidence-map `impact`: `exports.X =`, `module.exports.X =` and `module.exports = { ... }` are declarations; `.vue` and `.svelte` files are code (script blocks lexed as TypeScript, template references count); readable files over 1 MiB are listed under `unknown` `skipped_large` instead of being dropped. (7) ui-design: a redesign approval needs a recorded answer (`questions/NNN.answer.md` or `INTENT.md#anchor`) or a `Q<n>` of the NEEDS_INPUT round that does not sit next to a meeting, review or planning word; "approved in the Q3 planning review" is refused. (8) benchmark: a `EXPECTED.md` with no id-keyed items but a `Traps`, `Planted facts`, `Must hold` or `known answers` heading is read structurally (table rows and bullets, positional ids `F<n>`/`T<n>`), so the contract-proof, impact-map, insights and migration-validation fixtures are measurable without editing their EXPECTED files; prose is still never an item. (9) The `visual_proof:` line is parsed by one rule in plan-contract's and verify-proof's validators (optional bullet plus space, optional backticks), pinned by tests in both. (10) Follow-up of the re-review: negation words inside hyphenated names (`no-case`, `is-not-empty`, `skip-list`) are not negations (the lexicons require the word not to touch `-_/.@`, and the element or package name is removed from the clause first); heredoc and triple-quoted SQL bodies are scanned, and a paren-less or computed `execute`/`raw` argument, or a string that is no SQL statement, makes the file `unanalyzed` even when other known operations are present; only the up path decides `destructive` (a drop on the down path is `down_destructive`, V18, never executed); an unparsed manifest has an exit: a `manual_review` row `{file, note, diff_sha256}` whose hash the validator recomputes with `--repo/--base` lets the result be `PASS` at `medium` (without the recomputation it stays `low`); a redesign approval `Q<n>` needs round wording (a bare quarter label is refused); `validate-migration.mjs` without `--repo` prints a self-attestation warning and `CONFIDENCE: medium`. — Alternatives rejected: **keeping `--path` as a restriction and requiring the validator to catch the omission** (the validator is not run by the control plane, so the differ itself must not hide changes); **running a discovered ORM migration on the throwaway database to "measure" it** (A52: destructive statements are never executed, and a framework we cannot see may do anything); **a numeric weight per hazard** (A91); **making the dependencies focus UNSUPPORTED without a plan** (A88 fixed that the manifest diff always exists, and `agent.sh` rejects it; `pass_low` has the same effect, the gate stays open); **editing the four EXPECTED files to fit the benchmark parser** (the fixture is the source of truth, the parser follows it); **one shared helper for the negation lexicon or the `visual_proof` rule** (skills are standalone, check-shared.sh pins copied sections only). — Limits: the non-SQL scan is a token and string scan, so a migration that builds names or statements from variables is `unanalyzed`, not understood; a vendored or generated migration framework with its own DSL is read only by the tokens above; negation detection is a word list, not parsing; the host and integrity comparison covers npm lockfiles and `yarn.lock`, not the other lockfile formats; `validate-migration.mjs --repo` needs the migration files in the working tree. — `skills/proofline-lead/scripts/check-result.mjs`, `skills/contract-proof/scripts/diff-contract.mjs`, `skills/contract-proof/scripts/validate-contract.mjs`, `skills/data-design/scripts/classify-migration.mjs`, `skills/data-design/scripts/migration-check.mjs`, `skills/data-design/scripts/validate-migration.mjs`, `skills/exploit-proof/scripts/diff-manifests.mjs`, `skills/exploit-proof/scripts/validate-dependencies.mjs`, `skills/proof-review/scripts/check-config.mjs`, `skills/proof-review/scripts/validate-config.mjs`, `skills/evidence-map/scripts/impact.mjs`, `skills/ui-design/scripts/validate-ui.mjs`, `skills/plan-contract/scripts/validate-plan.mjs`, `skills/verify-proof/scripts/validate-verify.mjs`, `tests/benchmark/benchmark.mjs`, `control-plane/agents/config.yaml`, `control-plane/agents/scripts/risk-test.sh`.
- **A95. The end-to-end harness records the evidence the new capabilities need, and a separate expectation file scores them; no existing item changes.** `tests/e2e/run.sh` writes `out/git-global-before.txt` and `out/git-global-after.txt` (sha256 of `~/.gitconfig`, of the XDG git config and of `git config --global --list`, or `absent`) so C-18 has evidence; the harness only reads the global config. Every harness reply is logged in `out/replies-log.txt` with its kind (`plan-approval` or `question`), task id and pending request, and `summary.txt` carries `plan_approval_replies` and `question_replies` next to `replies`: the second plan approval of a refreeze (replies=6 against the limit of 3 in a COMPLEX run) is now distinguishable from question replies. What is answered is unchanged: a plan approval every time, the scripted reply to a question, never a destructive, single-step, scope, budget, commit, push or waiver request. New artifacts: `agent-pipeline.txt` (always present), `insights.txt` (`proofline insights` after the run, with `insights_exit` and the hash of `.agents` before and after in `summary.txt`), `metrics/*`, `visual-artifacts.txt` (hashed screenshots and visual-proof files). `tests/e2e/expected/CAPABILITIES.md` adds items N-1 to N-6; `evaluate.md` scores them in a separate table and verdict line, so they never loosen or replace an existing item. `tests/e2e/capabilities-test.sh` dry-runs one scenario and asserts the files and the global-config record without a runtime. — Alternatives rejected: adding the N items to COMMON.md or the scenario files (existing EXPECTED files must not change, and runs made before the capabilities existed would fail them); excluding plan-approval replies from the count in the harness (the evaluator needs the raw record and C-14 wording is the authority); a new scenario for the capabilities (the existing scenarios already carry UI, contract and migration paths).
- **A96. A TRIVIAL run has no design phase: UI signals are shown, never acted on.** What: for class TRIVIAL, `risk_ui_exempt` (agent.sh) makes a signal whose check is `visual` (`ui_path`, `ui`) print `check=- (class TRIVIAL: no design phase / no QA gate)` with `design_role=-`, skip it in `risk_missing_flags` (so `next` dispatches no designer and `record` adds no missing-`ui` finding), and drop it from the `design_role.` lines. STANDARD, COMPLEX and CRITICAL are unchanged (designer by signal, as A78). Why: the end-to-end TRIVIAL run (copy fix in `public/index.html`) got `design_role=designer` from `ui_path`, the lead dispatched a designer, `design/UI.md` existed and the lead spent about eight calls on `ui: yes` and `tdd_exemption`; A36 already decided that a TRIVIAL copy edit in a UI file needs no `## UI` section and no designer, and A78 reintroduced it through the signal. The validator needed no change (it is class-aware); the one validate-plan error of that run, `Tests: AC-3 names no test file in backticks that exists or is in scope`, is the Tests rule, answered by naming a scope file in the line. Alternatives rejected: removing the `ui_path` signal for every class (STANDARD copy-only changes before A78 left designer dispatch to the lead's judgment, A78 deliberately made it deterministic); deciding by file extension or diff size (a heuristic the class already encodes); a config switch per class (the lead must not select its own checks). Capability expectations: N-2 `## Risk` is required only when the plan declares a risk flag or `pipeline` lists a flag-requiring signal; N-3 `dispatch` records are expected only when `dispatch.sh` was used (the Agent path writes step and run records). — `control-plane/agents/scripts/agent.sh` (`risk_ui_exempt`), `risk-test.sh`, `GATES.md`, `tests/e2e/expected/CAPABILITIES.md`.
- **A97. C-18 and S-6 from the STANDARD round 1 evaluations: shell writes outside the copy are refused by the hook whatever their spelling, and GREEN/REFACTOR evidence needs an earlier RED and a changed implementation.** What: (1) *Hook.* Reading the runs again, the `/tmp/x` commands counted as C-18 breaches (`cat > /tmp/x`, `> /tmp/x`, `npm test > /tmp/x`) were already denied by the A56 guard ("never /tmp/x directly"); the files named in the evaluation (`qa-t1-*.mjs`, `t1-findings.txt`) are dated before A56, from earlier rounds. What the guard still let through was any absolute target that is neither under `/tmp`/`$TMPDIR` nor in the project: `> ~/x`, `> $HOME/x`, `cp f /Users/me/x`, `tee /opt/x` (the run-scoped path policy caught `~` and `$HOME` inside a run, with another message; nothing caught them before a run exists). `scratch_guard` now denies every shell write (redirection, `tee`, `cp`/`mv`/`install`/`rsync`/`ln` targets, `touch`/`rm`/`truncate`, `dd of=`) whose resolved absolute target is outside the project root and the registered worktrees, `/dev/*` excepted (`> /dev/null`, `2>&1`, `>&2`, `/dev/stderr` stay allowed); `~`, `$HOME` and `$TMPDIR` are expanded, `//` and a `/private` prefix normalised. Both stray-path messages now say "Scratch files go under `.agents/runs/<ID>/scratch/` (before a run exists: `.agents/local/scratch/`); only worktrees go under `$TMPDIR`" instead of the older "go under $TMPDIR". The role texts already state the scratch location in every role table (`roles/*.md`, `roles/README.md`), so they and the generated adapters are unchanged. The Codex adapter runs the same hook script (`adapters/codex/hooks.json`). Residual (policy-only, in ENFORCEMENT.md): writes the tokenizer cannot see, computed paths, `cd` plus a relative path, a script writing a file by itself; the older raw-text `/tmp` check can still refuse a `/tmp` string inside quotes. (2) *Evidence order.* `agent.sh worker-evidence` stores a `scope_snapshot` (sha256 of every frozen scope path) in each record. GREEN and REFACTOR are refused for a non-exempt target path with no RED record of that path; GREEN is refused while no implementation path (a scope path that is not a test file by name or folder; any path if the scope holds only tests) differs from the snapshot of the latest RED for it; REFACTOR is refused before a GREEN or FIX record. A path with `tdd_exemption` is not held to RED; records without a snapshot (older runs) skip the change check. The evaluated run recorded GREEN-1 and REFACTOR-1 seven seconds before RED-1, so a green was recorded before the fix existed. Why: both findings were made from run records; the first was partly a mis-attribution (denied attempts are not writes) but exposed the real gap above. Alternatives rejected: a per-criterion RED/GREEN pairing (evidence records carry paths, not criteria, and `verify-worker-evidence` already pairs per path); deriving "implementation changed" from the diff (new untracked files and the standalone topology's uncommitted tree make hashing the frozen scope more reliable); denying reads of `/tmp` too (outside the finding); tracking `cd` in the tokenizer (a heuristic on top of a heuristic).
- **A101. `freeze` and `refreeze` validate the PLAN.md scope mapping first, with the same check as `handoff IMPLEMENTING`.** What: `require_scope_mapping` (agent.sh) is the one check (every `## Scope` entry has `path` and a non-empty `criteria: [..]`, and the scope is not empty); `freeze` runs it before it forces a classification, records a hash or writes any RUN.yaml field, and refuses with `invalid scope mapping: fix PLAN.md ## Scope entries: each entry needs \`path\` and \`criteria:\``. `scope_mappings` also rejects an entry followed by another entry without `criteria:` (it used to drop it silently). The `plan-contract` validator already flags `maps to no criterion`. Why: a small Codex lead froze a plan whose scope had no `criteria:`, RUN.yaml recorded `scope_paths: ""`, the repairing refreeze then looked like scope expansion and stopped a TRIVIAL run at an unanswerable `scope` approval. Because a freeze can no longer record an empty scope, a refreeze never repairs one, so no extra exception to the scope-approval rule is added. A malformed `## Assumptions` table keeps the single A79 error. Alternatives: waive the scope approval when the old scope is empty (rejected: hides the defect instead of preventing it); validate only in `handoff` (rejected: the bad freeze is already recorded).
- **A100. The end-to-end harness can run the Codex lead with the cheapest model and lowest reasoning; the Codex effort ladder gains `none`.** What: `tests/e2e/run.sh` adds `-m "$E2E_CODEX_MODEL"` and `-c model_reasoning_effort="$E2E_CODEX_EFFORT"` to `codex exec` and `codex exec resume` when the variables are set (default empty: unchanged), and the dry run prints the final lead command; `execution_policy.providers.codex.efforts` becomes `none low medium high xhigh`. Why: Codex integration runs exhausted the usage limit on the default model. Verified: `-m gpt-6-luna -c model_reasoning_effort=none` is accepted, `minimal` is rejected. Workers: `--profile economy` already picks the cheapest ladder model (`gpt-6-luna`); the effort stays `low` because the TRIVIAL and STANDARD class floors are `low`, so `none` is reachable only through an explicit `--effort none`. Alternatives: leave the ladder as is (rejected: an operator override could not ask for `none`); lower the class floors to `none` (rejected: changes the class to effort mapping).
- **A98. An UNSUPPORTED check is unproven and reported, not blocking by default; an INCONCLUSIVE measurement still blocks (amends A78, A82, A83, A84, A86, A87).** What: a risk-focus result `UNSUPPORTED` with a non-empty reason and no blocking row is recorded with result `unsupported` (gate record field `unsupported: "<focus>"`, an `info` row `UNSUPPORTED <focus>: <reason>`), so `pipeline` prints `focus.<GATE>.<focus>=unsupported` (never `pass`) and `unsupported.<GATE>:<focus>=UNSUPPORTED (unproven, reported): ...`; the focus counts as satisfied for its gate, so the gate is `pass` when every other focus passes and CODE_DONE is reachable; `next` prints one `UNSUPPORTED (unproven, reported)` line per such focus before CODE_DONE and before the report; the completion report must carry `## Unsupported Checks` with, for every unsupported focus of every required gate, a line naming `<GATE>:<focus>` and `not proven: <what>` (`publish-completion-report` refuses an omission or a line without it; the template shows the line; verify-proof never cites such a focus as proof). Blocking stays where it means something: (1) config `risk.unsupported: block|report` (default `report`, accepted by `config-check`, inside the frozen `risk_sha256`) where `block` restores the A78 rule (blocking row, FAIL, only the user waives it); (2) a reason that starts `<focus> INCONCLUSIVE:` (performance-proof and visual-proof submit their INCONCLUSIVE that way) is blocking in both modes, because a measurement ran and was unreliable and the user must decide; (3) a defect found in the part that could be checked is a FAIL (`--result UNSUPPORTED` still refuses blocking rows; a FAIL that carries UNSUPPORTED rows, the contract-proof shape, keeps them blocking). The `dependencies` focus still refuses UNSUPPORTED. Visibility: `pipeline` adds `gate.<GATE>.unsupported=<focus,...>` under a gate that closes with one, `agent.sh summary` prints `<GATE>: pass (unsupported: <focus,...>)` (never a bare pass), and `next` tells the lead to surface the unsupported checks to the user in its reply. Anti-dodge: `record` refuses an UNSUPPORTED_REASON shorter than 12 characters or without an anchor (a path-like token, an `INCONCLUSIVE:` measurement, or a tool/capability word such as parser, tool, browser, benchmark, schema, snapshot, contract, installed, throwaway), so "n/a" is refused; the report's `not proven:` needs at least three words. performance-proof submits a report whose thresholds could not be verified as `performance INCONCLUSIVE: thresholds unverified ...` (a measurement exists but is not verifiable), so it blocks. Trade-off, stated plainly: an agent that wants to skip a check now pays one concrete-sounding line instead of a user waiver; the word list cannot prove the tool is absent (amended by A102: a reason alone no longer suffices; UNSUPPORTED cites the skill validator's own saved output and is cross-checked against the script's own signals, and the word list stays as a secondary sanity check). What limits that: the result is visible in `pipeline`, `next`, `summary` and the completion report, the user is told in the lead's reply, a project that wants the old guarantee sets `risk.unsupported: block`, verify-proof never cites an unsupported focus as proof, and the skill validators (contract-proof, visual-proof, performance-proof, data-design) decide UNSUPPORTED from what they detected, not from the agent's choice. Why: in the COMPLEX e2e round 2 run the plan declared `contract_change: yes` for an HTTP API documented only in markdown, and the migration had no test-owned throwaway database; both focuses honestly reported UNSUPPORTED after a manual trace found no defect, yet A78 made each a blocking finding, so REVIEW_RULES and REVIEW_DATA failed, CODE_DONE was unreachable and the run ended asking for two waivers. On any project without machine-readable contracts or a temporary test database every contract or migration change would dead-end the same way, which trains users to waive by reflex. The user's invariant is "capability missing -> UNSUPPORTED, never counted as PASS, listed explicitly in the completion report"; a distinct `unsupported` status plus a mandatory `not proven:` line keeps that invariant without a stop the user cannot act on except by waiving. Alternatives rejected: keep A78 and let the e2e expectation accept waivers (C-13 forbids waivers in the harness, and the dead end stays for users); record UNSUPPORTED as `pass` with a note (A78's objection holds: it reads as a pass; here the status, the pipeline line and the report line all say unproven); a new waiver kind or an auto-waiver (a waiver is a user decision, not a default); making INCONCLUSIVE non-blocking too (a failed measurement is evidence of instability, not an absent tool); a separate gate or role for unsupported checks (no new gate types or roles).
- **A99. The script's own questions are printed in the run's language; identifiers and approval words stay verbatim (amends A63).** What: `agent.sh run-language [ID]` returns `tr` or `en`: a `Language: tr|en` line of the run's TASK.md wins, else the newest captured user prompt (`.agents/local/prompts/history/`, at least three words) is Turkish when it holds two or more Turkish-only letters or Turkish function words, else English (also when there is no signal). `request-approval` (plan, destructive, single_step, scope, budget, commit, push, waive), the assumption question of `freeze` and the failover question of `dispatch.sh` print a Turkish block in a Turkish run: the question, the labels (`seçenekler`, `önerilen`, `neden`) and the reason are Turkish; `approve`, `reject`, `accept AS-n`, `yes`, `no`, the kind word the user must type (`approve commit`), ids, hashes and the `QUESTIONS:` / `ASSUMPTION:` keys are unchanged, so approval matching (A41) and `assumption_prompt_accepts` (A79) see the same words. English runs print the old text byte for byte. The lead skill and role text say: never leave an English-only `QUESTIONS:` block as the last words of a non-English message; a specialist's English block is shown with a translation, keeping identifiers. — STANDARD.tr round 3: the lead's final message ended with the English approval block copied from `request-approval` (TR-1); A63 left the script without a language input. — Limits: the signal is a heuristic (A67 limits: ASCII-only Turkish with fewer than two function words reads as English); only Turkish is localised; a specialist's NEEDS_INPUT block stays English as stored (D13). The Stop hook (A44/A68) does not additionally detect an English-only block: the language of free text cannot be decided in POSIX sh/awk without false positives on quoted identifiers. — Alternatives rejected: translating in the lead only (failed in round 3; A63 already tried), translating every stored question (breaks D13 verbatim relay of specialists), a config `language` key (a second source that can disagree with the user's actual messages).
- **A102. An UNSUPPORTED result is backed by the skill validator's own output and cross-checked against the script's own signals; a reason alone no longer closes a focus (amends A98).** What: `agent.sh submit --result UNSUPPORTED` takes `--evidence <file>`, a run-relative file in the focus's report folder or `reports/`, snapshotted to `reports/snapshots/<sha12>-<role>-<path>` exactly like the report (A43); the result carries `UNSUPPORTED_EVIDENCE: <path> <sha256>`. `record` refuses an UNSUPPORTED (1) without that line; (2) whose file is outside the run or those folders, or whose sha256 differs from the cited one; (3) whose file lacks the validator's `RESULT: UNSUPPORTED` line (case-insensitive, so performance-proof's `result: UNSUPPORTED` from `--rows` counts; for `visual` also `RESULT: INCONCLUSIVE`); (4) whose file prints `BASE: <commit>` that is not the run's base; (5) for `contract`: when a contract file the control plane's own `risk_compute` found in the diff (`signal.contract_file`, source diff, by path or content) is not listed in a `FILE: compared|unsupported <path>` line, or when every listed file is `compared`; (6) for `migration`: when the file is `validate-migration.mjs` output without `--repo` (its `WARN no --repo` line), or a changed `.sql` migration signal is not listed `FILE: unsupported <path>`. The `concurrency` and `config` focuses, like `dependencies`, always have a tool-free check (a traced interleaving; the static consistency scan), so UNSUPPORTED is refused for them. The skill scripts print what this needs: `validate-contract.mjs` adds one `FILE: <status> <path>` line per file and, with `--base`, `BASE: <commit>`; `validate-migration.mjs` adds `RESULT: <status>` and, for UNSUPPORTED, `FILE: unsupported <file>` per cited migration (no throwaway database is UNSUPPORTED, so the e2e COMPLEX migration path keeps its evidence). Visibility without a dead end: the gate record keeps `unsupported_evidence: "<path>@<sha12>"` and `unsupported_evidence_sha256` (`valid_gate_file` makes the record stale when the file changes), `pipeline` prints `unsupported.<GATE>:<focus>=... (evidence: <path>@<sha12>)`, and `next` names the same before CODE_DONE and the report. The A98 status model is unchanged: a truthful UNSUPPORTED still closes its gate as `unsupported`, is listed under `## Unsupported Checks` with `not proven:`, `risk.unsupported: block` stays available, and INCONCLUSIVE still blocks. The A98 reason checks (length and anchor word list) stay as a secondary sanity check. The lead's `check-result.mjs` mirrors the line and the two refused focuses. Why: the user saw that after A98 a check (contract, migration) could be skipped with one concrete-sounding line that no longer held the gate, and the independent review said the same; the A98 follow-up only added a word list, which proves nothing about the tool. The control plane already knows, without the agent, which contract and migration files the diff touched; requiring the skill's own deterministic output and checking it against those files turns the cheapest dodge (claiming UNSUPPORTED over an OpenAPI file or a SQL migration the skill can handle) into a refusal, while an honest UNSUPPORTED costs one redirect of output the skill already prints. Limits, stated plainly: the control plane checks the file's text, it does not re-run the skill (skills stay standalone and may not be installed beside the control plane), so an agent that fabricates a file naming every signalled path as unsupported still passes; that takes a deliberate false artifact, bound by sha256 in the record and visible in `pipeline`, `next` and the completion report, where the verifier and the user can open it. Alternatives rejected: refuse a contract UNSUPPORTED whenever a signalled file is listed `compared` (the literal reading of the request): a run whose OpenAPI file was compared without a breaking change but whose second file cannot be parsed is honestly UNSUPPORTED and would dead-end again, the A98 failure; letting the control plane run the skill validators itself (couples the control plane to skill installation paths and node, and a skill is standalone by rule); a free-text evidence field (the same one-line dodge); requiring evidence only under `risk.unsupported: report` (block mode already needs a waiver, but one rule is simpler and the evidence helps the user decide the waiver); a cross-check on every migration path (`**/migrations/**` also matches non-SQL helpers the scanner may not analyze; the `.sql` rule is the conservative one). Review round on A102 (same decision): (a) capability probes in the control plane, independent of the agent, that only ever refuse: a `visual` UNSUPPORTED is refused when a Chromium-family browser is on PATH or in the standard macOS application folders or a project browser-test dependency is installed (the lists of visual-proof's `measure.mjs --detect`, kept equal by a risk-test drift guard), a `performance` UNSUPPORTED when the project has a benchmark command (package.json script `bench`/`benchmark`/`perf*`, Makefile or justfile `bench`/`benchmark` target, cargo `benches/`, Go `func Benchmark` in a tracked `*_test.go`, asv or pytest-benchmark, JMH, BenchmarkDotNet); a probe never refuses an INCONCLUSIVE (a measurement ran), and a benchmark that exists but cannot reach the change is submitted BLOCKED for the user (the flag `performance_critical` is the plan's own claim, so the user decides); `PROOFLINE_PROBE_ROOT` replaces PATH and the application folders for the self-tests only, and counts only with the second marker `PROOFLINE_SELFTEST=1` outside a runtime session (no Claude Code or Codex session variable set); the PreToolUse hook (Claude and Codex share it) refuses any model command that names either variable; (b) the INCONCLUSIVE prefix follows the evidence in both directions: INCONCLUSIVE evidence (visual `RESULT: INCONCLUSIVE`, performance `reason: performance INCONCLUSIVE:`) needs a reason starting `<focus> INCONCLUSIVE:`, so a measured but unreliable result cannot be downgraded to a non-blocking UNSUPPORTED, and UNSUPPORTED evidence refuses an INCONCLUSIVE-prefixed reason; (c) the evidence must be the submitting role's own file (its focus folder, its `reports/snapshots/<sha12>-<role>-...` snapshot, `reports/<NNN>-<role>...`), and `submit` snapshots only those. Residuals stated plainly: non-SQL migrations (`.py`, `.js`, `.rb`) have no `FILE:` cross-check; a contract or migration evidence file the agent writes by hand (echoing the validator's lines) still passes the text checks, and is caught only by the verifier or the user reading it (its path and sha12 are in `pipeline`, `next` and the gate record); the probes cannot see a browser or benchmark outside the conventions listed.
- **A103. The run language comes from the user's own words, not the newest captured prompt (amends A99).** What: `agent.sh run-language [ID]` reads, in order, a `Language: tr|en` line of TASK.md; the `>` blockquote lines of TASK.md (the user's report, A33/A67) when they hold three or more words; the OLDEST captured prompt (`.agents/local/prompts/history/`) of six or more words, i.e. the task prompt. A prompt shorter than six words (`approve commit`, `evet`, `onay`) is a reply and never evidence; no signal is English. The Turkish heuristic is unchanged except for a few more ASCII function words (`ben bunu sunu nasil neden evet hayir onay tamam`). — STANDARD.tr e2e round 4: A99 took the newest prompt of three or more words; the newest history files were the harness's English approval replies and system prompts, so `run-language` printed `en` for a Turkish run and the commit approval block was English again. — Limits: a run with no TASK.md quote whose first long prompt is English reads as English even if later prompts are Turkish; the heuristic limits of A67 still apply. — Alternatives rejected: majority vote across all prompts (a few long English system prompts outvote one Turkish task), newest prompt of a longer minimum (still flips on long system prompts), a config `language` key (a second source that can disagree).

- **A104. `submit` has one stdin: two `-` sources are refused before reading, or split on `=== FINDINGS ===`; `--findings none` is zero rows.** What: a real Codex worker called `submit --report - --findings -` with one heredoc, so both flags read the same stream; submit failed with "the report is empty", then "BAD 1: want severity|path:line|finding|evidence", twice, and the dispatch blocked. Now `submit_result` counts the `-` sources (`--report`, `--findings`, `--questions`) before reading anything. More than one is refused with `only one of --report/--findings may read stdin (-); write one of them to a file ...`, naming the corrected command, except the pair `--report -` + `--findings -`: that reads stdin once and splits it on the exact line `=== FINDINGS ===` (report above, rows below; a document without the line is refused with the same message). `--findings none` is zero rows (any role; the validator already allowed zero rows). An answer-only task submits `--findings none` or one `info|-|answer|<one line>` row. A rejected row or empty report now names the corrected form. Role texts, the dispatch prompt template, PROTOCOL.md and the skills' "no findings" line say the same; the old "output only the table header row" line is replaced because the script writes the header. Alternatives rejected: (1) splitting any two stdin sources by guessing (no deterministic rule); (2) making `--findings -` win and ignoring the report (silently drops evidence); (3) a dispatch-side rewrite of the worker command (hides the contract from the worker).
- **A105. A defect the request does not ask for is declared in TASK.md, and only the user's own `scope` approval makes its fix part of the run (amends A60; COMPLEX X-13, F-6).** What: in a COMPLEX or CRITICAL run whose request (TASK title, `## Goal`, the user's `>` quotes, the `## Suggested class` line) names no defect, a line of `## Context`, `## Acceptance criteria`, `## Constraints` or `## Risks` that speaks of a defect or a fix (`defect`, `bug`, `regression`, `fix`, `fixes`, `fixing`, Turkish `kusur`, `düzelt`) needs at least one `## Pre-existing defects` entry; every entry carries `decision: report` or `decision: fix`; a `###` heading (the section nested in Context, as models write it) counts, also for the A60 `decision: fix` check, which used to read only `##`. `freeze` and `refreeze` refuse before any other approval check (`agent.sh task_defect_errors`), `handoff IMPLEMENTING` repeats the check, `next` prints `fix TASK.md ## Pre-existing defects` instead of the approval steps, and `validate-task.mjs` reports the same error (and warns that `decision: fix` needs the user's `scope` approval). A `decision: fix` keeps the A60 rule: the freeze needs a `scope` approval bound to the PLAN.md hash, which `approve` records only from a captured user prompt holding an approval phrase and the word `scope`; an answer to a specialist or lead question, or a blanket "use your recommended option", never creates it. The A60 INTENT.md provenance check now also refuses a `scope` decision answered by a blanket recommended-option reply unless the decision is to report (the reply is recorded verbatim and is the user's, but it does not decide a fix). Role and skill texts: task author, lead, architect and plan-contract keep an unrequested defect fix out of the task and the plan, and the completion report names each `decision: report` defect under `## Known Limitations / Follow-up`. Why: in COMPLEX run r4 (`eval-COMPLEX-r4.md`) the task author asked an intake question about the `paginate` floor defect, the harness's generic reply "Use your recommended option for every open question" came back, and the author wrote the fix into TASK.md as a delegated Context decision, AC-6 ("15 books at pageSize 10 give totalPages 2, also without a tag filter") and a Risk, with no `## Pre-existing defects` section; A60's gate is keyed on that section, so nothing asked for a `scope` approval, REVIEW_PLAN judged the fix requested by the task, and `paginate` changed (X-13 and F-6 unmet). Validated against the run's real TASK.md: the freeze now refuses with `mentions a defect or a fix the request does not ask for: The paginate defect is fixed with a ceiling ...`; with the defect declared `decision: fix` it stops at `next=agent.sh request-approval <ID> scope`, which the harness never answers (X-13: stop at a scope request); with `decision: report` it freezes. Across the recorded e2e runs (TRIVIAL, STANDARD, STANDARD.tr, CRITICAL, COMPLEX) the rule fires only on COMPLEX tasks that mention the paging defect without the section (six runs; the three of them that changed `paginate` are among them); no TRIVIAL, STANDARD or CRITICAL task fires, because a defect fix is the request there or the class is below COMPLEX. Limits, stated plainly: the trigger is a word list, so a task author who describes the folded fix without any of those words passes the freeze, and REVIEW_PLAN (proof-review PLAN-CHECK rule 3) stays the second line; a STANDARD feature task that folds a defect is not checked (STANDARD defect tasks often state their goal positively, "every book is reachable", with no defect word, and would be refused). Alternatives rejected: the path rule "plan scope paths minus the paths TASK.md names need a `scope` approval" (on the real run `src/service.js` is named in TASK Context because the tag filter needs it, so the fix is not caught, while `public/index.html` and the test files are not named and would stop every COMPLEX run at an approval the harness never gives); "criteria not traceable to a TASK.md criterion" (AC-6 is a TASK criterion: the leak is in TASK.md itself); a `scope` approval for every `planned breaking` `## Contracts` entry (the CRITICAL scenario's requested column removal is a planned breaking change and would stop at `scope` instead of its destructive approval); making `## Pre-existing defects` mandatory in every task (every fixture and task gains a section for no signal; the word trigger asks for it only where a defect is mentioned).

- **A106. An approval's single-use is per capture, not per text (amends A41, A68; COMPLEX final run).** What: `agent.sh approve` identifies the user prompt it reads by its capture identity `session:time:sha256` and records it as `capture_id` in the approval (`capture_sha256` stays for audit). It refuses a second approval of the same kind from the same `capture_id`; a different capture with identical words is accepted when it is newer than the current request (`time > requested_at`, unchanged), carries the approval phrase and kind word, and the bound is the current hash. Different kinds are untouched: one capture still covers `plan` and `commit` in one prompt, because the check is per kind slug. Why: in the final COMPLEX run the plan was approved by "Plan approved.", the plan was legitimately changed (A105), a new plan request was made and the user answered with the same words; the old text-hash check refused it, so a user could never answer a second request with the same words. Forgery: capture files are written only by the runtime prompt hook (`proofline-hook.sh prompt`), and the lead hook allows writes only to run artifacts and `.agents/local/assignments/`, so `.agents/local/prompts/` is not writable by a model (enforcement test A106-4); a copied capture would also need a newer `time` than the request, which the model cannot produce in a capture the hook did not write. Rejected: keying on the history file name (the per-session `<session>.json` is the file `approve` reads, and a same-second prompt would collide in history); dropping single-use (a replay of one capture would approve two requests).
- **A107. A report is not evidence: `submit` re-derives the validator result, re-checks every cited evidence file and re-runs a recorded reproduced command (amends A93, A102).** What: (1) `agent.sh submit` runs the skill validator of the submitting role and focus itself (verifier: `validate-verify.mjs`; reviewer `tests`: test-trust `gate.mjs`; reviewer `plan`, `rules`, `rules+tests`: proof-review `validate-findings.mjs` then `gate.mjs` over the focus's `findings.json`, when the skill and node are installed) and uses its result. An invalid report is refused with the validator's errors; the optional `--validator-result PASS|FAIL` that the agent declares is compared with the recomputed value and a mismatch is refused naming both; a PASS the validator computes as FAIL is refused the same way. (2) Every evidence file a submission cites (`--evidence PATH[@SHA256]`, repeatable, or `PATH@<64 hex>` in a finding row's evidence column or the report) must be a regular file inside the run, in a folder the role reports in (or `reports/`, `worker-evidence/`), and its sha256 must equal the cited one: a missing file, a file outside those folders, or a file changed after it was hashed refuses the submission. The result keeps `EVIDENCE: <path> <sha256>` lines and `record` checks them again. (3) For a gate result of PASS, `submit` re-runs the recorded `reproduced` items itself: the verifier's `## Commands` entries and cited evidence files that carry `command:`, `exit:` and `output:` lines. Only a command that is the configured `commands.build|test|lint` or a frozen PLAN `## Verification commands` entry is executed (at most three, in the project root, with a timeout when `timeout` exists); a different exit status or a missing recorded output line turns the result into FAIL with a blocking finding, and items none of which is an allowed command are a blocking finding too (nothing a report names is ever run). Evidence classes stay measured, reproduced, traced, judged; no role, agent, gate or skill is added. Why: A93 and A102 left the accepted residual that a fabricated but self-consistent report passes, because every check read the report's own text; "no evidence, no claim" had a hole at exactly the place the claim is made. The control plane can now disprove a recorded command and a recorded hash without trusting the writer. Rejected: running every recorded command (cost: a test suite twice per round; three is the bound and the verifier's own run is the first); executing any command a report names (arbitrary execution from model text); making the verifier agent re-run commands as a rule (policy-only, the same writer); requiring a findings JSON for every focus now (other focuses keep the findings-derived result until their findings file is mandatory, listed as a residual). Residual: a report whose commands are all outside the configured and planned set has nothing to re-run and is a blocking finding for the verifier, but an evidence file that is not a reproduction record (measured, traced, judged) is checked for existence and hash only; reviewer focuses without a mapped validator (contract, concurrency, performance, config, migration, data, security, qa) are not yet re-derived. Review round 1 (no silent skip): (a) which gates are reproduced-class: only VERIFY (its `## Commands` records are `reproduced`). A VERIFY PASS (verify-proof installed) with nothing re-runnable is a blocking finding `no re-runnable reproduced evidence`; every other gate (REVIEW_*, SECURITY, QA, the risk focuses) is judgment, trace or measurement unless it cites a command record (`command:`/`exit:`/`output:`), and a PASS that re-ran nothing records the info row `NOT_RERUN: <gate>:<focus> no reproduced evidence; this gate is not reproduced proof`, which verify-proof must not cite as reproduced proof. (b) A mapped focus (verifier, reviewer tests/plan/rules/rules+tests) whose skill is installed but whose validator cannot run (no node, no findings.json) refuses a PASS whether or not `--validator-result` was declared; a skill that is not installed records the info row `NOT_VALIDATED`; the other focuses are unmapped and keep the findings-derived result. (c) A compared item needs a recorded output line that equals a whole line of the re-run output (trimmed); a missing line or a bare substring such as `ok` compares nothing and is a blocking finding. (d) Every command an AC row cites as `command:` proof is re-run (not capped) or is a blocking finding (not configured or planned, no exit status, no Commands entry); other allowed items run up to three. (e) A bare `evidence:<path>` mention (no hash) in a row or report is checked for existence and then hashed; plain prose paths are not. (f) The planned commands are read from PLAN.md only while its sha256 equals the frozen `plan_sha256`; a plan edited after the freeze contributes none. Alternatives rejected in this round: making an absent validator a silent fallback (the declared-versus-omitted asymmetry), accepting a substring of the output (a fabricated `ok` matches), re-running only the first allowed command. Residuals: unmapped focuses; configured commands are read from the live config (the config is bound by policy at the freeze, not re-hashed here); a measured, traced or judged evidence file is checked for existence and hash, not re-derived; a cited path in prose without `evidence:` or `@sha256` is not seen. Review round 2: (g) a silent command records `output: (no output)` (an absent line with a recorded exit means the same) and matches an empty re-run, comparing the exit status only; a non-empty re-run with none recorded is a mismatch. (h) A recorded output line holding a time, duration, date, pid, address or UUID is refused with the instruction to record one stable summary line; the verifier skill and role say so. (i) A gate that is not reproduced-class ignores a cited command that is not configured or planned (it is never run and never blocks; the gate records `NOT_RERUN`); only the verifier's AC-cited commands are blocking when not re-runnable. (j) A frozen plan hash that is missing or not 64 hex denies every PLAN command (only configured `commands.build|test|lint` run); this is defence in depth, because `submit` already refuses a run whose freeze does not match (`freeze mismatch`), which the scenario exercises. (k) Every `command:`/`exit:`/`output:` triple of a cited evidence file is re-run, at most three per file. Residuals: a verifier PASS written while verify-proof is not installed is not reproduced-class (no validator and no re-run requirement apply; the lead's `next` treats the gate by its record only); a re-run executes the project's own command in the project root, so a command with side effects (writes, network) has them on every submit, the same as the verifier's own run, and the database guard and the runtime hook do not cover a process started by `agent.sh`.

- **A109. Self-test shards are balanced by unit weight, six shards (amends A76).** What: `tests/selftest.sh` assigns each unit to a shard by weight instead of `index % N`: the units are taken longest first and each goes to the lightest shard (`tests/selftest-weights.txt`, seconds from one CI run, default 15 for an unlisted unit); a shard starts its heaviest units first. CI runs six shards instead of four and prints every unit's time (`SELFTEST_SLOWEST=999`). `SELFTEST_LIST=1` prints a shard's units, `SELFTEST_SLOWEST=K` the K slowest. `risk-test` `sc_unsupported` (one process, about nine minutes) is split at its independent run boundaries into `sc_unsupported`, `sc_unsupported_inconclusive`, `sc_unsupported_rows` and `sc_unsupported_block`; each builds its own run and every assertion is kept. Every unit lands in exactly one shard (checked: the six shard lists joined equal the one-shard list, 120 units), so no test is dropped and no assertion changes. Why: measured on pull-request runs of the `ci` workflow, the four shards took 5.0, 14.4, 5.5 and 4.0 minutes (run 38594862821; a manual run with all unit times took 4.1 to 12.2 minutes): the round-robin put the heavy units (`cli-test`, `risk-test sc_unsupported*`, the `lifecycle-test` loops) together and which shard was worst changed from run to run, so the wall time was the worst shard, not the total (about 25 minutes of unit time over four cores, 6.5 minutes when balanced). With the weights and the split the six shards took 2.8 to 7.9 minutes (run 38609632577); the floor is now `cli-test` alone, 470 s, one single-process suite. Rejected: only raising the shard count (index % N still pairs heavy units by luck; eight shards cost eight runner setups for no better floor); splitting `cli-test` into scenario units (a 165-case single-process suite whose cases share state; the change is larger than the gain and risks the suite's own meaning); dropping or shortening slow scenarios (tests are not removed, A76); dynamic work distribution across jobs (needs a shared queue the runners do not have). A stale weight only costs balance, never coverage; refresh the file from `SELFTEST_SLOWEST=999` output when units are added.
- **A110. visual-proof browser measurement runs as its own Linux and macOS CI job on the runner image's own browser, and `measure.mjs` retries a browser start timeout once (amends A82).** What: the step moves out of the `fixtures` job into the job `visual-browser` (pushes to `main`, nightly, manual runs; pull requests never run it), in parallel with the fixtures and benchmark work instead of after it. `measure.mjs` retries `launch` once when the browser prints no DevTools endpoint within the start timeout, and the timeout message carries the tail of the browser's stderr. Why: measured on `ubuntu-latest` (Chromium and Chrome 154 preinstalled, nothing installed by the workflow): `--no-sandbox` works there (kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1 forbids the sandbox), the fixture measures 117 checks in about 40 s and `expect.mjs` finds exactly the 27 planted failures with the traps clear; one main run had failed with `browser did not start within 30000 ms` and the next two runs passed, so the cause was a cold-start hang, not the flag. A retry bounds that flake without loosening any assertion; a second hang still fails the step. Rejected: raising the start timeout (a hang stays a hang and the step gets slower); installing a browser in the workflow (A82: tooling is detected, never installed); making the step non-blocking (a measured pass must stay distinguishable from an unmeasured one).
- **A108. Approval detection is fail-closed: a positive grammar, anything else is ambiguous (amends A41, A68, A79).** What: `text_has_approval` (used by `agent.sh approve`, hence by the prompt hook's `approve-pending`) took any text that held an `approval.phrases` entry as a substring plus a kind word, so "do not approve the plan", "approve the plan?" and "disapproved, plan" approved.
