Now the reply is normalised (ASCII case folding under `LC_ALL=C`, ASCII punctuation removed, apostrophes dropped, other bytes unchanged) and approves only when EVERY word is (a) a word of an allowlisted approval phrase (default list: `approve, approved, yes, ok, okay, go ahead, lgtm, commit it, push it, onay, onaylıyorum, onayliyorum, onaylandı, onaylandi, onayladım, onayladim, evet, tamam, olur`; `approval.phrases` still overrides), (b) a word of the kind's own words (`kind_words`: the kind word, `destructive migration delete drop`, `single step`, `budget round rounds`, the finding id), or (c) a filler (`the, a, an, it, this, please, for, with, to, and, lütfen, lutfen, bu, planı, plani, için, icin`); it also holds a whole allowlisted phrase and a kind word (unchanged requirement; hash binding and A106 per-capture single-use unchanged) and no question mark. Any other word, a negation, a hesitation, an extra clause or anything unrelated, makes the reply ambiguous: no approval and no rejection, the request stays pending, and `next` prints "the user's last reply is not an explicit approval ... relay the question again" (`approval_wait_o`) until the lead asks again with `request-approval`. Every approval question lists the accepted phrases and the filler words next to the `approve <kind>` instruction (Turkish block in a Turkish run, A99; English otherwise). Why: the absence of a rejection word is not consent; an approval is a security decision (D3, D13) and a wrong yes writes an approval the user never gave. A longer legitimate reply is re-asked; that is the accepted cost of failing closed. Tests: `scenario_approval_fail_closed` in `lifecycle-test.sh` (ambiguous, empty, unrelated, negated and hesitant answers leave the request pending, under the C and a UTF-8 locale; every allowlisted TR and EN phrase approves; the question lists the answers); existing approval texts of the suites were shortened to the grammar. Rejected: a hold-word denylist on top of the substring match (first form of this decision: it leaked on any negation it did not list: "I shouldn't approve", "I cannot approve", "yes, delete nothing", "onay vermiyorum", "onay yok", "onaylamıyorum", "just kidding"; a denylist cannot be complete in two languages with suffixes); requiring the whole message to be exactly one phrase (a kind word and a polite filler are normal); fuzzy or model-judged intent (not deterministic); changing the A79 `accept <id>` check (already a start-of-prompt plus no-negation rule, unchanged).
- **A112. A non-TRIVIAL plan with a `## UI` section declares `visual_writes:` at plan time (amends A78, A81, A82, A98).** What: `validate-plan.mjs` reports a missing `visual_writes: <how the data the page writes is thrown away>` line as an error when the class is not TRIVIAL (STANDARD, COMPLEX, CRITICAL: the classes whose pipeline has a QA gate, so QA's visual focus runs for a UI plan) and the plan has a `## UI` section (the same line shape `validate-visual.mjs` reads as the same-origin-write allowance), and `agent.sh freeze` refuses the same plan (only when gates are enforced) with a message naming the line to add. The designer role, the plan-contract sections reference and the PLAN templates say to write it whenever QA visual will run; `visual_writes: none, <why>` is accepted when the page sends no same-origin writes. TRIVIAL (no QA gate, A96) is unchanged. The line patterns of `agent.sh`, `validate-plan.mjs` and `validate-visual.mjs` are identical in shape and a test asserts they accept and refuse the same sample lines. Why: an end-to-end COMPLEX run froze a plan without the line, QA visual ended INCONCLUSIVE, the fix needed a plan amendment and a second plan approval, the fourth reply exceeded the three-reply cap and the run stopped at IMPLEMENTED; the need is knowable when the plan is written. Alternatives rejected: keying it on COMPLEX/CRITICAL only (STANDARD has qa: yes, so a STANDARD UI plan hit the same INCONCLUSIVE and amendment); leaving it to QA INCONCLUSIVE and an amendment (the defect itself: it costs a second approval); raising the reply cap (hides the miss, loosens a harness limit for a plan defect); auto-approving amendments (the user's approval of what QA may write is the point of A41); deriving a default (the line is a free-text allowance of how data is discarded, not a path, so no safe default exists and inventing one would widen what QA may write).
- **A114. A headless Claude worker is started with an explicit permission mode (amends A46, A56).** What: `dispatch.sh` passes `--permission-mode <mode>` to every Claude worker it starts, from config `dispatch.claude_permission_mode` (`bypassPermissions` default, `acceptEdits`, `default`; `config-check` refuses any other value). With `bypassPermissions`, dispatch first verifies that `.agents/hooks/proofline-hook.sh` exists and `.claude/settings.json` wires a `PreToolUse` hook; otherwise the step is BLOCKED with a message naming `proofline doctor`, and no unconfined worker is run. The mode is ledgered (`dispatch claude_permission_mode=...`) and printed in the dispatch line. The Codex branch is unchanged. Why: a real mixed run (Codex lead, Claude reviewer and verifier, and failover) ended BLOCKED at IMPLEMENTED: a headless `claude -p` in default mode cannot approve `agent.sh submit`, `fingerprint`, test commands or file writes, so no dispatched Claude worker could record a result; all-Claude runs only worked because native Task subagents inherit the lead's permissions. A worker cannot be prompted, and the confinement is the PreToolUse hook (role write paths, scratch guard, stash guard, destructive-SQL guard, no `agent.sh approve`), which runs in every permission mode. Rejected: inheriting the lead's mode (not detectable from a headless child); `--allowedTools` lists (arbitrary test and build commands cannot be enumerated); dispatching Claude workers only through Task (breaks a Codex lead and failover); an unconditional bypass without the hook check (an unconfined worker). Dependencies and residuals: WebFetch, WebSearch, MCP and Task tools are unreachable for the worker only because its `--agents` tools list (generated agent or `tools.conf`) omits them; the hook check is the script present, non-empty and parsing plus a `PreToolUse` entry for it in `.claude/settings.json` (settings.local.json-only or plugin-provided wiring is refused, fail closed); the worker still inherits user-level Claude settings (possible follow-up: `--setting-sources project,local`).
- **A111. Every `git stash` subcommand is denied in the project's working tree for a specialist; evidence worktrees stay free (amends A71).** A STANDARD.tr end-to-end run failed C-3 on one item: the QA subagent ran `git stash list` in the project tree. The command is read-only but is a `git stash` invocation, and the A71 hook let it through. What the hook does now (`adapters/claude/hooks/proofline-hook.sh`, `stash_guard`): for a specialist during an active run it reads the scanned commands (nested `sh -c`, pipelines, `;`/`&&` lists, env and `cd` prefixes included), skips git's global options (`-C <path>`, `--work-tree`, `-c`, `--no-pager` and other flags) and denies `stash` with any subcommand (bare, list, show, push, pop, apply, drop, clear, create, store, branch) unless the effective directory (hook cwd moved by `cd` and `git -C`) lies inside a registered worktree other than the project; the denial names the evidence worktree as the allowed alternative. `git stash` text as an argument of another command (`grep`, `echo`) is not a git invocation and stays allowed. Fail-closed on what the text cannot say: the hook does not expand shell variables, so a `-C`/`cd` path is resolved only when it is a literal path, or starts with `$TMPDIR` or `$HOME` (expanded to a literal and resolved physically, symlinks and `..` included). Any other variable, a command substitution or backticks, `cd -`, `$OLDPWD`, `pushd`/`popd`, `env -C`/`sudo -D`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, or a `cd` that sits in a subshell, pipe or background job before the stash is read as the project, so the stash is denied (a wrong denial costs one retry with a literal path). A first word after the options that is not a plausible subcommand name (a value of an option the hook does not know) followed by the word `stash` is read as a stash. Known value-taking global options are skipped (`-C`, `-c`, `--git-dir`, `--work-tree`, `--namespace`, `--super-prefix`, `--exec-path`, `--config-env`, `--attr-source`; `--list-cmds` and `--exec` are skipped with their value too). A directory that does not exist when the hook runs is unresolved (a failed `cd`, or a link made later in the same command), so it is read as the project; an evidence worktree is therefore created in one call and used in the next. Command substitution: the scanner splits an unquoted `$(...)` or backtick pair into a nested command, so the records alone do not see `git -C $(pwd) stash list`; a text safety net covers it. Every `git [known options] stash` (or a `$`/backtick where the subcommand goes, `git $S list`, `git stash${IFS}list`) in command position that no record resolved to an evidence worktree is denied, including behind wrappers (`find -exec`, `watch`, `parallel`, `ssh`, `setsid`, `chronic`, `unbuffer`, `script -c`, `su -c`, a function body). The net skips a match whose command word only prints or searches (`echo`, `printf`, `grep`, `rg`, `cat`, `sed`, `awk`, `head`, `tail`, ...), and a word after `git` that is not an option (`git log stash`, `git diff main..stash`, `git branch stash`, `git commit -m stash`, `git grep stash`, `git config stash.x`) is never a stash. Known limits, not closed: a function or alias that wraps `git stash` defined in an earlier call or an obfuscated subcommand (`git st""ash`, a built string run by `eval` is already refused by A34) is not seen, and a printing word followed by a real stash in the same segment is skipped. Other cases the text does not see (reviewer's LOW findings, accepted): a quoted substitution or variable in subcommand position (`git "$S" list`, `git "$(echo stash)" list`), `echo stash | xargs git`, `` `which git` stash ``, `git stash\ list`, and a stash run by another program (a Python `subprocess` list form). `GIT_WORK_TREE=`, `GIT_DIR=`, `--work-tree` and `-c core.worktree=` anywhere in the same git invocation make the target the project, so the stash is denied. Fail-closed false positives, accepted (retry with a literal `git -C <worktree> stash` in its own call): a `git stash` inside the message of a command that is not a printing word (`git commit -m "... git stash ..."`), `(cd <worktree> && git stash)` in a subshell, and `git --git-dir=<worktree>/.git --work-tree=<worktree> stash`. `git checkout --` and the other mutating subcommands keep the A71 rule unchanged. — Rationale (recorded by the author): C-3 says the command is not invoked in the project tree, and a subagent cannot be expected to know that `list` is the safe subset the rule never named; the hook is the deterministic place. — Alternatives rejected by the author: loosening EXPECTED C-3 to allow read-only subcommands (moves the target to hide a product gap and leaves `show` and `stash@{n}` forms ambiguous); accepting it as model behaviour (the hook exists to make this deterministic, and the run was scored FAIL for it).
- **A113. `submit` allocates an explorer's report name (amends A43, X-4/M-2).** Parallel explorers each wrote `reports/001-explorer.md`, overwrote each other, and one was resubmitted: three explorer results where COMPLEX allows two. Now the explorer writes its report anywhere in scratch and passes it with `submit --report <file>|-` (any `--artifact` is ignored; BLOCKED without `--report` stores the reason as the report). `submit` validates the content first, then picks the next free `reports/NNN-explorer.md` with an atomic noclobber create (parallel submits cannot get the same name, capped at 1000 attempts), copies the content there and cites a snapshot of it in the result and ledger row; a submit refused after the allocation (language scan, validator, record) removes the file and its owner entry again. The allocation is remembered per dispatch token under `reports/.owners/`: the same token updates its own report, a re-dispatch under a new token gets a new name, and a tokenless submit succeeds and gets a new name every time (it cannot update one). An explorer that names a report path itself (`--artifact reports/001-explorer.md`, `reports/./001-explorer.md`, `reports//...`: paths are canonicalised in `normalize_artifact` before every check) is refused with the corrected command, and `record` accepts an explorer result only when it cites a `reports/snapshots/` copy made by `submit`, so `record --file` cannot cite another dispatch's live report. The hook's owns.conf still lets an explorer Write `reports/NNN-explorer.md`; only `submit` decides what is accepted. `dispatch.sh` also claims its sequence number with an atomic `mkdir`, so parallel dispatches no longer share a token, log or submission status. Rejected: serialising explorers (loses the parallelism `max_explorers` exists for and still leaves name choice to the model); letting the lead rename files afterwards (the overwrite has already destroyed one report and its result cites it); lead-assigned names via a reservation command plus a `REPORT:` line (first draft: nothing forces the lead or the explorer to obey, and it left stubs for roles that never needed one); per-token owner files over a name the explorer chooses (first-submitter-wins is defeated by a direct Write before submit). Known limit: a result may still cite an existing snapshot of another explorer's report through `record --file` (snapshots are immutable and content-addressed, so it cannot be altered, only reused); the lead's EVIDENCE.md consolidation reads each result's own header.
- **A115. A TRIVIAL freeze refuses an executable-code scope (amends A31, A61).** A real run had the lead author TASK.md with Suggested class TRIVIAL, classify TRIVIAL and freeze a plan scoping `src/service.js` and `tests/service.test.js` (two paths, inside the TRIVIAL bound). Nothing at freeze judged code against text; A31 fired only at IMPLEMENTED, so the lead reclassified STANDARD and refroze after the code existed: C-4 (a transition recorded twice) and S-6 (QA_PLAN before IMPLEMENT) were broken. Now `pipeline_check` (freeze and refreeze, the only ways to a frozen plan; `handoff IMPLEMENTING` verifies the plan hash, so a code scope cannot be added later without a refreeze) fails a TRIVIAL plan whose `## Scope` paths match the glob list A31 uses (`review.executable_code`, one shared helper `paths_executable`): "the TRIVIAL pipeline covers text and configuration only; scope has executable code: <paths> — classify STANDARD". Test files are code by that list; `.html`, `.css`, `.md`, `.json`, `.txt` are not, so a copy fix in `public/index.html` stays TRIVIAL. A31 stays as the check of the real diff at IMPLEMENTED and CODE_DONE; it now fires only when `review.executable_code` is wider at the diff than at freeze. `next` and the lead lifecycle name the rule. Rejected: forcing the class from the lead-authored Suggested class (the lead authors it, so it only repeats the claim); keeping only the late A31 check plus a refreeze (violates C-4 and S-6 by construction); a file-count threshold (the two-path bound is what let two code files through).
- **A116. `cleanup` also removes the run's `.agents/local/assignments/<ID>/` directory (amends A11).** What: after every existing precondition passes, `cleanup` deletes `.agents/local/assignments/<ID>/` together with the run directory; a symlink or non-directory there is refused with a clear message before anything is removed, so both directories stay; a missing directory is fine; other runs' assignment directories and the parent are never touched. Why: the lead's per-run assignment files outlived the run and accumulated. Rejected: a separate `agent.sh prune` (a second command to remember, and it needs its own "which runs are finished" rule that cleanup already has); garbage-collecting by age (can delete a live run's assignments, and the age threshold is arbitrary).
- **A117. `.claude/` is ignored as a whole in this repository; `proofline init` never ignores it in a project; the e2e harness and evaluators clean up their own temporary output.** What: the repository's `.gitignore` carries the single line `.claude/` instead of five individual entries (nothing under `.claude` is tracked). `proofline init` keeps adding only `.agents/local/` and `.agents/runs/`; the CLI test asserts, after one and two inits and with a pre-existing `.gitignore`, that no `^/?\.claude(/|$)` line appears. `tests/e2e/run.sh` installs a trap on EXIT (and HUP, INT, TERM) that stops a lead process group still alive and, without `--keep`, removes the project copy and the isolated Codex home, including after a dry run or a failure (before, only a completed non-dry run did); `out/` always stays, the whole run directory stays with `--keep`. `evaluate.md` and the e2e README tell an evaluator to write only its evaluation file and remove every scratch file, copy, extracted tree or temporary worktree it made, leaving nothing in the repository checkout. `capabilities-test.sh` asserts the dry-run cleanup and `--keep`. Why: agent state, progress notes, evaluations and worktrees under `.claude/` kept appearing as untracked files, and a per-file list always missed the next one; an installed project's `.claude/settings.json` is meant to be committed, so the rule must not travel with `init`; failed or interrupted harness runs and evaluators left copies and worktrees behind. Rejected: cleaning by age (a sweep deletes what a live run still uses and hides the leak instead of ending it); ignoring only specific `.claude/` files (the list is always one file behind); having `init` ignore `.claude/` (it would hide the project's committed Claude settings and agents).
- **A118. A stale-lock break never strips a live holder of its pid.** What: `acquire_lock` takes a dead holder's `pid` file by renaming it to `pid.brk.<pid>` and removes the lock only when the renamed file still names the dead holder; otherwise it puts the file back and keeps waiting. Before, a waiter that had read a dead holder's pid removed whatever `pid` file was there when it acted, so a holder that took the lock in between lost it and two commands ran the read/modify/write transaction at once (ledger sequence and allocation collisions, seen as a rare `sc_parallel_lock` failure on CI). `gates-test` `sc_lock_break_race` reproduces it deterministically with a fake `cat`; the test fixtures also use captures dated one hour ahead (a capture 5 s ahead went stale on a slow machine) and no longer let cleanup of the fixture directory decide the exit status. Why: mutual exclusion is the lock's only job. Known limits: a waiter killed (kill -9) between the rename-aside and the `rm`/`mv` back leaves the lock directory with no `pid` file and a `pid.brk.*` file, so every later waiter times out after `PROOFLINE_LOCK_TIMEOUT` (120 s by default); the old code had the same class of window, and the leftover file now also makes `rmdir` fail. If a live holder releases while its `pid` file is renamed aside, its release is skipped, the directory keeps a dead pid and the next waiter breaks it normally (one spurious `lock-break` ledger row); harmless. No further stale-lock heuristic is added. Rejected: a second mutex around the break (the acquiring side would still not take it); `flock` (not in POSIX sh, absent on macOS by default); a mkdir-only lock without a pid file (a crashed holder could never be told from a live one, so it would never be broken); a sleep in the tests (hides the race, does not close it).
- **A120. The Write/Edit tools may not write outside the project copy, and the e2e harness replies to a question only while one is open (C-18, C-14; amends A97).** What: (1) *Hook.* In the COMPLEX run 3 the designer called `Write` on a path whose directory name had a hyphen where a separator belongs (`.../-Users-...-proofline-<session>/scratchpad/e2e-root/.../project/DESIGN.md` instead of `.../-Users-...-proofline/<session>/...`), and the same for `design/UI.md` and `ui.json`; the files landed in a new sibling tree under `/private/tmp/claude-501/`. The cause was not a string-prefix bug (`relpath` matches `"$root"/*`, so `<project>-x` is outside): `path_ok` returned success for every `@outside` path, so A97's shell guard had no counterpart for the file tools. the pretool loop (after `target_ok`) now refuses an outside target of Write, Edit, MultiEdit, NotebookEdit and Codex `apply_patch` unless it is inside a registered git worktree or under the runtime's own storage (`~/.claude/projects/*/memory/`, `~/.claude/projects/*/tool-results/`, `~/.claude/plans/`; `settings.json`, `hooks/`, `rules/`, `CLAUDE.md`, `agents/`, `skills/` and `commands/` stay refused); a symlink among the parents is still resolved first. (2) *Harness.* `tests/e2e/run.sh` sent the scripted question reply whenever no approval was pending, up to `E2E_MAX_REPLIES`; the lead of that run had stopped on a request only the user could fulfil (delete a stray file) and four replies changed nothing. A new `tests/e2e/question-open.sh` decides: a reply goes out only when `agent.sh next` says "a question is pending" or the lead's last message holds a question mark; otherwise the loop stops and `summary.txt` records `stopped=no-open-question` with the last run state (`stopped_state`). Plan approvals are unchanged; destructive, single_step, scope, budget, commit, push and waive requests are still never answered. Why: both were real defects found by the evaluation; EXPECTED C-14 and C-18 are unchanged. Alternatives rejected: a longer `E2E_MAX_REPLIES` or a lower one (hides the loop); matching the model's wording for "no question is open" (language-dependent); denying outside writes only inside a run (the shell guard A97 already applies before one). Residual: a model that stops without a question mark while it does want an answer gets no reply (the run then ends as unfinished, which is visible); the runtime-storage paths are matched by pattern only.
- **A119. A stray file in a run directory is recoverable: the hook refuses `sed -i` with an option-looking suffix, and the lead has `agent.sh discard-stray`.** What: (1) the hook denies `sed -i` (also `-ni` and similar) when the next word starts with `-` (`sed -i -E`, `-i -e`, `-i -n`) or with text glued to `-i` that starts with a letter or `-` (`-iE`, `-ie`, `-in`, `-i-E`), for every caller; on BSD/macOS sed that word is the backup suffix, so the command leaves `<file>-E` beside the target (seen as `runs/T-1/QA_PLAN.md-E`). The message names the portable ways: the Write/Edit tool, `sed -E ... file > scratch && mv scratch file`, `perl -pi -e`. GNU `sed -i.bak`, `sed -i '' ...`, `sed -E` and `sed -n` without `-i` are unchanged. (2) `agent.sh discard-stray <ID> <file-name>` (lead only, run not terminal, completion report not published) removes one TOP-LEVEL regular file of the run directory: the argument is a single file name (no `/`, no `..`, no leading `.`), never a symlink or directory, and its lower-cased name must not equal a protected top-level name of the `known_control_artifact` registry (TASK, PLAN, RUN.yaml, COMPLETION_REPORT, LEDGER.log, VERIFY, EVIDENCE, QA_PLAN, QA_REPORT, `pid` and the registry directories), whether or not the pipeline needs it now. The comparison is case-insensitive because macOS/APFS is: `plan.md` and `PLAN.md` are one file there. Deletion is `rm -f -- <canonical run dir>/<name>`. It writes a `discard-stray` ledger row with the path and sha256 and `next`/`handoff`/`verify-scope` name the command in their "unexpected run artifact" refusal. The hook needs no change for the command: the lead may run any `agent.sh` subcommand and every other role is refused by the existing allow-list and by `require_lead`; raw `rm` in a run directory stays denied. Why: the stray file made `handoff` refuse while the hook denied `rm` for every role, so a whole COMPLEX run deadlocked with no legal way out. Known limits: strays inside subdirectories are not handled (validation tolerates them, and a subpath would make the registry depend on case and symlink resolution); the check-then-delete is not atomic (a TOCTOU window exists; the command is lead-only and the lead is the only writer of that directory at that moment); the `sed` check is a word heuristic like the rest of the scan (a script held in a variable or `xargs sed -i -E` is not seen) and also denies `-iE`, `-ie`, `-in` (glued suffix) and the harmless `sed -i -f script file` / `-i --`; a stray whose name equals a protected name in another case can never be discarded. APFS also folds some Unicode (U+017F reads as `s`, U+212A as `K`), so `discard-stray` accepts only `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` names (checked under `LC_ALL=C`) and also refuses any name that is the same file (same inode, compared with `ls -di`) as a protected name, so whatever else the filesystem folds cannot reach a control artifact; `RUN.yaml.tmp` is protected too. The `sed` deny does not see a backslash-escaped command word (`sed\ -i -E`), an option word held in a variable (`a=-E; sed -i $a`) or built from `${IFS}` (`sed -i${IFS}-E`). Rejected: letting roles `rm` in the run directory (reopens the writable-run-state hole the hook closes); auto-deleting unknown files at `handoff` (silently destroys a file that may be evidence, and hides the cause); renaming the stray (it stays in the run directory and still fails `validate`).
- **A121. The e2e harness records the database before and after a run and a verdict (CRITICAL K-9, K-13; amends A95).** What: `tests/e2e/db-probe.mjs` copies the bytes of `data/reading-list.db` to a temp file and probes the copy read-only; it prints the first two lines of the old probe (`columns=`, `legacy_notes_non_null=`) unchanged, then the `legacy_notes` sha256 and short values, per-table columns and row counts, the applied migrations (`schema_migrations`, else `no migrations table`) and the sha256 of the file bytes (`no database` if absent). `run.sh` writes `db-probe-before.txt` right after the project copy is prepared and before any lead starts, `db-probe.txt` after the run, `db-probe-diff.txt` and `db_unchanged=yes|no` in `summary.txt`; the dry run does the same and `capabilities-test.sh` asserts `yes` on an untouched copy. Why: an evaluation could not judge K-9 because the old probe held only the columns and a count: no migration list, no stored values, no content hash and no earlier probe to compare. EXPECTED files are unchanged. Alternatives rejected: relying on transcripts only (shows what the lead said it ran, not what the file holds); running sqlite3 against the live file (opens it, may create a journal or change it, and the project's own tooling is what the run must not trigger). A probe that cannot run (no node, non-zero exit) writes `probe failed: <reason>` and the verdict is `db_unchanged=unknown`, never `yes`. The `-wal` file next to the database is hashed on its own line (`db_wal_sha256=`, `absent` if none) so an uncheckpointed change shows; `-shm` is ignored. Residual: the probe opens a copy of the main file only, so the stored values in an uncheckpointed WAL are not read, only detected through the WAL hash.
- **A122. The debug-proof guard also reads the task's own text (amends A35, A105; STANDARD S-6).** What: the `handoff IMPLEMENTED` guard that requires `reports/NNN-debug.md` fired only when the TASK title or Goal held a defect word, so a pagination bug fix titled "Every book is reachable through pagination" passed `CODE_DONE` without the report in two regression runs. It now also fires when a line of `## Goal`, `## Context`, `## Acceptance criteria`, `## Constraints`, `## Risks` or a user `>` quote uses the words of A105 (`defect`, `bug`, `regression`, `fix`, `fixes`, `fixing`, Turkish `kusur`, `düzelt`; one shared list, `defect_sec_re`/`defect_sec_tr`, used by `task_defect_errors` too). `## Pre-existing defects` entries are findings and do not count. TRIVIAL and the existing exemptions stay. The failure names `reports/NNN-debug.md` and the debug-proof skill. EXPECTED is unchanged. Why: the trigger must not depend on the lead's wording of the title. Rejected: title-only keywords as today (the miss); always requiring the report for STANDARD (a feature has nothing to reproduce); asking the model whether the task is a bug fix (not deterministic); reading the RED `expected_failure` (the tdd-proof skill tells implementers to write ordinary actual-vs-expected text, so it flags nearly every feature run). Limit: the word list is a heuristic. A lead that keeps defect words out of TASK.md avoids the guard (a model-honesty limit, not enforceable here), and a Constraint such as "do not fix other bugs" asks for the report although the run is not a fix; the lead then writes the short reproduction.
- **A123. A fix round may touch a frozen plan-scope path that shares an acceptance criterion with a failing finding.** A COMPLEX run failed after fix round 1: the gate's `fix_scope` held only the finding paths (`app.js`, `repository.js`, `routes.js`), the correct fix also needed `src/service.js`, a file of the frozen plan scope; `handoff IMPLEMENTED` was refused, the lead asked for an amendment and a second plan approval that nobody could give, and the run ended at PLANNING with every gate stale. Rule: the bound `verify_fix_bound` enforces is the existing union `fix_scope` (finding paths, A77 symbols dropped) plus every path of the frozen PLAN.md `## Scope` whose `criteria:` list shares at least one criterion with a plan-scope path of the failing findings, found with `scope_mappings` (`fix_scope_siblings` in `agent.sh`). A finding path that is not in the plan scope contributes no criteria; a path outside the plan scope, or sharing no criterion, stays refused (scope creep, and the A60 scope approval for unrequested fixes, are unchanged). The gate record's `fix_scope` is not rewritten; `agent.sh next` (open fix round) and the refusal print the computed allowed paths (`fix_path=` lines, `fix_allowed_paths`) for the implementer's `ALLOWED_SCOPE`, and the refusal names the way on (a path the plan never scoped needs the user's scope approval, a PLAN.md amendment and `refreeze`; `amend` works from CODE_DONE only). The regression-test rule (a frozen-scope test path is always inside the fix) is unchanged. Tested by `scenario_fix_scope_shared_criteria` and `scenario_fix_scope_no_criteria` in `lifecycle-test.sh` (the older fix-scope scenarios now give `impl_test.txt` its own criterion). — Alternatives rejected: the whole plan scope in every fix round (no bound: a fix for one criterion could rewrite unrelated files); asking for an amendment (a second plan approval for a change inside the approved plan, the dead end of the run); letting the lead widen `fix_scope` by hand (an unchecked, unrecorded widening by the role that dispatches the fix). — Limits: a plan whose scope entries all list the same criterion lets any plan path be fixed; a file the fix needs that the plan never scoped still needs an amendment.
- **A124. A dispatch in flight is visible, a worker that wrote nothing is BLOCKED with a reason, and the implementer prompt states the window (amends A46, A114).** What: (1) *In flight.* `dispatch.sh` writes `dispatch/<NNN>-<role>-<runtime>.running` (task, role, runtime, pid, start time, "wait for the command to return; do not re-dispatch") before it starts the worker and removes it when the attempt ends; its first lines are `pid=` and `role=`. The marker stays until `finish` (the EXIT trap) removes it, so it covers window-close, metrics and the status check as well as the worker; a retry or failover attempt replaces the previous attempt's marker. No signal trap was added: where `sh` runs the EXIT trap on TERM or INT (bash in POSIX mode, as on macOS) the marker goes with it, and after SIGKILL or under dash it is left behind and goes stale. `agent.sh next` and `pipeline` read the marker: with a live pid `next` prints `next=wait: dispatch.sh <ID> <role> in flight (pid N); ...` instead of the dispatch instruction (SKILL.md rule 1 says do what `next=` says, so an obedient lead no longer re-dispatches) and `pipeline` prints `in_flight=`; a marker whose pid is dead is stale, and so is a marker older than `dispatch.timeout_seconds` + 60 s even with a live pid (pids are reused; one attempt cannot outlive its timeout); `next` removes stale markers with a `note=stale dispatch marker removed` line (it mutates state, dead or over-age markers only), so a legitimate retry is never blocked. `next` and `pipeline` name the first live marker and say others may be in flight (`dispatch/*.running`). `check-result` on an empty or missing `.md` with a live marker says `dispatch in flight (pid N): wait for the dispatch command to return` instead of `malformed header: missing ROLE`. (2) *Nothing written.* A worker that exits non-zero (not 124) or yields no result and leaves its log empty gets one line in that log and `dispatch.sh` exits 6 with `BLOCKED_REASON: <role> on <runtime> produced no output (exit N); log: ...` instead of exit 1 with an empty file. (3) *Prompt.* For a role that writes code (implementer, designer while IMPLEMENTING) the dispatch suffix says that `dispatch.sh` already opened the write window (it leaves no file until `window-close` writes `worker-evidence/WINDOW-<n>.yaml`); for every role, without `--prompt-file`, it says that no assignment was given (do the role's default step from the run's TASK.md and PLAN.md); neither is a reason to report BLOCKED. `DISPATCH.md` tells the lead that `D` is synchronous. Why: in a Codex-lead failover the lead issued the verifier `dispatch.sh` and `cat dispatch/*.log` in one parallel tool batch, read the still-empty files as "empty result", re-dispatched, and ended its turn while the second verifier was still running (`agent.sh next` still printed the dispatch line, which the lead obeyed); the harness's 300 s alarm, of which the lead had already used about 3 min 40 s, was a contributing factor and killed both, so both outputs stayed 0 bytes (isolated HOME, `PROOFLINE_REAL_HOME`, A46 and A114 were not at fault: the same Claude reviewer ran fine from that lead, and a hand run with time to finish produced output). In a hand-driven run a Codex implementer was dispatched without `--prompt-file`, found no assignment under `.agents/local/assignments/` and no window (the open window is only a fingerprint held by `dispatch.sh`), and submitted BLOCKED. Verified by `dispatch-test.sh` (0-byte worker, marker while running and gone after, prompt lines present for the implementer and absent for a reviewer with an assignment). Alternatives: refuse a second dispatch of the same role while one runs (explorers and focus-split reviewers legitimately run in parallel under one role name); write the marker into the `.log` (a command-template runtime's stdout is copied to the `.md`, so the marker would become the result); require `--prompt-file` for the implementer (breaks hand-driven runs that rely on the plan alone); a window file the worker can read (a second source of truth beside the fingerprint bracket).
- **A125. Front matter, YAML and TOML must parse in a strict parser, and Markdown links must resolve; a dependency-free test guards both.** What: GitHub rendered `skills/evidence-map/SKILL.md` with "Error in user YAML: mapping values are not allowed in this context at line 2 column 674". The `description:` was a plain scalar holding `: `, which YAML forbids; Claude Code and other runtimes read the same front matter. The audit found the same defect in `exploit-proof` and `proof-review` (the latter also 1318 characters, over the 1024-character `description` limit of the Agent Skills format). Fixes: the three descriptions are double-quoted (text unchanged for `evidence-map` and `exploit-proof`; `proof-review` shortened to 947 characters (with the quotes), dropping the gate-rule and `config` focus detail that its own references already state); `docs/reference/generate-skills.sh` now removes the quotes and escapes of a quoted description (before, `plan-contract` and `proofline` rows kept stray quotes); `skills/proofline-lead/references/LIFECYCLE.md` pointed to a missing `DISPATCH.md#implementer` anchor (now `#3-implementer`); the root `README.md` pointed to the superseded results section of `tests/e2e/README.md` (now the current one); four templates (`data-design` x2, `proofline-lead` completion report, `ui-design` ledger) held unescaped `|` inside table placeholders such as `<yes|no>`, which splits a GFM cell (now `\|`). New `tests/skills/frontmatter.test.mjs` (in `node --test tests/skills/`, and in the always-run `static` CI job so documentation-only pull requests are checked too): a strict YAML subset parser for every Markdown front matter, every tracked `.yaml`/`.yml` and the Codex `.toml` agents (plain scalar with `: ` or a trailing `:`, `#` in `name`/`description`, indicator-led plain scalar, tab indentation, unbalanced quotes or flow collections, text after a closing quote, duplicate keys, missing closing `---`; for skills a `name` equal to the directory and a `description` of at most 1024 characters: that limit comes from the external Agent Skills format, is documented nowhere else in this repository, and the test applies it), and a Markdown check (unclosed fences, relative links and anchors, table column counts, heading level jumps, forbidden `v2`/`2.0` wording, `agent.sh <subcommand>` names in code spans against `agent.sh`, cited `A<n>` against this file). It also checks the known-bad evidence-map shape fails. Why: GitHub was the only thing that noticed, after the release. Alternatives rejected: installing a YAML library (a dependency in a repository whose rule is "nothing is installed"; Node 22 ships no YAML parser); relying on GitHub to flag it (after merge, invisible to agents that read the file). Residual: the parser is a subset (block scalars, flow collections and anchors are checked for shape only, not evaluated); `control-plane/agents/config.yaml` is read by `agent.sh`'s own awk reader, which strips the outer double quotes and never unescapes, so its backslashes inside double quotes are accepted verbatim (a real YAML parser would reject them; the file is not meant for one); Turkish approval words in `agent.sh`, its tests, `docs/decisions.md` and `tests/e2e/tasks/*.tr.md` are product data, so the "English only" rule is not machine-checked.
- **A126. The debug-proof guard ignores a task whose only defect is a declared pre-existing one (amends A122, A105).** What: a COMPLEX feature run (e2e A1, tag books) was refused `handoff IMPLEMENTED` because the A122 word scan matched `fixes` in a Context assumption ("the request fixes only case-insensitivity") and `defect`/`fix` in a Risks line that discusses the `paginate` defect the task had declared under a nested `### Pre-existing defects` with `decision: report`. The lead reworded TASK.md and refroze, which needed a second plan approval the reply cap forbids, so the run stopped at PLANNING. Now, in the A122 scan, Goal, `>` quotes and Acceptance criteria always count; Context, Constraints and Risks count only when the task declares no `## Pre-existing defects` entries (the rule `task_defect_errors` already applies to the request, same section parser, `###` nesting included); an entry with `decision: fix` is a requested fix and always asks for the report. The refusal states both remedies: write `reports/NNN-debug.md` with the debug-proof skill when the task repairs a defect, or declare an unrelated defect as `decision: report` (no reword, no refreeze). `agent.sh next` never ordered `tier` before `handoff IMPLEMENTED` (the lead chained it by itself). Why: a declared defect with `report` is explicitly not part of the request, and discussing it in prose must not make a feature a defect fix. Rejected: reverting the A122 extension (S-6 bug-fix runs would pass without a report again); allowing a refreeze after one approval (weakens the reply cap); dropping the guard for COMPLEX (the pagination miss was a COMPLEX-class risk too). Limit: a real bug fix that also declares an unrelated pre-existing defect and names its own defect only in Context or Risks is not asked; its title, Goal, quotes and criteria still are. A dummy `decision: report` entry costs nothing at plan time; after freeze it needs an amendment and a plan approval and it is named in the completion report; the guard does not check that the entry's path exists or lies outside the plan scope. A `####` heading level is not matched as `Pre-existing defects`.
- **A127. A plan-level VERIFY failure routes to amendment and refreeze, never to a fix round on control artifacts; a submit under a typed token no longer loses its status file (amends A123, A107, A124).** What: a Codex-lead failover check (e2e check 3) ended at VERIFY. The plan's `## Verification commands` held one negative grep; the verifier could not count its own positive greps (A107 re-runs only planned or configured commands) and failed AC-2..4 with a row on `PLAN.md`. `agent.sh next` then offered an implementer fix round with `fix_path=PLAN.md` and `fix_path=VERIFY.md`, frozen control artifacts the implementer cannot edit, and the lead read the docs as "an amendment always needs a scope approval" and stopped to ask for one. (1) `fix_allowed_paths` drops every control artifact of the run (`known_control_artifact`; a path under `.agents/runs/<ID>/` or a top-level control name as findings spell it: PLAN.md, VERIFY.md, TASK.md, RUN.yaml, LEDGER.log, QA_PLAN.md, QA_REPORT.md, EVIDENCE.md, COMPLETION_REPORT.md; no second list), so `fix_path=` never names one; a product path that shares a directory name (`reports/x.csv`, `design/tokens.json`) stays a fix path. (2) `next` counts the blocking rows a plan answers: the row path is a control artifact of the run; script rows (UNSUPPORTED, risk signal) are excluded as `ns_b` does, and row text is never matched, so a code row that says `verification commands fail: expected 3 got 2` keeps its fix round. When every non-script row is such a row there is no fix round and `next` prints `amend PLAN.md ## Verification commands (lead owns it ...), then agent.sh refreeze <ID> <AMENDMENT>; a scope approval is needed only if the amendment adds scope paths`, then the rest of the route: a PLAN.md change gives a new plan hash, which voids the worker evidence (`valid_evidence_file` binds `plan_sha256`) and every gate, so the implementer is re-dispatched to re-record RED and GREEN (a real failing test, then a real change: GREEN is refused when no implementation file changed since RED; a path with a `tdd_exemption` needs no evidence), then `handoff IMPLEMENTED`, then every gate again, VERIFY included. A commands-only amendment on a non-exempt path therefore has no honest RED: the plan's Verification commands must be complete before freeze, and `next` says so; when the verifier merely failed to cite a configured or planned command, A107 rows get a hint to re-dispatch it (`dispatch.sh <ID> verifier`) instead. Evidence validity is unchanged (integrity). `amend` refuses before CODE_DONE (a reopen needs a finished run), so the route is the amendment file under `amendments/`, the PLAN.md edit and `refreeze`; a refreeze asks for a `scope` approval only when the new plan adds scope paths and for a `budget` approval only when the fix budget is exhausted (both unchanged, asserted). `scenario_plan_level_verify_fail` walks the route to CODE_DONE. A gate mixing plan and code rows keeps its fix round, now without control paths. (3) GATES-AND-FIXES.md and GATES.md say exactly this. Anomaly: the haiku verifier recorded two FAILs (003, 004) yet `d001-verifier-claude.status` never existed, so `dispatch.sh` read "no submission" and re-ran the verifier on sonnet (005). `submit` writes the status under `--agent` when typed, else `PROOFLINE_AGENT`, and writes nothing when the token is empty; a rejected submit also writes a file, so every submit of that attempt ran with an empty token. The generated role files tell a worker to pass `--agent <token from your start context>`, a hook-supplied id that a headless `claude -p --agent` worker never receives; a typed empty value overrode the token `dispatch.sh` exports, while the sonnet rerun omitted it and wrote `d002`. Product defect in token precedence; the exact typed value is inferred, because the worker transcript is not kept. Now `PROOFLINE_AGENT`, when set, wins over `--agent`, unless the typed id is a subagent the hook registered (SubagentStart writes `.agents/local/submissions/<id>.started`): a nested subagent inside a dispatch worker inherits `PROOFLINE_AGENT` and would otherwise write the worker's status file and be blocked by the SubagentStop hook; lead-native subagents have no such variable and keep `--agent`. And the `dispatch resubmit` ledger note carries `token=` and `status_files=` so a missing file shows next time. Why: the control plane must not depend on a model copying a token it was never shown. Rejected: a dispatch fallback that accepts a recorded result without a status file (parallel dispatches of one role, or a lead-side `record`, make the attribution a guess, and a false accept skips a gate); rewording the role files only (a model can still type a wrong token); a second fix-path list for control artifacts (drifts from the registry); letting `amend` run before CODE_DONE (it reopens a finished run). Limit: a plan row is recognised by its path only; a row that complains about the plan without naming a control artifact gets a fix round, and the lead's diagnosis (GATES-AND-FIXES.md step 1) covers it. The subagent marker is a convenience, not a boundary: `submit` checks that some `<id>.started` exists for the typed id, not that it belongs to the caller, so a worker can type another live subagent's id, and an inline interpreter (`python3 -c "open(...,'w')"`) can write into `.agents/local/submissions/`, the same gap as every other `.agents/local` file; the impact is limited to writing that subagent's status file. SubagentStop removes its marker after its own status check; markers of a subagent that never stops are not cleaned up (`cleanup_run` does not know the ids; they are empty files under git-ignored `.agents/local`).
- **A128. The e2e harness answers every pending plan approval, also the second one after an amendment, and reports its reply cap (amends A95, A120).** What: three COMPLEX runs (A1, B1, REG) ended with an unanswered second plan approval. Cause, two defects in `tests/e2e/run.sh`: (1) `pending-kind.sh` read the approval kind only from `agent.sh next`, but after an amendment during a fix round `next` still prints the fix-round step (`agent.sh handoff T-1 IMPLEMENTING`) while the lead has already asked `request-approval T-1 plan` (`approvals/003-request-plan.yaml`, no answer); the kind was empty and the loop took the question path; (2) there, the commit/push guard grepped the lead's message, which holds the approval question with its "accepted answers: ... commit it, push it ..." list, found "commit it", and broke silently (no `stopped=` line). Fix: `pending-kind.sh` takes the run's `approvals/` directory and, when `next` names nothing, reads the newest file: `NNN-request-<kind>.yaml` is a request nobody answered (any kind, so a commit or scope request is still never answered); the file slug is mapped back to the kind (`kind_slug` in `agent.sh` only turns `:` into `-`, so `waive-F-1` is `waive:F-1`); the decision moved to `tests/e2e/reply-decision.sh`: only exactly `plan` is answered as an approval, every other kind (and any unknown one) stops unanswered, and with no kind the lead's text is searched line by line for a commit or push request, skipping the accepted-answers line of an approval question (English `accepted answers:`, Turkish `kabul edilen onay sözcükleri:`; a genuine commit request is also covered by the newest approvals file); the reply cap is checked only when a reply would be sent and then ends the run with `stopped=max-replies` and the pending state (plan replies stay counted in `plan_approval_replies` and in the cap, default 6). C-14 (tests/e2e/expected/COMMON.md) says at most 3 harness replies were needed; with the default E2E_MAX_REPLIES of 6, any COMPLEX run with an amendment and refreeze now legitimately shows 4+ replies: decision needed from the maintainer (EXPECTED not changed). Tests: `pending-kind-test.sh` builds `approvals/` directories (second plan request, every kind slug, both accepted-answers labels); `reply-loop-test.sh` runs the real loop of `run.sh` against a stub lead (plan pending until the cap gives `stopped=max-replies`, every other kind gets no reply). Rejected: letting the lead run `approve` (the approval is the user's reply); answering any lead message containing "approve plan" (the text is also in the accepted-answers list of questions); removing the cap (a lead that asks forever must stop); excluding plan approvals from the cap (an unbounded loop is the failure the cap exists for).
- **A129. Freeze and refreeze refuse a TASK.md acceptance criterion that maps to no scope path and has no explicit test (amends A101, A127).** What: a Claude lead froze a TRIVIAL plan whose AC-2 ("No other change") was in no scope entry's `criteria:` and had no named test. `freeze` only checked that each scope entry has a non-empty `criteria:` (A101); `validate-plan.mjs` would have said `AC-2: maps to no scope path and no explicit test`, but the lead never ran it, so the defect surfaced at VERIFY (`PLAN maps AC-2 to no scope path ... command proof requires tdd_exemption on every mapped PLAN scope path`) and cost an amendment, a refreeze and a re-recording of the evidence (A127). Now `agent.sh freeze` (so `refreeze`, which is `freeze` with an amendment), right after the A101 check and before anything is recorded, runs the plan-contract validator (`.agents/skills/plan-contract/scripts/validate-plan.mjs PLAN.md TASK.md --class <class>`) and refuses only on its two coverage errors, `<AC>: maps to no scope path and no explicit test` and `scope <path>: maps to no criterion`, quoted verbatim, with the fix: add AC-n to `criteria:` of the PLAN.md scope entries that implement it, or name an explicit test for it in `## Tests`. Every class, TRIVIAL included. Every other validator error stays advisory at freeze. Like the other validator calls it runs only where the skill and `node` are installed. `agent.sh next`: when a failing VERIFY row says an AC maps to no scope path or that command proof requires `tdd_exemption`, the plan-level route adds the right edit target, the `criteria:` of the PLAN.md scope entries (front matter) and a planned command proving the AC, not only `## Verification commands`. Tests: `scenario_freeze_ac_coverage` (TRIVIAL plan, AC-2 unmapped while every scope entry has criteria: refused, naming AC-2; accepted once mapped; refreeze refused, then accepted with an explicit test) and the `next` text case in `scenario_plan_level_verify_fail`. Existing fixtures are unchanged: they do not install the plan-contract skill, so the check is skipped there. Why: a plan defect must be refused at PLAN time, by the same rule the validator applies, not found by the verifier. Rejected: running the whole validator at freeze (unrelated checks such as sections, class limits or Assumptions would block freezes the lead can still repair later; the contract stays advisory); leaving it to the lead (the lead did not run it, and the cost is a whole round); catching it at VERIFY (the status quo: late and expensive); a second coverage rule in `agent.sh` (would drift from the validator). Limit: without `node` or the installed skill the check is skipped; a row is recognised by its text for the `next` hint only.
- **A131. The e2e harness chooses the scripted question reply per scenario, and replies to a question only when one is really open (amends A95, A120, A128; CRITICAL K-3, K-5, K-6, C-14).** What: a CRITICAL regression run on main 9e70f03 (FAIL 30/35) answered every lead question with one generic text ("Use your recommended option for every open question."). The intent-grill question's recommended option was the non-destructive one ("stop using the column, keep the data"), so the reply steered the task to `destructive: no`: no destructive stop, K-3, K-5 and K-6 could not occur; the generic text also could not settle the later high-risk assumption question, and a 4th reply went out when none was open (`stopped=no-open-question` came only afterwards, because the lead's explanation held a question mark). Fix: (1) `run.sh` uses `tests/e2e/replies/question.<SCENARIO>.txt` (line 1) as the question reply when the file exists, else line 1 of `replies/<SCENARIO>.txt`; plan replies still come from line 2 of the generic file. `question.CRITICAL.txt` is the user's intent only: the data in the legacy column is no longer needed, never keep it, the column should go. It must never carry process or design words (stages, expand/contract, ordering, a destructive stop, "never run a migration"): those are what the product must discover and do itself (K-3, K-6, K-13), and a reply that supplies them leads the witness; only the keep-or-discard decision belongs to the user (K-16). It creates no approval: the approval grammar (`text_has_approval`) rejects any reply without an allowlisted phrase, and the reply holds no such phrase and no question mark (the test checks it against `approval_phrases` of `agent.sh`); a blanket reply never creates an approval (A60, A106, A108). (2) `question-open.sh` takes the task id: a question mark in the lead's last message counts only when no task exists yet (the intent-grill question is chat-only) or `agent.sh next` names no `agent.sh` step; a recorded pending question (`a question is pending`) always counts. A lead that explains why a blanket reply is refused, while `next` names a concrete step, gets no reply. The harness still never answers a destructive, single_step, scope, budget, commit, push or waive request. This file is harness INPUT (the user's words), not an answer key: every EXPECTED file is unchanged. Tests: `reply-loop-test.sh question-file` runs the loop for CRITICAL and COMPLEX against a stub lead (CRITICAL sends the per-scenario text, COMPLEX the generic one) and checks the text against the approval allowlist; `pending-kind-test.sh` covers the new open/none cases. Rejected: one reply text per scenario holding several branches of answers (the harness would be reading the model's wording); having the harness answer `accept <id>` (an assumption acceptance is the user's own decision); removing the question-mark rule (the intent-grill question is chat-only). Residual: a chat-only question while a task has a named next step gets no reply, and the run ends as `stopped=no-open-question`, which is visible.
- **A130. `NOT_RERUN` is a gate-record field, not a findings row; the completion report names every finding id and every not-reproduced gate (amends A107, A98).** What: A107 made `submit` add an info findings row `NOT_RERUN: <gate>:<focus> no reproduced evidence; this gate is not reproduced proof` to every non-verifier gate that re-ran nothing. Three e2e runs (TRIVIAL, STANDARD, STANDARD.tr) then had a lead write 'findings none' or 'no findings' for gates whose record carried that row, which an evaluator scored untruthful (C-22); the COMPLEX run failed C-22 the same way (fix round 1 where the pipeline said 2 of 2, REVIEW_DATA labels that did not match the gate), so the lead takes fix-round counts and labels from `agent.sh pipeline` and the gate records; the STANDARD report also described an info row that lived only in a report file, and `publish-completion-report` checked headings only, so a report with no finding ids was accepted. Now: (1) `submit` writes the marker as the gate-record field `not_rerun: "<gate>:<focus> no reproduced evidence; this gate is not reproduced proof"` (same style as the A98 `unsupported:` field, carried to `record` by a `NOT_RERUN:` trailer line of the result file) and adds no findings row, so 'findings none' is true for a clean gate. (2) `agent.sh pipeline` prints `not_rerun.<GATE>:<focus>=NOT_RERUN (not reproduced proof): <reason>`; `next` tells the lead to list each in the report. The old info row of records made by an earlier version is read the same way (`risk_not_rerun`). (3) `publish-completion-report` refuses a report that does not name, for every required gate, the id of every finding row of the current gate records (info rows included; UNSUPPORTED rows stay under A98's `## Unsupported Checks`), the refusal naming the gate and the missing ids; `none` is therefore accepted only for a gate with no finding row. The id must stand on a line that also names the gate apart from its ids, so 'scenarios QA-1 to QA-9' does not name a QA finding; every miss is collected and reported in one refusal with the line to add, and `agent.sh next` lists each gate's ids and not-reproduced lines before the report. The `NOT_RERUN:` trailer is sanitised (one line, no quotes, backslashes or control characters, 300 characters) and a focus matches exactly. It also refuses a report without the line `<GATE>:<focus> ... not reproduced (NOT_RERUN)` per `not_rerun` gate and one that calls such a gate reproduced. verify-proof rules 9 and 11, PROTOCOL, the lead's REPORT.md section and the report template say so. Tests: `sc_evidence_gap_gates` (field, no row, empty findings, `pipeline` line), `sc_evidence_gap_legacy` (old info row still honoured), `sc_report_finding_ids` (omitted id refused, `none` for a gate with an info row refused, missing or contradicting not-reproduced line refused, complete report accepted). Why: a bookkeeping marker must not pollute the findings a human or report reads, and a report must not be able to skip the findings. Rejected: telling the lead to mention info rows (an instruction the lead already had and missed twice; nothing checked it); deleting the marker (A107's semantics, that a gate without a re-run is never cited as reproduced proof, would be lost). Limit: the `NOT_VALIDATED` info row (validator skill not installed) is still a findings row and must now be named by id; the id match is textual (an id anywhere in the report counts).
- **A132. Each explorer's scratch report path is per dispatch, `decide` bounds explorer slots, and `git -C $VAR <subcommand>` is not a stash (amends A113, A111; X-4, M-2).** A COMPLEX regression run ended with three explorer results against `max_explorers: 2`. Cause: both parallel explorers wrote their findings to the same scratch file (`scratch/r.md`, a name each picked), the second overwrote the first before `submit`, `reports/001` and `reports/002` had the same content, and the lead dispatched a third explorer for the "lost" one. A113 made the REPORT name unique but left the scratch INPUT name to the model, and nothing enforced the limit. (1) The SubagentStart hook text, the SubagentStop resend text and `dispatch.sh` (which now claims its sequence number before it builds the prompt) name the exact file `.agents/runs/<ID>/scratch/<token>-report.md`; `roles/explorer.md` and evidence-map say to use it or `--report -`. `submit` refuses an explorer `--report <file>` whose path is not `.../<token>-...` (a delimited component, not a substring; a stdin report, the BLOCKED reason and a tokenless submit are exempt). (2) `agent.sh decide <ID> explorer` refuses when the slots of the planning phase are taken. A slot is a DELIVERED report (an explorer result with `RESULT: DONE` recorded after the last `freeze`/`refreeze` ledger event) or an explorer dispatch still running (`dispatch/*-explorer-*.running` with a live pid). BLOCKED and NEEDS_INPUT hold no slot (the retry and the ANSWERS re-dispatch complete the same slot), nor does a dispatch that returned nothing, so a lost slot is re-dispatched without exceeding the limit. The count restarts at each freeze and refreeze, so an extra explorer after a plan amendment is allowed; a limit of 0 (TRIVIAL) is not checked here. The refusal names the limit, the delivered results and the running count. Native Task starts pass `decide` too (the lead calls it before every dispatch). (3) The A111 text safety net counted any `git -C $VAR ...` as a stash: its pattern allows `-C` as a bare flag and then reads the `$` of the flag's value as "a variable where the subcommand goes". Three command lines of the run (`git worktree add ...` with `git -C $W/wt status`, `git -C $WT status`, `git -C $W apply`) were denied as `git stash` although none contains the word. The awk now skips such a match only when the option is not itself a value (`git -c -c $S`), and what follows the value is whitespace and a bare literal word (`status`, `apply`); a quote, `$`, backtick, `-` or the end after the value keeps the count (`git -C $W "$S" list`, `git -C -C $S`, `git -C $W -C $S`, `git -C $W $S list`), and a real `git -C $W stash` still matches longer. A111's bypass battery is unchanged; these cases are new DENIED tests beside it. HONEST LIMIT: the three real command texts (kept verbatim in `tests/enforcement/a132-commands/`) are no longer denied as a stash, but they are STILL refused, now by the A97 write rule ("the command writes `$W/wt/$f` ... which cannot be resolved"), because they copy or redirect to a path built from a shell variable. That rule is correct fail-closed behaviour and is not changed here; such commands need literal write paths, so the stash false positive is fixed for `git -C $VAR` commands that write nothing through a variable, not for these three texts. Rejected: a counter file for slots (a second source of truth beside `results/`); counting decisions or every result file (a crashed, BLOCKED or NEEDS_INPUT dispatch would block its own retry); a Task-tool hook denying the Nth+1 subagent (the lead already passes `decide`, and the hook cannot read the class); `submit` choosing the scratch name (the file must exist before submit); resolving variables assigned earlier in the same command in the write rule (a new parser inside a deny rule). An explorer counts once however often it resubmits (distinct allocated reports, not result files), and a running dispatch whose submission is already recorded counts as its delivered result, not twice. Test hygiene (same change): `test-lib.sh`, `selftest.sh` and the other self-tests now exit 2 when their temp directory cannot be created, because with an unusable `TMPDIR` a fixture was built in the current directory (the real repository) and committed into it; `dispatch-test.sh` runs a test-lib script with a missing `TMPDIR` in a throwaway repository and checks exit 2 and no commit or config change. Residual: a lead that starts several explorers in one call before any dispatch marker exists is bounded only by the next `decide`. Known limits: (a) the stash net's new `match()` with a dynamic regular expression was run locally with macOS awk only; mawk and gawk (CI's awk) are covered by the CI selftest, not by a local run. (b) A quoted variable or substitution in subcommand position (`git "$S" list`, `git -C "$W" "$S" list`) is not seen by the net; this A111 limit existed before A132 and is unchanged.
- **A133. End-to-end items are tagged `[invariant]` or `[quality]`; C-14 allows 3 answers +1 per refreeze (maintainer decision 2026-10-17; amends A95, A131, C-14).** What: (1) Every numbered item of `tests/e2e/expected/` (COMMON, TRIVIAL, STANDARD, STANDARD.tr, COMPLEX, CRITICAL, FAILOVER, CAPABILITIES) carries exactly one tag after its title; no item is removed, loosened or reworded beyond the tag. `[invariant]` is a security or process guarantee and must hold in every run; `[quality]` depends on model behaviour and is scored as a rate. A doubtful item stays invariant. A run passes when every applicable invariant item is met and the quality met-rate (met / applicable) is at least 90%; CAPABILITIES `N-` items follow the same tags. (2) C-14 now reads: at most 3 answers, +1 for every refreeze (answers are harness replies, plan approvals and question replies together; each refreeze after an amendment allows one more); tagged invariant. This closes the open item in `tests/e2e/README.md`. (3) `tests/e2e/evaluate.md` tables get a tag column (after the status column, so the benchmark parser is unchanged) and the verdict line is `E2E: PASS|FAIL (invariant <met>/<applicable>, quality <met>/<applicable> = <pct>%)`; the `CAPABILITIES:` line likewise. Why: one model slip in a wording item failed a whole scenario (COMPLEX passed twice in 11 runs), while security and process guarantees must never be traded for a rate. Quality items (all others are invariant): F-9 (report content), X-3 (acceptance-criteria content), X-9 (which tests the run wrote), K-4 (contract text in the plan). Kept invariant on purpose although they depend on the model: C-22 (a false claim in the report is an evidence failure), K-12, K-6 (what the stop message says), S-3 to S-5 (the fix itself), S-11 (states that commit and push await approval), K-2 (evidence), K-3 (the `destructive` flag drives K-6, K-7 and K-9), X-10 (carries the design-sync VERIFY outcome). Rate: invariants are judged per run and must be 100%; the quality rate is met / applicable quality items pooled per scenario over all runs of that scenario in the campaign (a single run also reports its own rate), and the scenario passes at 90% or more. Known limit: results in `tests/e2e/README.md` were scored before the tags and are not re-scored; no scenario was re-run.
- **A134. A full approval sentence approves, for every kind; the approval stays fail-closed (amends A108).** What: "I approve the plan" and "Planı onaylıyorum" were re-asked: the A108 grammar has no word `i`, and the Turkish object `planı` is a filler, not the kind word `plan`. Now `text_has_approval` also approves a reply that, after the A108 normalisation (ASCII case folding, punctuation and apostrophes removed), EQUALS one full sentence of its own kind (`approval_sentences`): `i approve|i approved <object>` and `<object> onaylıyorum|onayliyorum|onayladım|onayladim`, optionally with the run id (`... for <ID>`, `<ID> için|icin <object> ...`). Objects: plan `the plan | planı, plani`; destructive `the destructive step | the destructive change | yıkıcı adımı, yikici adimi | yıkıcı değişikliği, yikici degisikligi`; single_step `the single step | tek adımı, tek adimi`; scope `the scope | kapsamı, kapsami`; budget `the budget | another round | bütçeyi, butceyi | bir round daha`; commit `the commit | commit'i`; push `the push | push'u`; `waive:<ID>` `the waiver of <ID> | <ID> feragatini` (the finding id is required, as before). Whole-reply equality, so "I approve the plan but not the migration", "onaylıyorum ama ...", "maybe I approve the plan", "I do not approve the plan", "planı onaylamıyorum", "approve?", an empty reply, a sentence quoted INSIDE other text (the reply is then no longer equal to the sentence), while a reply that is only the quoted sentence (`"I approve the plan"`; quotes are punctuation) still approves, and another kind's sentence ("I approve the commit" for a plan, another run id or finding id) stay unapproved and are re-asked; the question mark rule, hash binding, per-capture single use (A106) and the A108 word grammar are unchanged. The approval question shows the first English and the first Turkish sentence of its kind, capitalised as in the docs (`or the whole reply as one full sentence`). Turkish case folding stays ASCII only, so an upper-case non-ASCII letter (`BÜTÇEYİ`) does not match; the ASCII variants and lower-case forms do. The sentence list is built in, not read from `approval.phrases`. Why: the maintainer decided that approval stays fail-closed and that the natural full sentences users type (and `docs/guides/prompt-catalogue.md` quotes) must work. Tests: `scenario_approval_fail_closed` in `lifecycle-test.sh` (every sentence of every kind except waive approves, the listed negatives do not, under the C locale) and `sc_waiver` in `gates-test.sh` (waive sentences, bare `the waiver`, wrong id and wrong kind refused). The harness replies of `tests/e2e/replies/` (`Plan approved.`, `Plan onaylandı.`) are unchanged and still approve (`reply-loop-test.sh`). Rejected: adding `i` and the Turkish object forms to the filler list (loosens every reply: "i approve" plus any kind word would pass in any order); a denylist for the new sentences (A108's finding); an id-less `the waiver`.
- **A135. A Codex lead runs on the base model, never the cheapest (amends A100).** What: `execution_policy.lead_base_model.codex: gpt-6-sol` in `control-plane/agents/config.yaml` is the single source of truth (the lowest model of the Codex ladder `gpt-6-luna gpt-6-sol gpt-6-astra` that meets the lead tier floor 2; it is also the shipped economy lead row). `exec-policy.sh check-lead --runtime RT [--model M]` exits 3 with `LEAD_BELOW_BASE` (naming the base model) for a lead model below it and `UNKNOWN_MODEL` for one outside the ladder; an unset model is refused (`LEAD_MODEL_UNSET`: the runtime default cannot be verified), so the caller passes the model it really runs. When an installed `.agents/config.yaml` lacks the key the base is derived from the ladder and the lead tier floor of `roles.conf`, so enforcement never turns off, and `proofline doctor` warns about the missing key. `tests/e2e/run.sh` runs it on `E2E_CODEX_MODEL` for a Codex lead and for the FAILOVER resume, before the first `codex exec` and every `codex exec resume` (both use the one variable); with `E2E_CODEX_MODEL` empty it defaults to the base model, so `-m` is always passed, so `E2E_CODEX_MODEL=gpt-6-luna` now exits 2. `proofline doctor` fails with `lead model:` when the Codex lead resolves below the base and passes otherwise. Workers (implementer, tester, reviewer, explorer, verifier) and mixed runs with a Codex worker may still use `gpt-6-luna`. Why: the lead plans and decides; a floor-class model cannot carry that, and A100 let the harness select it. Alternatives: docs only (rejected: the maintainer wants it enforced); raise `lead` tier floor to 3 (rejected: the economy profile deliberately leads on the standard tier). Not covered: a lead launched outside the harness, by hand with `codex` and its own default model, is not seen by the resolver (doctor checks the model the policy resolves, not the one a human typed).

- **A136. Merging a pull request requires every check green, enforced on the merge path by a wrapper and a hook, not by an instruction (amends A41, A111; CONTRIBUTING "Merge when CI is green").** What: the maintainer's rule is that nothing is merged unless CI is completely green. A written rule was not enough, so the merge path itself checks. (1) *Wrapper.* `proofline merge <PR-NUMBER> [--merge|--squash|--rebase] [--delete-branch] [-R OWNER/REPO]` (`bin/lib/merge.sh`, in the CLI because it must work in a repository that has no `.agents/`, such as this one; default method `--merge`). It runs a fresh `gh pr view <PR> --json number,state,isDraft,headRefOid,statusCheckRollup` and refuses unless the answer is for that PR, `OPEN`, not a draft, has a 40-digit head SHA and a NON-EMPTY rollup in which every check run is `COMPLETED`/`SUCCESS` and every status context `SUCCESS`. Pending, failed, cancelled, timed out, stale, neutral and unknown entries refuse (neutral and cancelled are not green); no checks at all refuses (green cannot be proven). A `SKIPPED` check run passes only when `gh pr checks --required` does not list it as required and every required check is a pass (the CI of this repository skips whole jobs on documentation-only pull requests and `visual-browser` on every pull request, and GitHub itself counts a skipped check as passing; a skipped REQUIRED check refuses). Then the head SHA is read again with a second `gh pr view` and must equal the checked one, and the merge runs as `gh pr merge <PR> <method> --match-head-commit <sha>`, so GitHub itself refuses if the head moved after the check. Fail closed, naming what was seen: gh or jq missing, gh failing (offline, not logged in, no such PR; gh's text is quoted), output that is not the expected JSON, a PR argument that is not a plain number (a URL or branch name cannot be verified). `--admin` (overrides the checks) and `--auto` / `--disable-auto` (merges later, without a fresh check) are refused by the wrapper itself. (2) *Hook.* `proofline-hook.sh` runs a merge guard on every PreToolUse Bash call for every caller (lead, subagent, headless worker), with or without an active run, AND in a project where proofline is not installed (when the plugin hook is on, it protects every repository the session works in; every other hook rule stays a no-op without `.agents/config.yaml`). On the scan records it denies: `gh pr merge` whatever the spelling (the words `pr` and `merge` among the arguments of any command word that is not a printing or searching word, so `-R o/r`, flags between the words, `GH_TOKEN=x`, `env`, `command`, `sudo`, `timeout`, `nohup`, `xargs`, `ssh host`, `sh -c`/`bash -lc` (also nested), quoting, an absolute path and a variable as command word are all seen; `--admin` and `--auto` are one more case of the same denial); `gh api` whose arguments name a merge endpoint (`.../pulls/N/merge`, `.../merges`), `mergePullRequest`, `enablePullRequestAutoMerge`, `enqueuePullRequest`, `auto_merge` or a `git/refs/heads/<main|master>` update; `gh alias` (an alias could hide `pr merge`); `git push` with a protected branch as the destination (`main`, `master`, `canonical_branch`, the `origin/HEAD` branch; `+main`, `HEAD:main`, `x:refs/heads/main`, `--all`, `--mirror`, a refspec built from a variable or substitution), and a bare push or `push origin HEAD` while a protected branch is checked out in the directory the command runs in (`git -C <literal path>` is followed; an unreadable `-C`/`--git-dir`/`--work-tree` is protected); `git merge` while a protected branch is checked out, or in a command that switches to one (`--abort`/`--quit`/`--continue` pass); `git update-ref refs/heads/<protected>`. Every denial message names `proofline merge`. Read-only `gh pr view/checks/list`, `gh api` reads of other endpoints, feature-branch pushes, `git merge` on a feature branch, `git pull --ff-only`, commit messages and `echo`/`grep` text that mention `pr merge` are allowed. (3) *Runtimes.* The guard is the same script for Claude Code (plugin hook and project install) and Codex (`.codex/hooks.json`), so the denials are identical where the hook runs. Codex limit: project hooks load only when the project is trusted and reviewed (`/hooks`), and the Codex wiring calls `.agents/hooks/proofline-hook.sh`, which exists only in an installed project, so a Codex session in a repository without proofline installed has NO guard and only the wrapper's own checks; a headless run needs `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust`. Codex tool names other than shell are not scanned. Tests (`tests/enforcement/merge-guard-test.sh`, a stubbed `gh` on `PATH`, a throwaway repository under `$TMPDIR`; no git config outside it is touched): all green merges with `--match-head-commit`; red, pending (check run and status), neutral, cancelled, no checks, null rollup, head mismatch, draft, merged PR, wrong PR number, malformed SHA, unparsable output, gh failing, `--admin`, `--auto`, a URL, gh missing, jq missing and a skipped required check are refused with the reason; a skipped non-required check merges; about 60 spellings of a direct merge are denied by the hook (with the wrapper named) and the wrapper, feature-branch pushes and read-only commands are allowed; the guard also runs in an installed project. The hook half fails against the hook of `main`. Rejected: an instruction in the lead's prompt (the point is that an instruction is not enough); a GitHub branch-protection rule alone (needs repository admin rights, is not part of this repository, and `--admin` bypasses it for an owner; recommended in addition, not instead); requiring a wrapper-issued token file for `gh pr merge` (a second state file, and the hook cannot tell whether the file is fresh); letting `--admin` through with a reason (the maintainer decision is that it is always denied); `--auto` through the wrapper (it merges later, after a check this wrapper did not make; the lead re-runs `proofline merge` once CI is green); counting a SKIPPED check as a failure without condition (it would block every pull request of this repository: `visual-browser` is skipped on all of them). Known limits (a heuristic on the command text, like A111): (a) a script or program the model writes and then runs, `sh -c "$VAR"`, `eval`, a command word built by the shell at run time that is not `gh`-adjacent, `gh api -F query=@file` and a merge through the web UI or another tool than Bash are not seen; with a run active the existing rules (`eval`, non-literal command word, nested shells) already refuse several of them, with no run they pass. (b) `gh pr list --search merge`-like commands that carry the words `pr` and `merge` as separate arguments are denied (one retry with other words). (c) `git pull` of a feature branch into a checked-out `main`, and `git branch -f main`, create a local merge or move without a push and are not denied; the push to the remote is. (d) The `--required` lookup relies on gh's text `no required checks` to tell "none required" from a failure; any other error refuses. (e) The wrapper needs `jq` (the hook needs `jq` or `node`). (f) A project that does not load the hook at all (plugin off, no install, Codex untrusted) is protected by nothing but the wrapper, which the model can skip; the maintainer should keep branch protection on as well. Review round 1 (this supersedes the text above where they differ). WORDING: the guard denies the spellings it can read; it does not make a merge impossible. GitHub branch protection with required status checks is the fail-closed backstop and must stay on. (i) `git push -o -n origin main` and `git push origin main -o -n` were read as a dry run: the value of `-o`/`--push-option`/`--receive-pack`/`--exec`/`--repo` is now skipped, and only a real `-n`/`--dry-run` flag lets a push through; `--repo=X`/`--repo X` supplies the remote, so the next word is a refspec. (ii) Denied without a run and without an install: a variable, substitution or backtick where the `gh` subcommand goes (`m=merge; gh pr $m 7`, `gh pr "$M" 7`, `gh pr $(echo merge) 7`, `gh $SUB merge 7`, `gh api $EP`; a literal subcommand followed by a variable, `gh pr view $N`, is fine); `curl`/`wget`/`http`/`https`/`xh`/`xhs`/`aria2c` aimed at `github` with a `pulls/N/merge`, `merges`, `git/refs`, `contents`, `graphql` or `auto_merge` path; `gh api` with `--input`, a field read from a file (`=@`), a `mutation`, a write (`-X`/`-f`/`-F`/`--field`) to `contents/`, or to `git/refs|commits|trees` that names main/master or a variable; text with `gh pr merge`, a merge endpoint, `git merge` or a push to main/master that is piped, here-doc'd or here-string'd into `sh|bash|zsh|dash|ksh`, passed to `python|node|ruby|perl|php|lua -c/-e`, `awk system(`, `eval`, or a `git -c alias.X=!...`; `git merge`/`git push` after a `cd` the text cannot resolve (`cd -`, a variable, `pushd`, a `cd` in a subshell or pipe), after `env|sudo -C`, or with `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_WORK_TREE`/`--git-dir`/`--work-tree` (the directory is then unknown, which is protected); a `PATH=` assignment (env or export) in a command that names gh, merge or proofline; any command naming `PROOFLINE_SELFTEST`.
