(iii) The wrapper resolves `gh` and `jq` itself, prints `using gh at <path>`, refuses a PATH with an empty or `.`/`..` entry or an existing relative directory, and refuses a gh or jq found in the project, the working directory or a temp directory unless `PROOFLINE_SELFTEST=1` (the tests set it; the hook denies a model command that names it). (iv) The wrapper now ALWAYS asks `gh pr checks --required` and refuses when a required check is not a pass or is missing from the rollup, which covers a workflow that has not registered its check yet right after a push; with no required checks configured, an empty rollup still refuses, but a rollup that holds only the checks registered so far looks green, so a repository without branch protection and with late-registering workflows (workflow_run) can still be merged too early. HONEST LIMITS, not covered: a script or program the model writes and then runs (its text is not scanned), a payload assembled at run time (`'gh pr '+'merge'`, base64, a file read by an interpreter), `eval`/`sh -c "$VAR"` that does not match the text sinks above, an HTTP client with the URL in a variable or file, a gh extension, the GitHub web UI or another tool than Bash, a fake `gh` placed in a system directory, `git pull` of a feature branch into a checked-out main and `git branch -f main` (local only), and a `cd` into a main checkout hidden behind a variable inside a subshell (denied as unknown only when the text shows the `cd`). Not tested: a real GitHub repository (the stub replays gh's JSON shapes; `statusCheckRollup` and `gh pr checks --json name,bucket` follow the documented gh output), Codex and the plugin wiring end to end, mawk/gawk (CI's awk) on the new scan paths beyond the existing selftest run.
- **A137. A user who already said the data is not wanted and the column should go is not deferred or refused at intake (amends A131, A32, A55; CRITICAL K-3, K-5, K-6).** What: a CRITICAL regression run on main 3fefcd5 never left intake (3 replies, then lead-4 refused a 3rd relay). Evidence: the task author's round 1 asked D-2 with options (a) export values and keep the column, drop as a follow-up | (b) keep values, drop as a follow-up | (c) discard and drop here, 'needs your explicit approval', recommended (a); the harness reply (intent only, A131) answered the data question but no letter; round 2 asked 'drop in this task or a follow-up (D-6)', (c) 'the workflow doesn't allow me to write that criterion'; round 3 asked 'Shall I take (a)?'. Root cause: not the harness. `roles/task_author.md` carried one unconditional rule: criteria only for the non-destructive step, the drop is a follow-up, 'Must not write a criterion that needs the drop'. So even after the user said the values are not wanted and the column should go, the task author kept offering the deferral as the recommended option, asked a process question (this task or a follow-up; intent-grill rule 5 forbids implementation questions) and called the user's own intent unwritable. An intent-only reply cannot answer a question whose options differ only in step order. Fix: (1) `roles/task_author.md` splits the rule: when the fate of the values is open it asks only that and writes the non-destructive step; only when the user's OWN words say explicitly that the stored values are not wanted (or may be discarded) AND that the column should go does it write the whole removal as criteria (app no longer reads, writes or returns it; the column no longer exists after the new migration), leave the order of steps to the plan, and never offer the deferral as the recommended option, ask whether or when to drop, or refuse the criterion. Hedged, partial or inferred wording ('maybe', 'no longer need the column') is not that statement and gets the data-fate question (keeping or discarding data is the user's decision, K-16). The destructive stop is the safety (A55), not an intake refusal. (2) `roles/data_architect.md`: such a task gets the drop as a `contract` step, a separate later migration, still `destructive: yes`, still behind the destructive approval (A32 unchanged; `single_step` stays forbidden). (3) `tests/e2e/replies/question.CRITICAL.txt` is UNCHANGED (A131): the role fix has to prove itself alone, and a reply that adds 'now, not later' leans toward an answer key. No EXPECTED item, safety rule or approval grammar changed; no answer key. Tests: `tests/skills/task-author.test.mjs` (role text and generated agent carry the explicit-statement rule and the hedge rule; the reply file stays intent-only and unchanged); both fail on main. Not tested: a live CRITICAL run (the next regression round decides). Rejected: letter replies per question number (an answer key, A131); loosening the budget of 3 replies (C-14).
- **A141. The freeze accepts a destructive step in phase `contract` (a separate, later migration file) as well as `deferred` (amends A52, A55, A137; CRITICAL K-3, K-6).** What: after A137 the CRITICAL run left intake but stopped in PLANNING with no PLAN.md. Conflict: `roles/data_architect.md` (A137) plans a requested column drop as a `contract` step (`design/DATA.md` S3, `| S3 | contract | drop_column | ... | yes |`), but `plan_migration_shape` (`control-plane/agents/scripts/agent.sh`, awk `if (dest == "yes") { if (ph == "deferred") late = 1; else early = 1 }`) and `migrationShape` (`skills/plan-contract/scripts/validate-plan.mjs`) counted only `deferred` as late, so the `contract` step counted as early (one-step) and the shape was `missing`; `single_step` is forbidden for it (A52). The lead refused to patch around it. Why a rule fix: `deferred` means not in this change's diff (data-design M4 fails if it is); a task whose user said the data is not wanted and the column should go has acceptance criteria that need the drop in this change (A137), so `deferred` cannot carry it, while `contract` is the phase MIGRATION.md already defines for drops after no code reads the old shape. K-3 asks for a drop 'in a separate, later migration', which a distinct migration file after the stop-reading step is. Fix: both shape checks treat a destructive step in `contract` or `deferred` as late; a destructive step in `expand`, `backfill` or `switch`, or a destructive plan with no preceding non-destructive step, is still one step and refused. Unchanged: the plan is `destructive: yes` (A55), CRITICAL, the destructive approval after FREEZE and before IMPLEMENT bound to the PLAN.md hash (A69, K-6), `single_step: yes` only with the task's own words (A52) and then its own approval, the destructive-SQL hook. Tests: `tests/skills/plan-contract.test.mjs` (fixture `fixtures/plan-contract/critical-data-section.md` is the plan section of the CRITICAL run: accepted; `single_step`, drop in expand and a missing header refused; the old assertion that `contract` is one step now asserts it is accepted), `gates-test.sh` refreeze scenario uses a `contract` step through freeze and the destructive approval. Not tested: a live CRITICAL run. — Rejected: planning the user's wanted drop as `deferred` (cannot meet the A137 criteria, reintroduces the intake stall); allowing `single_step` for it (A52).

- **A138. A docs-writer skill and a shared writing profile (`writing:` config block).** What: (1) `control-plane/agents/config.yaml` gets `writing: { profile: auto|ste|plain, strictness: relaxed|strict }` (defaults `auto`, `relaxed`); `agent.sh config-check` validates both values and refuses an unknown `writing.*` key. `auto` means English text follows a simplified-technical-English style (`ste`) and Turkish text a plain-language style (`plain`). (2) The profile is written once in our own words in `tests/shared/writing-profile.md`: instruction sentence at most 20 words, explanatory sentence at most 25, paragraph at most 6 sentences, noun phrase at most 3 words, one instruction per sentence, active imperative instructions, one word per thing, warning as instruction then risk. `tests/shared/check-writing.mjs` measures only the first three (sentence and paragraph length; front matter, code, tables, headings, comments and link targets ignored; a list item or a sentence that opens with a base-form verb is an instruction), prints the other five as judged, and exits 1 only under `strict`. Per D29 each skill carries a copy (`references/WRITING.md`, `scripts/check-writing.mjs`); `tests/shared/check-shared.sh` fails when a copy differs. (3) New skill `docs-writer` with modes `create`, `update` (diff or range) and `audit`, owned by the architect role (no new role) and reachable as `/proofline docs create|update|audit`. Its evidence script `scripts/check-docs.mjs` proves that quoted repo-internal links (and anchors), file paths, `--flags`, project subcommands and config keys exist and prints `doc:line: <kind> not found: <claim>`. (4) Knowledge step (`LIFECYCLE.md` section 7): a task that changed user-visible behaviour also runs docs-writer `update`; documents under `knowledge_scope_root` in the step, other documents as paths in the frozen plan scope. No new gate, no new state. Why: documents drifted from the code and no check named the missing fact; sentence length was a matter of taste. Alternatives rejected: one shared file read by several skills (D29); a language model to judge length (a script decides, models propose); checking noun phrases and voice by script (needs a parser, false positives); a new docs gate (cost; the knowledge step already exists); copying a standard's text or glossary (we summarise the rules in our own sentences, D33). Tests: `tests/skills/docs-writer.test.mjs` (both scripts, config validation, copies identical) and `fixtures/docs-writer`. Not tested: Turkish text (the instruction heuristic is an English first-word check, so for other languages the sentence class can be wrong; relaxed only warns); a live docs run.
- **A139. A persistent project wiki: the `llm-wiki` skill, `/proofline wiki`, and `docs/wiki/` as default knowledge scope.** What: new skill `skills/llm-wiki` (0.0.1), structured like the reference workflow's wiki: the folder `docs/wiki/` with `CLAUDE.md` (rules), `KNOWLEDGE-PIPELINE.md`, `index.md`, `log.md`, `lint-report.md`, `raw/sessions/` -> `sources/<kind>/` -> `concepts/`, plus `entities/`, `decisions/`, `lessons/`, `syntheses/`, `archive/`; pages join through `[[slug]]` links and carry `title`, `status` (`current|accepted|archived|contradicted`), `source` (a repository file that exists) and, for a contradicted page, `contradicts`. The two rule templates ship as `assets/CLAUDE.template.md` and `assets/KNOWLEDGE-PIPELINE.template.md`. Scope: no new config key. `docs/wiki/` is added to the default `knowledge_scope_root` (now `DESIGN.md docs/design/ docs/adr/ docs/wiki/`), so the existing `in_knowledge_scope` logic applies unchanged (an existing install keeps its own `knowledge_scope_root`, so a wiki write stays implementation scope there until the user adds `docs/wiki/`; the new default applies to new installs): a path under the prefix is knowledge work, any other path is implementation scope; Transaction A reads the wiki on demand and read-only, Transaction B (optional, only for a changed durable contract) ingests, logs, lints, then `knowledge-done`. `.agents/KNOWLEDGE.md` states the flow. One dependency-free validator `scripts/validate-wiki.mjs` is the lint: it writes `lint-report.md` and checks exactly missing `source`, a `source` that is not an existing repository file, invalid `status`, contradicted without `contradicts`, duplicate titles, broken `[[wikilinks]]` (errors, exit 1) and possible orphans (warning); the report is never read as input. `/proofline wiki ingest|query|lint` is routed to the lead (slash command only); the lead role lists the skill. Differences from the user's earlier private llm-wiki skill, where the reference structure wins: `[[wikilinks]]` instead of relative Markdown links; a `source:` repository path instead of a `sources:` list that may hold task IDs (a task ID is history, run directories are disposable); `docs/wiki/CLAUDE.md` as the schema; a status set with `accepted` and `archived`; `log.md` as a dated bullet list of real operations only; no query log entry inside Transaction A; lint also writes `lint-report.md` and checks provenance, duplicate titles and statuses, but no longer checks stale claims, missing concept pages or one-way links (judgement checks stay with the agent); `raw/sessions/` for factual session summaries. Why: knowledge a run produces is otherwise rediscovered by the next run, and the knowledge transaction is the one place where documentation writes are already scoped and checked; reusing `knowledge_scope_root` adds no mechanism. Rejected: `wiki.enabled` and `wiki.path` keys (redundant with the scope root; never writing under it is the off state); a new gate or role; a terminal `proofline wiki` command; copying run artifacts into the wiki. Tests: `tests/skills/llm-wiki.test.mjs` (7), `fixtures/llm-wiki` (selfcheck plants seven defects), and `sc_wiki_knowledge_scope` in `control-plane/agents/scripts/gates-test.sh` (a wiki path is knowledge scope, a path outside is not). Not tested: a live run that writes the wiki (no e2e scenario).

- **A142. An explain-html skill (`/proofline explain <topic|TASK-ID>`).** What: new skill `explain-html` (architect, standalone) explains a topic, a task, a review or a completion report as one self-contained HTML page: inline SVG diagrams, inline CSS and JS, light and dark theme, semantic landmarks, `lang`, SVG titles, visible focus, fluid layout, no video. The page text follows the writing profile (copy of `tests/shared/writing-profile.md`, checked by `check-shared.sh`). A task id is read from `.agents/runs/<TASK-ID>/` (`TASK.md`, `PLAN.md`, review files, `COMPLETION_REPORT.md`); a missing file is named on the page, not guessed. Output goes to `.agents/local/explain/<slug>.html`, already ignored by `proofline init` (`.agents/local/`) and by the skill's `.agents/local/.gitignore`; it is copied elsewhere only when the user asks. `scripts/check-html.mjs` checks only: an external reference (URL scheme, `//host`, `fetch`, `import`, `@import`, `<link href>`, `<script src>`, non-data `<img src>`; the SVG `xmlns` is allowed), a non-empty `<title>`, a size of at most 500 KB. Why: a page is the quickest way to understand a run, and an offline file is safe to open and share. Contrast, landmarks and layout are judged by the agent, not by the script. Version 0.0.1. Rejected: a new gate or role (an explanation proves nothing about the product); an HTML parser or an accessibility engine in the validator (a dependency for a check the agent can judge); a CDN or web font (breaks offline use and leaks the read); an e2e test (the validator test and one fixture are enough for a page generator).

- **A140. The README states status and model expectations briefly; every end-to-end result lives only in `tests/e2e/README.md` (amends A132's documentation rule).** What: the root README lost the "Support levels" table and all per-scenario results. It gains "Status" (version 0.0.1, Claude Code primary, Codex, mixed and failover experimental, a count of more than 900 automated tests) and "What to expect from AI behaviour" (guaranteed invariants versus model-dependent output, a lead needs a base-level model). The tier and adapter rows moved to `tests/e2e/README.md`. The CONTRIBUTING checklist now asks that the README "Status" claims no more than the tests show. Count method: 736 passed (`node --test tests/skills/`) + 42 passed (`sh tests/fixtures/check-fixtures.sh`) + 149 self-test units (`SELFTEST_LIST=1 sh tests/selftest.sh`, equal to the sum of the six CI shards) = 927, written as "more than 900". Lead rule stated as the code has it: only `exec-policy.sh check-lead` (doctor, e2e harness) refuses a Codex lead below the base model or without a model; no other runtime has a base model configured.

- **A143. Read roles change nothing in the project tree, and the completion report's numbers come from the records (amends A71, A111, A130; C-3, C-22).** What: (1) *Read roles.* QA, reviewer, security reviewer and verifier (found by the same caller identity as every other hook rule: the `proofline-<role>` agent type, or `AGENT_ROLE` in a headless worker) may not change a file of the project working tree while a run is open. The PreToolUse hook denies Edit, Write, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit on a project path, redirections, `tee`, `cp`, `mv`, `rm`, `truncate`, `dd of=`, `install`, every in-place editor (`sed -i` in all spellings, `perl -i`/`-pi`, `ruby -i`, `awk -i inplace`, `patch`, `ed`, `ex`, `vi`, `vim`; the hook reads every operand that is an existing project file) and the git subcommands `checkout`, `apply`, `stash`, `reset`, `restore`, `clean`, `switch`, `rebase`, `merge`, `pull`, `rm`, `mv`. A git command is allowed when its effective directory (`git -C`, `cd`) is a registered worktree other than the project (A111 rules, extended). The old rule exempted any command that contained `git -C`, `git worktree` or `--work-tree` from the git check; `git -C . checkout -- f` and `git worktree list; git checkout -- f` passed. For a read role the exemption is gone: each git command is resolved on its own, and a wrapper (`find -exec`) that hides the directory is denied. A target the text cannot resolve (unexpanded variable, substitution, a `..` component, a directory that does not exist yet) is read as the project. The denial says where the evidence worktree is: create it under `$TMPDIR` in its own call (`git worktree add --detach "$TMPDIR/<name>" HEAD`), then use its literal path. Allowed in the project: the role's own run files (`owns.conf`) and `.agents/runs/<ID>/scratch/`. The role texts say it (mutation and trials only in the evidence worktree). The lead, the implementer, the designer and the explorer are not affected. (2) *Report facts.* `agent.sh report-facts <ID>` prints a Facts block from the records: class, required gates with status and every finding id, fix rounds used of the maximum, refreezes (ledger), files changed (task-owned paths), worker-evidence run records (total, ended pass, ended fail; a RED record ends fail on purpose, so this is not a count of failing tests). `publish-completion-report` removes an earlier block, validates the report as before (the generated block never satisfies a check), refuses a number in the lead's text that is not a recorded value, then appends a fresh block. The check reads digits and the English number words one to twenty ("Two tests"), directly beside the words (a verb as in "step 3 tests the lookup", "test files", "test cases", "test suites" and "0 tests failed" are not counts): before them (`7 tests`, `3 required gates`, `2 fix rounds`, `1 refreeze`) or after them with `:` or `=` (`tests: 40`, `gates: 5`), fenced code skipped. Allowed values: tests = the worker-evidence run counts; gates = the number of required gates and 0; fix rounds = used and the maximum; refreezes = the ledger count. The lead skill, the report template and `next` tell the lead not to count. — Why: the rule that a read role never changes the project tree was only partly enforced: the git check was skipped by any command that contained `git -C` or `git worktree`, and `ed`, `ex`, `ruby -i` and `awk -i inplace` were not seen. C-22 asks that a report claim points to a record, but the counts of tests, gates and fix rounds were typed by the model (A130 saw a wrong fix-round count). — Known limits: a script that writes by itself (`python -c`, `node -e`, an npm script) is not seen; no record carries the number of individual tests, so only run counts are checkable and any other test number is refused; other phrasings ("cases", "assertions", numbers in table cells, number words above twenty, Turkish number words) are model text and are not read; the read-role guard works only while a run is active; for a read role `find -delete`, `find -exec <writer>` and `xargs <writer>` must start in a registered evidence worktree (`xargs` is judged by the directory the command ends in; a read-only `xargs grep rm` can be refused by mistake); a read role's variable evidence-worktree forms such as `W=$TMPDIR/x; git -C $W apply` are now denied on purpose (A132's `git -C $W apply` case changed): create the worktree in its own call and use its literal path; `python`/`node` writes are unseen; a fix round written as the ordinal of one round (`fix round 1`) must equal a recorded value too; report-facts shows gate status for the current tree, so it reads `stale` after publish changes the task file. — Rejected: a new gate or role (the existing hook and publish step are enough); parsing free prose (not deterministic); letting the lead copy the numbers (the model would still type them); a variable table for `W=$TMPDIR/x; git -C $W ...` (the A111 rule of a literal path that exists is simpler).

- **A144. The README shows only the short story, `proofline help` lists every session command, and `proofline init --role ROLE=RUNTIME` sets a per-role runtime (amends A140; reuses `runtime.roles` of D6).** What: (1) *README.* The risk-signals bullet shrinks to three sentences (which risks add checks; an unrunnable check is UNSUPPORTED and never a pass). Its full text and the whole "Capabilities and what tests them" section, with the table and the counting paragraph, move to the new page `docs/concepts/testing.md` (long sentences were split for the writing profile, every claim unchanged; the risk-signals paragraph became a short intro, one bullet per focus and two closing paragraphs); the README links to it from "What it is" and "Documentation". The "Status" test-count sentence stays. The quick start gains the `--role` line and two notes: the wiki has no on/off setting (start it with `/proofline wiki ingest README.md`), and Codex needs one `/hooks` approval in the project folder. `proofline doctor` already warns about that approval whenever Codex is a runtime (`codex trust`); it cannot check the approval itself, so the note says "warning", not "failure". (2) *Help.* `proofline help` adds `docs create|update|audit`, `explain <topic|TASK-ID>` and `wiki ingest|query|lint` to "agent session", and the CLI dispatches them like `review`, `audit` and `debug` (it prints the instruction for the lead's session, with a usage error for a missing or unknown subcommand). A CLI test extracts the session commands from the help and from the routing row of `skills/proofline/SKILL.md` and fails when they differ in either direction. (3) *`init --role`.* The flag repeats; each value is `ROLE=RUNTIME`. ROLE must be a file name in `roles/*.md` (lowercase letters and underscore only), RUNTIME must be `claude` or `codex` and must be in `--runtimes`, like `--lead`. An empty, malformed, unknown-role or unknown-runtime value stops `init` before anything is written. The value goes to `runtime.roles.<ROLE>`; the last value of a repeated role wins. `runtime.roles` was already read (`exec-policy.sh runtime`, used by `agent.sh decide` and so by `dispatch.sh`; validated by `config-check`), so no read change was needed. The only code change outside `init` is that `cfg_set` in `bin/lib/config.sh` now also writes a 3-level key under an existing 2-level parent (`init` adds the parent `roles:` when an older config lacks it, turns a hand-edited `roles: {}` into a parent line, and refuses any other inline value with a message). Why: a reader should see what the project is and how to start in one screen; help and the entry skill must not drift; and a mixed team (for example a Codex implementer under a Claude lead) needed a hand edit of the config. Rejected: a new gate or role (none is needed); validating roles from a hard-coded list (the roles directory is the source); a `--role` that skips the `--runtimes` check (a role on a runtime that is not set up would fail at dispatch). Not tested: a real mixed run with an overridden role (the CLI test checks the config and the policy lookup).

- **A145. Standalone deliverables go to their project location after approval, evidence stays local; DESIGN.md defaults under `docs/design/`; task-author splits, sizes, sprints and optionally distributes work; project-wide decisions are ADRs with supersede (amends D10, D26).** What: (1) *Output.* A standalone skill writes its deliverable to the project location only after the user approves it. Evidence, ledgers and raw output stay in `.agents/local/<skill>/<target>/`, git-ignored. (2) *DESIGN.md.* The default moves to `docs/design/DESIGN.md`. An existing install keeps its path; `upgrade` offers the move and never moves the file. (3) *task-author.* Splits a large request into tasks, sizes each S, M or L, and groups them into sprints. Optional team distribution reads `.agents/local/team.yaml`, git-ignored, so no names reach the repository; assignments are informational. Tasks gain optional fields `size`, `sprint`, `assignee` and `depends_on`. (4) *Decisions.* A project-wide decision from an intent grill becomes an ADR; a new ADR that contradicts an older one supersedes it. A task-level decision stays in the task. The existing ADR mechanism is reused. Why: deliverables belong where the team reads them; evidence does not belong in the repository; team names stay out of the repository. Rejected: a new decision store (duplicates ADRs); writing deliverables without approval; moving DESIGN.md automatically on upgrade (rewrites user files); team names in the repository (privacy); a `[task]`/`[project]` extra column in INTENT (breaks validate-intent), so the prefix goes on the question cell. Known limit: the rule "the team question is not asked again when `.agents/local/team.yaml` exists" is checked only against the SKILL.md text; no product code reads `team.yaml` (accepted by the maintainer). Known limit: the planning block (`size`, `sprint`, `assignee`, `depends_on`) sits after the `# Task:` H1 and its optional **Status:** line, so `agent.sh` keeps reading the H1 on line 1. Known limit: in markdown.sh `strip_plan`, a planning block with no closing `---`, or a plain `---` horizontal rule as the first content after the H1, makes the rest up to the next `---` be treated as the block; the failure is loud (import reports MISSING fields), nothing is lost. Follow-up (separate job, not done now): task Markdown is parsed in three places (markdown.sh import, markdown.sh create, validate-task.mjs); move to one shared reader later. Known limit (fixed in 0.0.4): in validate-task.mjs `knownIds`, the inner `names` variable shadowed the outer one, so the file-name match `<ID>-*.md` never fired. Known limit: without `--tasks-dir`, the lookup also scans the file's parent directory, which is lenient; in run directories a `TASK.md` neighbour can add the id `TASK`, while unknown ids are still rejected.

- **A146. UI direction alternatives, redesign without DESIGN.md, a judged `character` aspect, font rationale (0.0.4; amends A145).** What: (1) ui-design offers 2-3 directions derived from evidence; the user picks one and the pick is recorded; never for `conform`. (2) A redesign from code without a DESIGN.md keeps the component inventory and does not use the current visual language as a reference. (3) visual-proof judges a `character` aspect for COMPLEX and CRITICAL when the basis is new or redesign; the `--ui` flag supplies the basis. (4) Font rationale: self-hosted web fonts, licence, performance. (5) DESIGN.md holds only the system. (6) Fix: task-author `depends_on` finds a task by the file name `<ID>-*.md` (the shadowed `names` variable of A145 is fixed). Why: output was generic, a redesign without DESIGN.md had no path, and character drift was never checked. Rejected: a new agent; a new mode; a separate visual-quality file; aesthetic rules in the validator; a style catalogue. Known limit: without a task file the validator cannot check `task_quote` against the user's sentence, only that it asks for a visual change. Known limit: `character` is required only when `--ui` supplies the basis. Known limit: the self-approval check does not scan the alternatives text. Known limit: the `character` judgement is the model's and never closes the gate alone.

- **A147. False-alarm fixes, method-skill precedence, foreign-context warnings, implementer TDD record, proven commands (0.0.5).** What: (1) visual-proof does not measure a non-HTML response (`not_measured`); content a setup injects is registered with `mark(el)`, a function handed to the setup script (not a page global), and is hidden and skipped while measuring; overflow inside a closed `<details>`, `[hidden]` or `[inert]` is ignored while the page has no horizontal scroll. (2) During an active run proofline's method skills (TDD, debug, review, plan, UI audit) are used instead of overlapping skills from elsewhere; language- and ecosystem-specific skills stay free; files other skills produce go to the run's `scratch/`. No skill is blocked. (3) `proofline doctor` warns, never FAILs, about foreign SessionStart/UserPromptSubmit hooks and about skills whose name overlaps a method skill, and about empty `commands.build|test|lint`. (4) The implementer instruction spells out the `tdd.json` block; a retry on code that already exists gets RED from the test run in a separate worktree at the base revision, then GREEN, without asking the user. (5) When `commands.*` are empty and the run proved a command, the lead asks one question after cleanup to write them. (6) `plan_section_present` no longer prints a grep error when PLAN.md does not exist yet. (18) The knowledge step (wiki ingest, `design_docs`, ADR, lesson candidates) also runs for a FAILED or BLOCKED run: `agent.sh knowledge-done` accepts them (no ledger entry, so cleanup still sees `terminate` last) and the lead command is `/proofline knowledge TASK-ID`. `delivery-check` and `draft-pr-body` refuse until `knowledge_state` is `KNOWLEDGE_DONE` or `not_applicable`; `proofline deliver` reports it. docs/wiki, docs/design and docs/adr are knowledge scope, so they are inside the delivery scope. Why: a run that failed still teaches something, and a pull request must not outrun it. Why (earlier items): a real run had 858 blocking visual rows that were all probe side effects, a Codex implementer returned RED/GREEN without the JSON block (`validate-tdd.mjs` exit 2), and foreign skills and hooks competed with the proofline method. Rejected: blocking other skills (ecosystem skills are useful and the gates verify the result whichever skill produced it); auto-merging a FAILED run's pull request. Known limit: the visual-proof setup script runs in the page's own JS world, so page code that intercepts it could obtain `mark` and hide an element; the gate guards against accident, not a hostile page. Known limit: the stray-file deletion rule (baseline of untracked files at the run's first hook event) cannot tell a file the user created later from one the run created, and a run already in progress without a baseline deletes nothing. Known limit: the draft pull request for a FAILED run is printed as a `gh pr create --draft` command and opened by the lead after the push approval; `proofline deliver` does not open it. Known limit: the content fingerprint still includes `.agents/` files, so a user edit to `config.yaml` can stale gates; only the scope check and tier ignore it. Known limit: Claude subagent token counts depend on `agent_transcript_path` or a path derived from `transcript_path`. Known limit: a run created before `knowledge_state` existed can neither pass delivery nor record the knowledge step (recreate or edit the run). Known limit: `knowledge-done` writes a ledger entry also under the open-run check removal; the FAILED-run draft body printed by `deliver` merges stderr into the body.

- **A148. 0.0.6, efficiency: selective gate repetition, one recorded build and test per commit, plan sections, interactive intake, digest.** What: (1) Each gate records the set of files it looked at; after a change only gates whose set changed go stale. An unknown set is stale. SECRETS and SECURITY are always stale on a product change. Gates that rely on a content-only signal are not selective. (2) The build and test run once per commit through `control-plane/agents/scripts/build-record.sh`, recorded by commit hash under `.agents/local/builds`; reviewers and the verifier read the record. The verifier's own independent re-run (A107) is unchanged. (3) `agent.sh plan-section` prints one section of PLAN.md. (4) task-author and intent-grill run in the main interactive session. (5) `agent.sh digest` prints a short run summary, and every stage transition says in one line that a context reset is safe (`/proofline resume ID`). Why: TASK-001 measured 146M Claude tokens; the lead used 210 turns and 45M tokens, the grill 15.7M; the run did 63 builds, 92 test runs and 24 `npm ci`; PLAN.md (49K characters) was read 23 times; the dependency security review ran 3 times without a `package.json` change. Rejected: a vector or memory store; skipping a gate on a guess (an unknown file set is stale). Known limit: the build-record key covers source only, not the node version or installed packages. Known limit: selective reuse uses the whole task-owned manifest, not a per-focus list.

- **A149. 0.0.7, learning: a lesson only from evidence and only with approval.** What: (1) Lessons: `lessons.sh` derives candidates from run evidence, writes them to `docs/wiki/lessons/observations.md` (each with a source gate or finding id; a sourceless candidate is rejected), validates them and counts repeats by script across runs. The lead proposes promotion at the second repeat (third run). Promotion into `AGENTS.md` `## Project rules` needs a `rule:<ID>` approval. (2) `agent.sh calibrate` prints config suggestions from metrics and plans; it never edits a file. (3) Friction report: `friction.sh` builds an anonymous, scrubbed report at run end; `friction send` files a GitHub issue only with a `report` approval bound to the report hash. Why: a lesson that nobody can trace to evidence, or that changes rules unasked, cannot be trusted. Rejected: a new memory store or vector DB; a self-modifying skill; automatic promotion; sending a report without approval; personal or team information in a report. Known limit: promotion goes only into `AGENTS.md`, not into config. Known limit: FAILED and BLOCKED runs cannot take the `rule` approval, so their candidates stay observations. Known limit: hook timeouts are not recorded in the ledger, so the report counts failovers and dispatch timeouts. Known limit: the end-to-end call from `metrics_run` is not covered by the lifecycle fixtures. Amended in 0.0.8 (A150): proposed at the second run.

- **A150. 0.0.8 patch.** What: (1) `knowledge <TASK>` is added to the entry skill (argument-hint, table) and to `proofline help`; the help/entry test now also compares the lead skill Commands table. (2) Lesson promotion is proposed at the second run (repeats>=1); this amends A149. (3) visual-proof `focus_order` false alarms (see A147): cause 1, the tabbables list included links in closed `<details>`, which a real Tab never reaches; the filter is limited to `details:not([open]) > :not(summary)`. Cause 2, `scrollIntoView`/`focus()` on the checked elements moved the sequential-focus start mid-page, so the first Tab started late (matters only with more than 40 tab stops). The fix skips the closed content and resets the focus start to `body`. On a built copy of a real site, `focus_order` fail rows went 724 -> 0 and `overlapping_interactive` 652 -> 0 (the 840 figure of A147 was not reproduced exactly). Why: the entry point gap was found after 0.0.7; the user wanted earlier promotion; a real run had 858 blocking rows. Rejected: loosening the `focus_order` rule; skipping the check for pages with `<details>`. Known limit: only the unit test covers cause 1; cause 2 was confirmed on a real site run, not in a test; the repro used `file:` URLs and its own config.

- **A151. 0.0.9, one-command delivery.** What: `deliver` prepares (knowledge first if not done; `agent.sh deliver-plan` writes SUMMARY.md with changed/knowledge paths, commit message, branch, PR kind/body, when merge and cleanup happen, open `rule:*` requests listed separately) and asks once; "approve delivery" is recorded as separate approvals for commit, push, pr, merge, cleanup, each bound to the content fingerprint and the summary sha256; `deliver-step` re-checks content/branch/message/body/HEAD and the step's approval before printing the commands the lead runs (agent.sh never commits/pushes/merges itself); FAILED run => draft PR, no merge step, cleanup after the PR is open; normal PR => `gh pr checks --watch` then `proofline merge`, cleanup only when MERGED; `rule:*` promotions are not covered by the delivery approval. Why: delivery took many separate prompts. Rejected: a blind single approval (not bound to shown content); cleanup before the PR/merge (loses the evidence the draft body and a failed merge report need). Known limit: a rule promotion changes AGENTS.md so the summary must be prepared and approved again. Known limit: if the summary output is piped into an early-closing reader, agent.sh can die holding its lock. Known limit: the real gh/push path is untested. Known limit: the commit trailer of the 0.0.9 delivery commits names Opus.
- **A152. 0.0.10, reopen a FAILED or BLOCKED run; no draft PR.** What: (1) `deliver-plan`/`deliver` refuse a FAILED or BLOCKED run in one line, `to reopen: /proofline reopen <ID>`; the draft pull request of A151 (`draft-pr-body`, the draft summary kind, the no-run `cleanup` approval) is removed, CODE_DONE is the only delivery path. (2) `/proofline reopen <ID>` (entry skill, lead Commands table, `proofline help`) runs `agent.sh reopen <ID>` (lead-only, reason on stdin): it needs the user's `reopen:<ID>` approval (bound to the PLAN.md hash, named for that run, recorded by the prompt hook like every approval; `approve` stays denied to every model), checks the plan freeze, the task contract and the scope again (files changed outside the frozen scope refuse the reopen), restores the execution state the run had before `terminate`, writes a `REOPEN` gate record marked `reopened_from` and a ledger row, and restarts the knowledge step. Gate records are not discarded: those that passed on unchanged inputs stay, a failed, BLOCKED or never-concluded gate and one whose inputs changed run again (`reuse` mode of 0.0.6); the fix-round allowance (limit stays `review.max_fix_rounds`) restarts at the reopen. A mutation-budget decision the plan needs (criteria above the budget) is asked first, before anything else is checked, and `reopen` applies the approved `raise-budget` itself. (3) The content fingerprint excludes `.agents/` (except the files a run's plan scope names: task-owned product content stays in), so a change confined to the control plane (`proofline upgrade` included) no longer makes a gate stale; only product files count. (4) `deliver-plan` on a CODE_DONE run whose knowledge step, report or DONE is missing prints what is left (exit 0, nothing written, no question) instead of `knowledge step not complete`; the lead runs those steps and asks once, and the one summary lists the knowledge-changed paths. The fingerprint and summary-sha256 binding of the approvals is unchanged. Why: a real run (TASK-001) ended FAILED on a test gate BLOCKED by the budget and a visual false alarm (fixed in 0.0.5/0.0.8), and nothing could take it to DONE. Rejected: delivering a FAILED run with a draft PR (it ships evidence of a failure as a deliverable, and leaves the run FAILED); the agent reopening on its own (a failed run is the user's decision: the approval is bound to the plan, recorded from the user's reply, and no model can create it); reusing `amend` (CODE_DONE only, discards every gate record, resets nothing for a terminated run); a new fingerprint scope that lists control-plane files one by one (drifts with every new control-plane file). Known limits: after a reopen a gate that had failed reads stale and runs again before any fix round starts; the commit approval no longer binds changes under `.agents/` (a commit still contains them; the pre-commit hook and the summary list them); a terminate reason is not re-checked on reopen (the user's approval is the check); `reopen` after a cleanup is impossible (the run is gone); deliver does not run the knowledge step itself, the lead does, before the summary and the one question; the real gh/push path is still untested.
- **A153. 0.0.11, scope discipline, observations, shortcut debt.** What: (1) The proof-review `plan` focus fails a hunk inside the plan scope that changes behaviour and ties to no acceptance criterion: an `unrequested` record with `"blocking": true` (schema, `validate-findings.mjs`, `gate.mjs` fail on it; the result row is `blocking`, `PLAN-CHECK.md` section 2 defines the test). A hunk that does not change behaviour (formatting, comments, import order) stays an informational `unrequested` record, and when the reviewer is unsure the hunk is informational; a path outside the scope stays informational as before (the lead's scope enforcement handles it). `blocking` is only valid inside a run. (2) The implementer report may carry an `## Observations` section (`file:line` and one sentence per issue seen but untouched because out of scope). The knowledge step shows them to the user as proposed new tasks; for each one the user approves, the task author writes a task into `tasks.dir`; nothing is written without the approval, and the implementer never fixes an observation. (3) A deliberate shortcut is marked in code with a comment `shortcut: <limit>, <when to improve>`. `agent.sh debt` (`debt.sh`, grep, no model; `proofline debt` and `/proofline debt`) lists them with `file:line`, limit and improvement condition, read-only and without a run; `agent.sh debt page` (lead, knowledge step) rewrites the one wiki page `docs/wiki/concepts/shortcut-debt.md` from the same list, so a removed shortcut drops off it, and writes nothing when the project keeps no wiki. (4) The implementer role gets a "check first" order: needed, already in the codebase, in the standard library, in an installed dependency, otherwise the minimum code. (5) Documentation rule: a new user-visible command, option or rule updates `proofline help`, the entry skill, the lead Commands table, the README and the relevant `docs/concepts` page before merge (CONTRIBUTING); the README has a command list for the first time (`reopen`, `knowledge` and `debt` included), and the CLI test compares it with `proofline help`. Why: a plan review that only listed unrequested changes let behaviour changes nobody asked for through; a corner cut on purpose was invisible after the run; a useful finding outside the task had nowhere to go but the implementer's urge to fix it. Rejected: blocking every unrelated change inside the scope (formatting, comments and import order are noise, and the reviewer cannot always judge; the rule would fail runs on style); making the external tool that inspired the check-first order and the shortcut comment a dependency (proofline is installable alone and names no third-party project, D33; both rules are written here instead); a model-written debt page (a script finds the comments, so the page cannot invent or miss one); letting the implementer file observations as tasks itself (a task is the user's decision, and the implementer owns no task folder). Known limits: a shortcut comment is only found with the `shortcut:` marker after a comment character, in files outside Markdown, `.agents/`, `.claude/` and `docs/wiki/`; the limit/condition split is the first `, ` or `; ` of the text; whether a hunk "changes behaviour" is the reviewer's judgement, the script only enforces the `blocking` mark; observations are model-reported and not counted by any gate.
- **A154. 0.0.12, installed files are control plane; one reopen question; the Stop hook supplies missing identifiers.** What: (1) A file `proofline init/upgrade` installs outside `.agents/` is control plane like `.agents/` itself (A152): the files `.agents/.proofline-manifest` lists outside `.agents/` (agent definitions, the entry skill), the patterns `.claude/agents/proofline-*.md` and `.codex/agents/proofline-*.toml`, and the marked proofline block of `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md`. `content_fingerprint` leaves them out (the block is cut from the two files before the tree is written), so a gate does not go stale; `verify_scope` lists them apart ("installed proofline files changed") instead of failing, which is what lets `reopen` pass after an upgrade. For `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` a change is control plane only when the file outside the block equals HEAD; any edit outside the block (`## Project rules`) is a product change. A path a plan scope names stays tracked and counts in full (A152). The changed-path list that feeds the review tier skips them too, so an upgrade never raises the tier. (2) `reopen` checks freshness and scope first, then asks ONE question: when PLAN `## Tests` has more criteria than the budget, both requests (`mutation_budget`, `reopen:<ID>`) are written with `joint: reopen:<ID>` and one summary ("Raise the budget to N and reopen the run <ID>?") is printed; `approve` judges a request with that field by the kind words of the reopen, so the user's one reply ("I approve the reopen of <ID>") records both approvals. Approvals are still created only by `approve` from the user's own captured prompt (the prompt hook); `approve` stays denied to every model; each approval is single-use and bound to the PLAN.md hash as before. A budget approval or reopen approval that already exists is not asked again; only one missing: the plain single request. (3) The Stop hook no longer blocks (no rewrite loop). A Stop hook cannot edit the assistant's message in either runtime, so when `report-tokens` names an identifier the last message lacks, the hook emits a `systemMessage` with the matching lines of `agent.sh summary` (verbatim, untranslated), shown to the user next to the report. Why: after upgrading to 0.0.11, `reopen TASK-001` refused with "files changed outside the frozen scope"; the only changes were what `upgrade` installs (A152 covered `.agents/` only); the budget and reopen questions took two runs; the lead rewrote the same report each time the hook sent it back. Rejected: a baseline hash change (RUN.yaml format; runs started before 0.0.12 keep working through the HEAD comparison); treating the whole `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` as control plane (hides the project's own rules edits); one combined approval kind (a second kind to bind, consume and document); a hook that rewrites the report (not possible); keeping the block and a longer skill rule (already failed, A44). Known limits: `.claude/settings.json`, `.codex/hooks.json`, `.codex/config.toml` and `.gitignore` are merged files that mix project and proofline keys; they stay product files. For `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` of a run started before 0.0.12 the baseline holds the whole-file hash, so the block-only change is recognised only against HEAD (an uncommitted or untracked file outside the block is judged as before). The appended identifier lines are a system message, not part of the model's text, and a runtime that ignores `systemMessage` on Stop shows nothing; the check is presence only (A44).
- **A155. 0.0.13, an upgrade commit does not stale the plan; every reopen has its own approval; the block is the first marker pair.** What: (1) `verify_freshness` accepts a `repository.base_sha` that differs from HEAD when `base` is an ancestor of HEAD and every file changed in `base..HEAD` is control plane by the A154 definition (`installed_control_path`, or `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` whose content outside the first proofline block is equal at `base` and at HEAD), and prints one line saying so. A path the plan scope names stays tracked (A152), so it makes the plan stale as before; so does any other changed file, a non-ancestor base or an `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` edit outside the block (stale plan: amendment and refreeze). (2) `reopen` reuses a standing request only through `pending_request`: the latest request of the kind counts only while no approval of that kind is numbered above it, so a used request (approved, consumed) is never reused even when the PLAN hash is unchanged; a still pending unused request stays reusable, which keeps the joint question of A154 one question. (3) `strip_proofline_block` removes only the first `proofline:begin` ... `proofline:end` pair; later pairs and text after the first end marker are product text. Why: in a site project the upgrade files were committed and `reopen` said "stale plan: repository.base_sha changed" though no site file had changed; a second reopen of the same run stuck on "request ... is already approved; request again" because the first reopen's request, bound to the same PLAN hash, was reused. Rejected: rewriting `base_sha` in the frozen RUN.yaml (the freeze and seal bind it); accepting any commit that does not touch the plan scope (a product change outside the scope would hide); making doctor fail on several marker pairs (a new failure for something the first-pair rule already handles safely). Known limits: the accepted move is printed by `freshness` and `reopen` and recorded once per move as a `base-moved` ledger line (deduplicated by its text); the moved `base_sha` stays in RUN.yaml, so later diffs still start at the old base (the control-plane files in them are skipped by A154). A begin marker without any end marker still leaves the file whole.
- **A156. 0.0.14, a new block-only AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md is control plane; an accepted base move updates `base_sha`; agents never kill by pattern.** What: (1) In the `base..HEAD` freshness check (`control_plane_range_only`), `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md` that is missing at `base` and committed at HEAD is control plane when the whole file is the first proofline block: both markers present and nothing but whitespace outside them (`block_only_file`). Text before the begin marker or after the end marker, a second marker pair, a lone marker or a file without markers keeps the plan stale as before; a file that already existed at `base` follows the A155 rule (outside-the-block text equal). (2) After `verify_freshness` accepts a moved base (A155), it writes `repository.base_sha = HEAD` into RUN.yaml; the old value stays in the `base-moved` ledger line ("base_sha moved (<old> -> <new>)"). `reopen` resets its RUN.yaml read cache after its freshness subshell. Checked before the change: no frozen or sealed hash covers `repository.base_sha`: `replace_hashes` (task, evidence, plan, QA plan, policy, pipeline, scope paths, risk) never reads RUN.yaml; `lifecycle_digest` (the ledger seal) lists lifecycle fields only; the baseline holds content hashes of paths; the task-owned patch (`task_patch_fingerprint`) is the content of the owned paths in the working tree, not a diff from `base_sha`. The users of `base_sha` (`task_changed_paths`, `path_changed_lines`, `risk_compute`, cleanup's `ctp_base`) read it as the start of the diff; every file changed in an accepted range is control plane (skipped by those readers, or identical before and after), so the product diff is the same from the old and the new base. The A155 text that said the freeze and seal bind `base_sha` was wrong. (3) Process killing: the role rules (`control-plane/AGENTS.md`, every `roles/*.md` Does/Must-not table, lead skill hard rule 12) say that an agent stops only a process it started itself, by its own PID. The PreToolUse hook (`kill_guard` in `adapters/claude/hooks/proofline-hook.sh`, beside the merge guard, for every caller, with or without a run, installed or not) denies `pkill`, `killall`, `killall5`, `skill`, `slay`, `fuser -k`, `kill -1`, a PID operand `0` (`kill 0`, `kill -TERM 0`), `kill` whose PIDs come from a `$(...)` or backtick substitution that runs `pgrep`, `pidof`, `ps`, `lsof`, `fuser` or `pkill`, and a pipe from one of those into `xargs kill`; signal 0 (`kill -0`, `-s 0`) passes. It reads the scan's command records, so a path (`/usr/bin/pkill`), quotes (`p""kill`), a backslash, `sudo`/`env`/`command`/`xargs`/`busybox` wrappers and `sh -c "..."` are seen; `kill <pid>`, `kill $!`, `kill $PID` and `kill $(cat pidfile)` pass. The hook receives only the agent's own tool calls: a command the user runs with `!` in the terminal does not go through PreToolUse and is not affected. Why: an upgrade that creates `AGENTS.md` or `CLAUDE.md` for the first time (only the block) staled the plan of a run although no product file changed; after an accepted move every later check recomputed the range from the old base and the record still named it; a kill by pattern (`pkill`, `killall`) can stop processes the agent never started, among them the user's own. Rejected: updating `base_sha` only at the next freeze (a reopen has none); denying every `kill` with a substitution (`kill $(cat pidfile)` is the normal way to stop what the agent started); tracking which PIDs an agent started (a process table per session, no reliable owner in the hook); a mass-kill list beyond `-1` and `0` (a process group by number is the agent's own job group). Known limits: (a) the kill guard sees only the model's tool calls and only the command text; a script that is written and then run, an interpreter call (`os.system`, `process.kill`), `find -exec kill`, a payload built at run time, and `eval` or a variable command word outside an open run are not seen; `kill $PID` passes even when the PID came from `pgrep` in an earlier command; `kill -1 <pid>` (SIGHUP by number) is denied too, use `kill -HUP <pid>`; the Codex runtime is covered only where the same hook is installed and trusted; (b) the rule is also a text rule, so a model that ignores it is stopped only when the command matches one of the spellings above; (c) after the base update, evidence a validator wrote before the move names the old `BASE:` and is refused as `UNSUPPORTED_EVIDENCE` (not the run's base), so a contract or migration validator run is repeated after an accepted move; (d) the update is written by whichever command first notices the move (`freshness`, a gate, `reopen`) with the same unlocked temp-file replace as every other RUN.yaml field write, so two commands noticing the move at the same moment may both write the same value, which is harmless, but a parallel write of another RUN.yaml field in that window can lose it as with any RUN.yaml write; (e) a run frozen before 0.0.14 whose move was already ledgered keeps its old `base_sha` until the next freshness check, which then dedupes the ledger line (same text) and writes the new base; (f) a newly created `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` with extra text (a project rule appended in the same commit) still stales the plan, and a `CLAUDE.md` that is only a symlink or a file without markers does too; (g) "whole file is the block" is judged on the committed HEAD blob in the freshness check, so a new block-only file that is still uncommitted is not covered by it, and `verify_scope`/`control_plane_only` (which compare against HEAD) still report it as changed outside the frozen scope until it is committed; (h) `content_fingerprint` now leaves out an `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` that is only the block (as if missing), so creating it by an upgrade does not change the fingerprint; a run whose gates were fingerprinted while such a file already existed (stripped to an empty file before 0.0.14) reads stale once after the upgrade to 0.0.14; (i) the kill guard reads the scan records: a substitution is seen as a bare `$` argument followed by the substituted command, so only the command right after `kill` counts, and `kill` text inside quoted strings, heredoc bodies and commit or PR bodies is ignored; `kill -0`, `-s 0` and `-n 0` (liveness probes) pass; `kill -TERM 0`, `kill -- 0` and `-1` anywhere are denied; `pkill`, `killall` without arguments or with `--help`/`-h`/`--version`/`-V` pass.
- **A157. 0.0.15, night mode: one approval of one summary for several tasks (`proofline batch`).** What: (1) `/proofline batch <ID...|all>` (`agent.sh batch-plan`, lead only; `proofline batch <ID...|all>` prints the hint) reads the task files of the markdown adapter, orders the listed tasks by `depends_on` (ties keep the listed order; `all` is the top-level and `todo/` files in ID order; a cycle refuses the whole batch with `dependency cycle: A -> B -> A` and writes nothing), and leaves out CRITICAL tasks, tasks whose `## Suggested class` is missing or unparsable (class unknown, fail closed), tasks that depend on an excluded task, and tasks whose dependency is neither in the batch nor in `done/`; the summary lists them under "not in this batch" with the reason. It writes `.agents/local/batch/SUMMARY.md` (fresh nonce per plan) and `order.tsv`, and requests the new no-run approval kind `batch`, bound to the sha256 of SUMMARY.md and never consumed: it is valid only while SUMMARY.md keeps that hash, so a new plan or an edit invalidates it. The summary lists the order, what the approval covers per task, the mutation budget cap (`batch.mutation_budget_cap`, default 40), the stop rules, what is NOT covered, the excluded tasks and the headless runtime with its permission mode. (2) `agent.sh batch-cover <ID> <plan|delivery|mutation_budget>` (lead only; every other role is denied by the command allow-list) is what the lead calls in a batch session instead of waiting. It writes the approval of a pending request of exactly that kind, in the format `approve` writes plus `source: "batch"` and `via_batch: "<summary hash>"` (`answered_by: "user"` stays: the user approved the batch), and only when a valid `batch` approval exists (written by `approve` from a prompt capture or a terminal), the task is in `order.tsv`, its run is classified and not CRITICAL (re-checked here: the lead may classify after the batch started), the run is not FAILED or BLOCKED, and the request is pending. `delivery` records one approval per pending step request (commit, push, pr, merge, cleanup) of the current deliver-plan summary, each with its `delivery:` hash; `mutation_budget` needs the criteria count of PLAN `## Tests` to be at most the cap, the file stores `cap:` and `raise-budget` refuses a larger N for an approval that has `via_batch`. Every other kind (`waive:*`, `scope`, `reopen:*`, `rule:*`, `report`, `budget`, `destructive`, `single_step`, `batch` itself) is refused with "not covered by the batch approval". Any failed check exits non-zero with one line and writes nothing. `deliver-step cleanup` appends `ID PR epoch` to `.agents/local/batch/merged.tsv` before it deletes the run evidence. (3) `proofline batch run` (`batch.sh run`, no agent session) refuses without a valid batch approval, a clean tree on the canonical branch and an authenticated `gh`; one `LOCK` (the driver's PID) allows one run, a stale lock is replaced. For each task in order, sequentially: tasks whose dependency is parked or skipped are skipped; the canonical branch is updated (`pull --ff-only`) and its CI is read (running: poll up to `batch.ci_wait_minutes`; red: stop; no runs: no signal); then one clean headless lead session starts for that task (`claude -p ... --permission-mode <dispatch.claude_permission_mode>` or `codex exec`), with the prompt of the lead skill's "Batch mode" section, its output in `logs/<ID>.log`. The outcome is read from records only: `merged.tsv` has the task (merged), run state FAILED or BLOCKED, an open question or an unanswered approval request (parked, reason `question`, first question line), otherwise parked "session ended without a result". Usage-limit text moves the same task to the next runtime (`batch.runtimes`; default `runtime.lead`, then the other CLI when installed), all runtimes exhausted or an authentication failure stops the batch with the task pending. After every task the worktrees the session added are removed and the checkout returns to the canonical branch; a dirty tree stops the batch. A stop keeps the pending tasks pending and writes `# stopped: <reason>` to `state.tsv`; running again resumes the batch (a parked task whose question was answered or whose run was reopened resumes its run). (4) `proofline batch status` (`agent.sh batch-status`) is the morning table (task, result, PR, merge time, park reason, pending question) from `state.tsv`, `merged.tsv` and the parked run's own question files, never from a log, with the friction report under it ("no friction report" when none) and how to resume. Why: seven tasks given in the evening each wait for a plan freeze and a delivery approval, so nothing finishes before morning; the approvals the user would give are known in advance for plan, delivery and a mutation budget raise, but nothing else is. Rejected: (a) blind pre-approval: approving before seeing a summary (the approval is bound to the summary hash instead, so what was approved is what runs); (b) CRITICAL tasks in a batch (they keep their per-task approvals; the summary says so); (c) all tasks in one session (context accumulates and one failure poisons the rest; each task gets a clean headless lead session and its state comes from files). NOT covered, always a question: waiver of a finding, scope expansion, a new dependency, reopen, destructive or single_step data changes, rule promotion, sending the friction report, a new fix-round budget, a mutation budget above the cap. Known limits: markdown task files only (other adapters are refused in one line); the headless lead session runs with the configured permission mode and, for codex, `danger-full-access` with `approval_policy="never"`, confined by the PreToolUse hook where it is installed; the driver waits for the headless session synchronously and removes the worktrees the session added; it does not check for leftover processes (A156 forbids pkill-style cleanup); limit and authentication text is read from the current session's own output only, never from the cumulative log; the CI check reads the latest run of the canonical branch only; `all` excludes `backlog/` and anything in `done/`; the summary hash binds the order and text, not the task files, which the per-task checks (class, freeze, gates) re-read; the real claude and codex headless paths are untested here (the suite uses fake binaries).
- **A158. 0.0.16, technical SEO of public HTML pages is a measured group of visual-proof.** What: (1) `visual.json` gets an optional `seo` block (`public_html`, `pages`, `site_url`) and `report.json` an optional `seo` section (`pages[]`, `items[]` with `id` `S-n`, `check`, `page`, `selector`, `value` = problems found, `threshold` `== 0`, `status` `pass|fail|not_measured`, `evidence`). Check ids: `seo_title`, `seo_description`, `seo_canonical`, `seo_hreflang` (only a page with alternates), `seo_h1`, `seo_open_graph`, `seo_structured_data`, per site `seo_sitemap` and `seo_robots`, and `seo_analytics_consent` (only a page that contains an analytics script, detected by shape: generic host/path words and `dataLayer` in `measure.mjs`, plus the project's own `seo.analytics` hosts and globals in `visual.json`; only a script element that can execute counts, a consent placeholder such as `type="text/plain"` is not a load). `measure.mjs` loads each seo page once with the same network guard and no interaction, so the analytics check sees what runs before consent; the checks are one pure function (`seoEvaluate`) over page and site facts, so `--assemble` (a project tool or runtime adapter, and the tests) drives it without a browser. (2) `validate-visual.mjs` checks the section like other measured items: statuses against values, every measured page accounts for every per-page check, no analytics item without a detected script, and the result is FAIL for a failed item. A failed item is a blocking row; `not_measured` (unresolvable address, missing sitemap or robots.txt, a sitemap index, an unreadable target) is an info row and never changes the result. (3) A project without public HTML pages (`public_html: false`, or no HTML response) runs no item: `seo` is `{status: not_applicable}` with one fixed note the validator accepts. A page blocked by robots (robots.txt, meta or header noindex) is listed `not_applicable` with reason `robots`. (4) Content SEO (keywords, text quality, readability, length) is out of scope and never measured or judged. Reports without `seo` stay valid; no existing check changed. Why: duplicate or missing titles, a canonical to a missing page, a non-reciprocal hreflang, a sitemap that misses a page and an analytics script that runs before consent are mechanical facts of a rendered page, and nobody measured them. Rejected: a new SEO skill or role (the measurement is the same probe and report in the same browser run; a second skill would duplicate tooling, config and the validator, and a role would add a gate for one focus); a separate analytics skill (one check on the same page, only when a script exists); content SEO scoring (taste, not a measurement, and it would fail runs on style); recommending analytics when a project has none (the report states facts about what exists). Known limits: consent-mode snippets define `dataLayer` without loading anything and still count; deployed origins are mapped to `base_url` by path; the robots.txt reader handles the `*` group only; a vendor whose host has no generic word is detected only through the user list. Tests: `tests/skills/visual-proof-seo.test.mjs`.
- **A159. The knowledge step may write the wiki, `not_applicable` needs a checked reason, and every command ends with the user's next step (amends A139, A147, A152).** What: (1) *Writing.* In the knowledge step (execution state CODE_DONE with the handoff not yet DONE, or FAILED or BLOCKED) the runtime hook's `path_ok` lets the lead write a path that `agent.sh in-knowledge-scope` accepts: `knowledge_scope_root` (default `docs/design/ docs/adr/ docs/wiki/`) and the `design_docs` paths, which `in_knowledge_scope` now includes so `verify-scope` and `verify-knowledge-scope` agree. Every other stage, `src/` and every product path, and the run artifacts the hook already protects stay denied; the hook reads the stage from `agent.sh summary` and fails closed when it cannot. (2) *Reason.* `agent.sh knowledge-done <ID> not_applicable "<reason>"` needs one specific sentence (the `reject_generic_justification` check, one line, no quote, backslash or `#`), records it in `execution.knowledge_reason` (new RUN.yaml field, reset by amend and reopen) and in the ledger line, and the completion report's generated Facts block shows `Knowledge step: not_applicable; not_applicable reason: ...`. The script refuses it, naming the first signal, when the task-owned diff holds a path under `docs/` or a `design_docs` file, PLAN.md lists a contract, has a Data section, flags `contract_change: yes` or names a design document, or the acceptance criteria contain a user-visible word (command, flag, option, config, output, error message, API, endpoint). Any signal refuses; the lead then does the real step. (3) *One rule.* `KNOWLEDGE.md` states it once (the step always runs after CODE_DONE, the wiki and the design documents are evaluated every time, the outcome is KNOWLEDGE_DONE with pages written or `not_applicable` with the reason); LIFECYCLE section 7, WORKFLOW, the lead role, the llm-wiki skill and its two templates point to it instead of describing an optional Transaction B. (4) *Next step.* `agent.sh next-user [<command> [<ID>]] [--error[=<kind>]]` (read-only, any role; the table lives in `control-plane/agents/scripts/next-user.sh`, installed with the control plane) prints `Next step: <user command or action>` from the command and the run state; `--commands` lists the table's commands. The lead ends every `/proofline` command with it, the Stop hook appends it as a systemMessage when the reply has no `Next step:` line (command from the captured last prompt, never twice), and `bin/proofline` prints it for its own commands and after every failure; `proofline status` and `resume` no longer print the lead's `next=` lines. The table is documented in the entry skill, the lead skill, the README and `docs/concepts/workflow.md`, and `tests/cli/cli-test.sh` compares its command list with `proofline help`. Why: in a real run the lead tried to write the wiki in the knowledge step and the hook denied it, then passed `not_applicable` on its own say-so so the wiki was never evaluated, and later suggested an internal command instead of `/proofline deliver <ID>`: the user ran it, only a commit happened and the delivery chain broke. Alternatives rejected: lifting the lead write ban for the whole run (the ban protects product code before CODE_DONE); a model-judged `not_applicable` (it was the failure); a table per surface (the lead, hook and CLI would drift); an `--error` argument that carries the failing message (the kind is cheaper and cannot leak internal text). Limits: the signals are word and path heuristics, so a task that changes behaviour without any of them still passes with a reason; the hook adds the line only for the user's last prompt when that prompt is the command (an answer to a question is not); the CLI prints the line only for its own commands, not for those that print the instruction for an agent session; for `/proofline task --grill` the Stop hook's first turn may point at the previous newest task; a run created before 0.0.17 has no `knowledge_reason` field, so `not_applicable` is refused there and the lead must do the real step.
- **A160. 0.0.18, night mode closes its own processes, runs Codex sandboxed and checks the CI of the merge commit; SEO measures analytics by behaviour and robots.txt per bot (amends A157, A158; issues #135, #137).** What: (1) *Per-task timeout.* `batch.task_timeout` (minutes, default 120, validated by config-check, shown in SUMMARY.md and so in the approval hash) bounds one headless session. On expiry the session's process group is closed, the task is parked with the reason `timeout`, tasks that depend on it are skipped and the batch continues. (2) *Own process group.* `batch.sh` starts each session as the leader of its own process group (`set -m` around the background start; where a shell cannot do job control, for example dash without a terminal, `setsid` when it exists; the leader writes its own pid and pgid and the driver signals the group only when both equal the pid it started), and when the task ends (exit, error or timeout) signals that group (TERM, a short grace, KILL). It never signals pgid 0 or 1, its own group, or a group it did not verify; without a verified group it signals only the session pid. No `pkill`, `killall`, `pgrep`, `kill 0` or `kill -1`. A156 stays: agents are still forbidden to kill by name or pattern and the hook's guard concerns an agent's own Bash tool calls; the driver is a program the user starts (a terminal, or `proofline batch run`), so a `kill -TERM -<pgid>` inside it needs no exception, and the driver must not be launched from an agent tool call. (3) *Codex sandbox.* Night sessions run `codex exec -s workspace-write -c sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true` (the project folder is writable, package installs reach the network; the override lives in one variable in `batch.sh`). `danger-full-access` only when `agent.sh batch-plan <IDs> --codex-danger-full-access` was given: SUMMARY.md then carries `codex sandbox: danger-full-access` and a WARNING line, the approval is bound to that text, and the driver reads the choice from the approved SUMMARY.md only, never from config or environment. (4) *CI of the merge.* After a task merges the driver reads the merge commit SHA (`gh pr view <PR> --json mergeCommit`), pulls with `--ff-only` and waits for the CI runs of that SHA (`gh run list --commit <sha>`): any run red stops the batch ("main CI is red"), still running polls up to `batch.ci_wait_minutes`, and no run within the new `batch.ci_appear_minutes` (default 10) stops it ("no CI run appeared for <sha7>"). Before the first merge it checks the runs of HEAD when there are any; none is no signal. (5) *Analytics by behaviour.* The probe records every request before consent (`{url, third_party, phase, initiator}`); `seo_analytics_consent` fails only for a third-party request before consent that belongs to an analytics candidate (its URL matches the generic words or `seo.analytics.hosts`, or its initiator does). A candidate found by name only, a lone `dataLayer` and a same-origin `pixel-art.js` are information (`analytics_detected`, an info row); other third-party requests are listed as `third_party_requests`. (6) *robots.txt per RFC 9309.* For Googlebot and Bingbot the groups with the bot's token (case-insensitive, equal tokens combined) apply, else `*`; longest path wins, Allow wins a tie, `*` and `$` wildcards, empty Disallow allows all; every published page and sitemap page is tested and a page blocked for some bots is a `seo_robots` failure naming the bot. A page blocked for EVERY tested bot is skipped as `not_applicable` (reason `robots`, the detail names the bots), as in 0.0.16 ("only for pages not blocked by robots"); a blocked sitemap page still fails. (5b) The site's own deployed origin (`seo.site_url`) is first party (`siteRef` decides before the probe's `third_party` flag), and an initiator equal to the document URL (what a parser-initiated stylesheet or font reports) never counts, so a path such as `/stats/` cannot make a third-party stylesheet analytics. The driver reads the Codex sandbox from the approved SUMMARY.md once, right after `batch-check`, and passes it to its sessions, so a session cannot edit the file to widen later sessions. Why: a night batch left a hung session and its children running for hours, ran Codex with no sandbox, trusted whatever CI run was newest after a merge, and the SEO check reported scripts that merely had a suspicious name and read only the `*` group. Alternatives rejected: killing by name or pattern (A156, and it hits unrelated processes); `danger-full-access` by default or from config (a night session would then run unsandboxed without the user choosing it); the latest CI run (an old green run hides a red run of the merge); script-name words as blocking (a file named pixel-art.js is not tracking); a `*`-only robots reader (it misses a Googlebot-specific Disallow). Limits: the Codex network override is the documented `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access` key, not run end to end here; on a system with neither job control nor `setsid` only the session pid is signalled; the markdown adapter and real sessions with fakes only stay as in A157; an initiator is known only when the browser reports it; only Googlebot and Bingbot are tested; a page blocked for every tested bot is skipped (`not_applicable`, reason `robots`) and not reported, so a robots-blocked page that was meant to stay private is not listed; only a page blocked for some bots, or one listed in the sitemap, is reported and the user may waive it. robots.txt rules that differ only by percent-encoding are not matched (false negative), and `Disallow: /dir/index.html` is not matched against a page collapsed to `/dir/` (false negative).
- **A161. 0.0.19, night mode verifies its process group and gives Codex a permissions profile; the kill guard reads indirect kills as a tripwire; freshness and fingerprint follow-ups; SEO compares robots.txt in percent-encoded form (amends A156, A157, A160, A154-A156 freshness rules; issues #127, #128, #131, #132, #133, #135, #137, #140).** What: (1) *Process group.* Night mode always starts the headless session as leader of a process group of its own. The driver tries `set -m`, then `setsid`, then a perl `setpgid` one-liner (perl ships with macOS), then python3 `os.setpgid`, and uses the first method whose probe process reports pgid equal to pid; the session checks the same itself and exits 125 otherwise. When no method works the batch is refused with one line (`batch: no way to start a headless session in its own process group on this machine ...`) and no session is started: the 0.0.18 pid-only fallback is removed, because a hung session could not be closed with its children (checked: signalling only the session pid left 3 of 3 descendants alive; every group method left 0 of 5). `PROOFLINE_TEST=1` with `PROOFLINE_BATCH_PGMETHOD` forces each method so every fallback is tested on every machine. (2) *Codex night profile.* Sessions no longer use the legacy `-s workspace-write`. Verified with `codex sandbox` (codex-cli 0.163.0) and the documented "Protected paths in writable roots": that mode keeps `.git`, `.agents` and `.codex` read-only, so a session could not branch, commit, add a worktree or write its run records, and the legacy `network_access` key does not apply to the profile. Sessions run in the named permissions profile `proofline_night`, passed as `-c default_permissions="proofline_night"`, `-c permissions.proofline_night.network.enabled=true` and `-c permissions.proofline_night.filesystem={...}`: read everywhere, write the project folder including `.git` and `.agents`, `.git/hooks`, `.git/config` and `.codex` read-only (a planted hook or `core.hooksPath` would run outside the sandbox), write `$TMPDIR`, network on. Verified inside the profile: `curl` to registry.npmjs.org returns 200; `git commit`, `branch`, `checkout -b` and `worktree add` inside the workspace succeed; `.agents/runs` is writable; `$HOME` is not. Package managers wrote caches under `$HOME`, which the profile keeps read-only: `npm view left-pad version` failed with EPERM on `~/.npm/_cacache` (curl worked), so "network on for package installs" was true only for tools that need no `$HOME` cache. The driver therefore passes `-c shell_environment_policy.set={...}` that points the caches into the writable temp dir (`$TMPDIR/proofline-pkg-<uid>`): `npm_config_cache`, `npm_config_store_dir`, `YARN_CACHE_FOLDER`, `YARN_GLOBAL_FOLDER`, `PIP_CACHE_DIR`, `GOCACHE`, `GOPATH`. Verified inside `codex sandbox -P proofline_night` with real commands: `npm view` and `npm install left-pad`, `pnpm add` (pnpm 10.33.2), `yarn add` (yarn 4.10.2), `pip3 download six`, `go get` and `go build` (uuid v1.6.0); `$HOME` stays read-only. Not verified, so not claimed: cargo, other package managers, a corepack download of an uncached version (`COREPACK_HOME` is not set, since redirecting it made the pnpm shim resolve another version). Rejected: making `~/.npm` and the other cache folders writable (opens `$HOME` for every tool). `codex exec` accepts the profile (it reaches the API and stops at the missing credentials; no model session ran). `--codex-danger-full-access` is unchanged, and the summary line starts `codex sandbox: proofline_night profile (`; `danger-full-access` keeps `codex sandbox: danger-full-access`. Git stays in the Codex session. The profile was verified only on codex-cli 0.163.0. `batch-plan` records `codex version:` in the summary when a Codex runtime can run, and the driver refuses a Codex runtime below 0.163.0 or with an unparsable `codex --version` (an error when the lead or `batch.runtimes` names it, left out with one line when it is only the installed fallback), instead of running an unknown sandbox.
